# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1992

## Introduction by Vipramukhya Swami

From the newspaper and television interviewers of the West to the leaders and *sannyāsīs* of the Gauḍīya-maṭha in India, ISKCON devotees are known for their *guru-bhakti,* or devotion to their *guru*. More specifically, what makes ISKCON “tick” is the dedication of the devotees to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. *Saṅkīrtana* devotees who are going out in the snow and rain, in the summer and winter, distributing Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books to the conditioned souls on every continent of the world, do so because of dedication and gratitude for the great treasure Śrīla Prabhupāda has given them. Temple presidents, GBC members, temple administrators, and regional secretaries are struggling with various managerial headaches, trying their best to push on the preaching mission because of their indebtedness to Śrīla Prabhupāda. Kitchen pot-washers, temple cleaners, *pūjārīs,* cooks, householders, *brahmacārīs, brahmacāriṇīs*—all are working because of their individual and collective devotion to Śrīla Prabhupāda. What makes ISKCON successful, what makes it grow, is the principle of *guru-bhakti,* or more specifically, Prabhupāda-*bhakti*. This is the oil that keeps the clockwork of ISKCON running.

This year, more than a thousand devotees from around the world (a mere sampling of Prabhupāda’s world-wide movement) came together at the Māyāpur festival in India and congregationally glorified Śrīla Prabhupāda, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. As 450 foreign devotees weaved through the streets of Navadvīpa on *nāga-saṅkīrtana,* loudly chanting the holy names of Gaurāṅga and the *mahā-mantra,* it melted the hearts of even ISKCON’s critics. Whatever problems there were in the past seemed to be evaporated by the *kīrtanas,* as the predictions of Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and Lord Caitanya manifested before the eyes of everyone. All over the world Kṛṣṇa consciousness is expanding, and it is manifesting itself in a magnificent way in Māyāpur. There, the great city of devotees predicted by Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and ordered by Śrīla Prabhupāda is taking shape as construction at ISKCON Māyāpur is creating dozens of new buildings everywhere. Those that go to the Māyāpur festival can witness this flowering of Kṛṣṇa consciousness themselves. ISKCON is not dead. ISKCON is an overflowing success as devotees forever increase their devotion and dedication to Śrīla Prabhupāda. *Guru-bhakti,* or the Prabhupāda-*bhakti* principle, is the manifest essence of this success.

The dedication of the devotees toward Śrīla Prabhupāda is like a great ocean. As they increase their devotion through thick and thin, in happiness and distress, in tears of joy and in the pains of struggle, this ocean increases in size. This great ocean is put in the big cauldron of the *saṅkīrtana* movement and stirred throughout the year until it boils over at two grand festivals, one happy, one sad. The first festival, in which this Vyāsa-pūjā book is offered and renewed every year, is called Vyāsa-pūjā, or the appearance day celebration of Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is a happy day. The second is in the month of Kārttika, and is the disappearance day observance of Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is a sad day. Both days, happy and sad, are a source of spiritual ecstasy for the dedicated servants of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Both days bring emotional, bitter-sweet tears to the eyes of grown men and women who have dedicated their all-in-all to the lotus feet of their divine master, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Both days are days of rededication to Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The *guru-bhakti* of the disciples and grand-disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda, though deep like an ocean, seems like a drop compared with the devotion Prabhupāda showed toward his spiritual master, Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. Whatever *bhakti* the devotees try to show toward Prabhupāda is learned from Śrīla Prabhupāda’s devotion toward his *guru*. Here, as with everywhere in ISKCON, Prabhupāda is the *ācārya*. He is that great teacher of the divine principles of *guru-bhakti* which is reflected in the hearts of the ISKCON devotees. Whatever glorification people offer toward the devotees for their *guru-bhakti* is offered by the devotees back to Śrīla Prabhupāda, for it was he who crossed the seas at the end of his life (leaving the holy *dhāma* of Vṛndāvana to bring Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the world), it was he who translated and wrote commentaries on Vaiṣṇava literature for the world to read, it was he who established the temples, initiated thousands of disciples, created the movement, and taught the devotees how to cook, bathe, clean, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, and worship the *arcā-vigraha*. He opened the doors to Lord Caitanya’s mercy and freely distributed the contents. And he did this all because it was the mission of His Guru Mahārāja—it was his offering of *guru-bhakti* to his spiritual master. This was the secret of Prabhupāda’s success. Not surprisingly, to the extent that his followers appreciate and imbibe Prabhupāda’s *guru-bhakti,* to that extent they can find their own success.

In this spirit, the present Vyāsa-pūjā book is the culmination of devotional appreciation toward Śrīla Prabhupāda submitted by GBC members, *sannyāsīs,* and temples around the world. Here one can be sucked in and drown in the great turbulent ocean of Prabhupāda-*bhakti,* replete with whirlpools and great waves, which flows from the hearts of devotees dedicated to Śrīla Prabhupāda. Though offered anew each year at this time, each year it becomes sweeter as the devotees increasingly rededicate themselves to their beloved lord and master, the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Vipramukhya Swami  
Regional Secretary, Western Canada

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-Pūjā by Suhotra Swami

Śrīla Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana Vyāsadeva is an empowered incarnation (*śaktyāveśa-avatāra*) of Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Empowered incarnations are devotees who exhibit one or more of seven of Kṛṣṇa’s own *śaktis,* or potencies. Śrīla Vyāsadeva is invested with the *jñāna-śakti,* the potency of knowledge, so much so that Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has described him as *sarvajña* (“all-knowing”). “The words spoken by this sage are Vedic evidence,” Caitanya Mahāprabhu has testified to Sanātana Gosvāmī (*munira vākya śāstra paramāṇa*), “and through his words alone can the materially conditioned souls attain spiritual knowledge.”

More than five thousand years ago Śrīla Vyāsadeva foresaw the present Age of Quarrel, in which human religion, truthfulness, cleanliness, peacefulness, mercy, memory, bodily strength, and life span are steadily cut down until mankind is no better than the lowly beasts. For those who yet retain a spark of inquisitiveness about the blissful state beyond repeated birth and death, the sage compassionately compiled the timeless Vedic wisdom into many volumes of Sanskrit hymns, histories, laws, science, and philosophy. The best of Vyāsa’s works is the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* The bona fide student of the *Bhāgavatam* revives the loving ecstasy of his soul’s inner devotion to Śrī Kṛṣṇa and is transferred from the material realm to Goloka, the supreme plane of transcendental consciousness, where the most intimate forms and pastimes of Godhead are revealed.

But grasping Vyāsadeva’s transcendental message is not a matter of dry academics or intellectual gymnastics. The genuine student of the *Bhāgavatam* or any other Vedic scripture must have Vyāsadeva’s blessings before real understanding can blossom in his heart. Therefore the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* directs its readers to offer their respectful obeisances to Vyāsa before beginning their recitation.

Now it may be asked, “How can someone today receive the blessings of a person who lived five thousand years ago?” Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the incarnation of God, is not different from the Supersoul in the heart of everyone. He can thus bestow transcendental knowledge upon anyone from within. Vyāsa is likewise not different from the words he has written; thus those who even today see through the eye of *śāstra* are blessed by his personal guidance. And because Vyāsa lives still at Uttara Badarikāśrama, in the high Himalayas, great devotees like Madhva, the founder-*ācārya* of the Brahma-sampradāya, were able to seek him out and obtain his personal blessings.

But for most of us, the blessings of Vyāsa must be received from the current representative of Vyāsa, the spiritual master who comes in disciplic succession from Madhvācārya, Śrīla Mādhavendra Purī, and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Our hearts are too polluted by lust to take Vyāsadeva’s counsel from within, and we are too feeble-minded to directly comprehend the meaning of his words (the Vedic scriptures) simply by reading them. It is certainly unlikely that any of us will get his *darśana* in the snowy wastes above Badrinātha. But Vyāsa can be seen and heard by us in the form (*vapu*) and teachings (*vāṇī*) of the *ācārya* who has been blessed by the power to broadcast the message of Vyāsa throughout the world.

According to the *Ādi-parva* of the *Mahābhārata,* the word *vyāsa* means “one who describes elaborately.” That pure devotee who is empowered to elaborately elucidate the Vedic scriptures represents fully the original Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana Vyāsa. His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is without a doubt the *vyāsa* of the modern world, as proven not only by the sheer volume of his writings and lectures, but by their astounding revelatory potency. Vyāsadeva’s purpose in compiling the Vedic literature was to rescue humanity from the folly of materialism; Śrīla Prabhupada has fulfilled that purpose by inspiring tens of thousands of people around the world to abandon wholesale atheism, scepticism, and hedonism for the path of pure devotion preached by *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.*

Each year on the anniversary of Śrīla Prabhupada’s appearance in this world, those whose lives have been transformed by contact with his message offer homage to him. In so doing they are, as Śrīla Prabhupada explained, “offering obeisances to the *paramparā* system,” worshiping all the *ācāryas* of the Brahma-Mādhva-Gauḍīya disciplic succession, through whom the blessings to comprehend Vyāsadeva’s Vedic scriptures descend.

Suhotra Swami  
Governing Body Commissioner

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Badrinārāyaṇa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You once gave the measure for becoming a successful preacher: “One must have the courage of an Englishman and the heart of a Bengali mother.” Certainly your life is the full embodiment of this standard.

As for courage, on a morning walk in Calcutta you were reminiscing about your childhood days. You described how you climbed up the scaffolding to the top of the Victoria Memorial. A devotee remarked, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, you must have been very brave.” You countered, “And I am still brave! Otherwise, how have I come to the West all alone?”

Regarding your compassionate heart, in Māyāpur one devotee was watching from the guesthouse veranda as a poor woman and her son picked through the refuse pit, looking for some morsel of food. The devotee turned to you and said, “Sometimes I feel so sorry for these people.” With a most deep and penetrating glance, you said to him, “Why just sometimes?”

Disregarding nay-sayers (“‘impossible’ is a word in a fool’s dictionary”), you risked your life and came to America as a “lone fighter.” From the dangers of the Bowery to San Francisco’s ranting, intoxicated challengers (“it is the darkest of darkness”), to the mundane scholars (“Dr. Frog”), and irate parents (“modern-day Dakṣas”), from Moscow to Africa and back, you boldly and bravely preached the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Along the way you nurtured your followers, dispensing Āyurvedic antidotes, marital advice, long-lost common sense, and an occasional dose of much-deserved “sauce.” You repeatedly rescued us from our foolishness and naivete. You often began a *darśana* by inquiring, “Is everything all right?” and you built a house the whole world could live in.

In explaining “causeless mercy,” you compared it to an unknown man walking up to someone on the street and handing him one million dollars. You have said that the nature of the debt owed the spiritual master is such that if you pay back five dollars, you realize that actually you owe ten. When you attempt to pay back the ten, then you realize you owe twenty.

Another year has passed and I am writing another Vyāsa-pūjā homage. As I look back over the year and consider how I have met you, I get some glimpse of your causeless mercy, how much I owe you and how little I have done to repay that debt. May I always feel indebted and some day become a worthwhile assistant. I beg to remain

Your servant,  
Badrinārāyaṇa dāsa

## Bhakti Charu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you, savior of the fallen souls and inaugurator of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement throughout the world.

On this most auspicious day I want to inform you that your ISKCON is sailing smoothly on the ocean of material nature, rescuing countless spirit souls from the whirlpool of birth, death, old age, and disease.

You so expertly designed this transcendental ship that in spite of innumerable storms in the turbulent ocean, the progress is unaffected. You, the perfect captain, trained your dedicated followers to navigate through all kinds of difficulties. In turn, they are also training their followers to become worthy grandchildren of Your Divine Grace.

As an unworthy servant of your lotus feet and an insignificant member of ISKCON, I am trying to render some service. I wonder whether my efforts are pleasing to you. When your pastimes were manifest, everything was so easy. Whenever we made some mistake and you were displeased, you immediately corrected us through merciful chastisement. Now that you have withdrawn your pastimes from our material vision, we cannot make out whether our efforts are pleasing you. I realize we have to become very vigilant and meditative to find out how you are accepting them.

You instructed me to translate your books into Bengali and personally trained me to do that job. Once when you were in Bombay you even told me, “Just translate my books and go back to Godhead.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the last few years I neglected that service. In 1987, soon after translating *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* I was appointed a GBC member. Your ISKCON was facing major crises at that time, and I thought that helping to solve those problems was my main responsibility. I felt ISKCON must be saved first; everything else was secondary. If ISKCON fell apart, what would be the use of anything else?

I never wanted to neglect my translation work, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but due to heavy managerial pressure and hectic traveling I could not translate regularly. I felt extremely guilty about that. At the end of almost every day, in spite of a busy schedule, it was as if I had done nothing because I did not translate your books. I was always aware that this is my main service, but I did not know how to disentangle myself from countless managerial responsibilities around the world.

When I was praying for you to reveal whether it was right to consider managerial responsibilities more important, you spoke through your disciples in the form of chastisement. You made it clear that translation work is what you want me to do.

I was naturally concerned how to disentangle myself from worldwide responsibilities and become absorbed in serving your personal order. Then you made the necessary arrangements to facilitate my service, exactly as you did when you were present in your *vapu* form.

Through the GBC’s of Māyāpur you directed me to manage the Māyāpur Gurukula. They arranged with the GBC body that I remain in Māyāpur for eight months a year. The International GBC’s made the proper adjustments, relieving me of many international responsibilities.

I became convinced that you are still with us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. This is how you always shower your mercy. First you rectify our mistakes by chastising us, and then you make all arrangements to situate us properly.

I can see you did not leave us. Although we cannot see you physically, you are very much there, guiding us all the time. My only wish is that I can become a puppet in your hands; then I will not have to worry about anything.

I never thought of getting involved in *gurukula*. But now that you want me to, I am plunging into that service and thoroughly enjoying the responsibility. Whenever I look at those bright, effulgent faces of the students, I realize they are the future leaders of this world. They are your grandchildren and potential pure devotees. I want to take care of them as a servant takes care of his master’s children. As I am your servant, I am their servant also.

You personally gave me the order to translate your books. Now, through your loyal representatives, you ordered me to take care of your Māyāpur Gurukula. These two duties have become my most important responsibilities now. Please bless me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I can execute these duties properly and always remain engaged in serving your lotus feet.

Always aspiring to remain at the dust of your lotus feet, I remain

Your humble servant,  
Bhakti Charu Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most merciful Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (18.75) Sañjaya reveals:

*vyāsa-prasādāc chrutavān*  
*etad guhyam ahaṁ param*  
*yogaṁ yogeśvarāt kṛṣṇāt*  
*sākśāt kathayataḥ svayam*

**“By the mercy of Vyāsa I have heard these most confidential talks directly from the master of all mysticism, Kṛṣṇa, who was speaking personally to Arjuna.”**

Also, he said, “Wherever there is Kṛṣṇa, the master of all mystics, and wherever there is Arjuna, the supreme archer, there will also certainly be opulence, victory, extraordinary power, and morality. That is my opinion” (Bg. 18.78).  Because of the blessings of his spiritual master, Śrīla Vyāsadeva, Sañjaya was able to receive the special mercy of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Similarly, in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.5.8-9) we find that once Vyāsadeva was very unhappy in spite of his great literary contributions. His spiritual master, Śrī Nārada, came and said, “You have not actually broadcast the sublime and spotless glories of the Personality of Godhead. That philosophy which does not satisfy the transcendental senses of the Lord is considered worthless. Although, great sage, you have broadly described the four principles beginning with religious performances, you have not described the glories of the Supreme Personality, Vāsudeva.” In this way Śrī Nārada pointed out the reason for Vyāsadeva’s unhappiness. Devarṣi Nārada further enlightened Vyāsadeva, saying, “Your Goodness has perfect vision. You yourself can know the Supersoul Personality of Godhead because you are present as the plenary portion of the Lord. Although you are birthless, you have appeared on this earth for the well-being of all people. Please, therefore, describe the transcendental pastimes of the Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa more vividly. Learned circles have positively concluded that the infallible purpose of the advancement of knowledge, namely austerities, study of the *Vedas,* sacrifice, chanting of hymns, and charity, culminates in the transcendental descriptions of the Lord, who is defined in choice poetry” (*Bhāg.* 1.5.21-22).

Thus by the mercy of Devarṣi Nārada, Śrīla Vyāsadeva was able to narrate the great epic *Śrīmad-Bhāgavam,* the complete science of God, for the welfare of mankind. Thus great souls exemplify that the spiritual master is the life and soul of the disciple. By becoming sincere, loyal, dedicated, and committed to the instructions of the spiritual master with unflinching faith, the disciple can get the blessings of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have unlimited transcendental qualities. You are the most faithful and confidential servant of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. In the history of Vedic culture you will be known as the most munificent transcendentalist, who walked every nook and corner of this planet singing Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare, reminding everyone about the transcendental destination of life’s journey. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the perfect representative of Devarṣi Nārada and Śrīla Vyāsadeva. You tirelessly traveled around the globe so many times just to enlighten people in the matter of *ātma-jñāna*. So you will be known as the Nārada Muni of the twentieth century. You flooded the whole world with transcendental literature, planting the seed of devotional science in the hearts of millions. Thus you will be known as the Vyāsadeva of the twentieth century.

You have mercifully given so many instructions to this most insignificant servant. “Go to all the colleges and universities presenting the scientific basis of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Prove the thesis that life comes from life. Prove that God is a Supreme Person. Make a scientific commentary on *Vedānta-sūtra.* Study carefully the details of the Fifth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and make a working model of the universe \[a Vedic planetarium\]. Organize a *saṅkīrtana* and cultural group in the scientific preaching presentation of *Bhāgavata* culture. Make a Manipur temple like our temples in Vṛndavana and Māyāpur…” In this way Your Divine Grace instructed me. Śrīla Prabhupāda, only by your blessings can all of your instructions be fulfilled:

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

Only by your divine blessings, a dumb man can speak and a lame man can cross over mountains. On this most sacred day of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, please bless me so that someday I can sing your glories properly.

Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

Dear Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Time has already revealed you as an empowered soul:

*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*

**“Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement. (Cc.** ***Antya*** **7.11)**

And Time also has revealed you as an intimate servant of him who knows the inner mind of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu—Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī:

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

**“When will Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, who has established within this material world the mission to fulfill the desire of Lord Caitanya, give me shelter under his lotus feet?”**

Time, the enemy of nondevotees, has befriended your servants by nourishing our affection for you as we witness sprouts, blossoms, and fruits flourishing within the preaching fields throughout the world.

We recognize that powerful preaching *śakti* revealed in Kaziastan and Bombay and Swangcho and Timonsora and Moscow and Armenia and Berlin and Durban and London through those who heard you—or—through those who heard from those who heard you—or—through those who heard from those who heard from those who heard you—as yours. You are to this day preaching as Mahāprabhu preached:

As soon as He \[Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu\] saw someone, He would request Him to chant “Hari! Hari!” Whoever heard Lord Caitanya chant “Hari, Hari,” also chanted the holy name of Lord Hari and Kṛṣṇa. In this way, they all followed the Lord, very eager to see Him. After some time, the Lord would embrace these people and bid them return home after investing them with spiritual potency. Being thus empowered, they would return to their own villages, always chanting the holy name of Kṛṣṇa and sometimes laughing, crying, and dancing. These empowered people use to request everyone and anyone—whomever they saw—to chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.… Simply by seeing such empowered individuals, people from different villages would become like them by the mercy of their glance. When these individuals returned to their villages, they also converted others into devotees. When others came to see them, they also were converted. In this way, as those men went from one village to another, all the people of South India became devotees. \[Cc. *Madhya* 7.97-104\]

Each devotee who sincerely serves you feels connected to your strength, your determination, your compassion, and your protection. Through you, they connect via *sampradāya* to the dust of the feet of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

*yāre dekha, tāre kāha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ajñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*  
*kabhu nā vādhibe tomāra viṣaya-taraṅga*  
*punarapi ei ṭhāñi pābe mora saṅga*

**“Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the** ***Bhagavad-gītā*** **and** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.*** **In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land. If you follow this instruction, your materialistic life at home will not obstruct your spiritual advancement. Indeed, if you follow these regulative principles, we will again meet here, or, rather, you will never lose my company”(Cc.** ***Madhya*** **7.128-29).**

Time thus reveals your position—although you secretly cherished it deep within your heart all along—as a confidential servitor of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, and the Lords of Vṛndāvana, Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

*nikuñja-yūno rati-keli-siddhyai*  
*yā yālibhir yuktir apekṣaṇīyā*  
*tatrāti-dākṣyād ati-vallabhasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“The spiritual master is very dear because he is expert in assisting the** ***gopīs,*** **who at different times make different tasteful arrangements for the perfection of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa’s conjugal loving affairs within the groves of Vṛndāvana. I offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.”**

May I eternally serve you,  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Girirāja Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace. All glories to your auspicious appearance in this world to deliver us fallen souls and give us transcendental bliss.

You said that everything is in your books. But how to get everything out of them? Your books themselves tell us:

*śrīmad-bhāgavatārthānām āsvādo rasikaiḥ saha*  
*sajātīyāśaye snigdhe sādhau saṅgaḥ svato vare*

**“One should taste the meaning of** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **in the association of pure devotees, and one should associate with the devotees who are more advanced than oneself and endowed with a similar type of affection for the Lord” (Cc.** ***Madhya*** **22.131).**

The purpose of the *Vedas* is to know Kṛṣṇa. And the essence of Kṛṣṇa is *rasa: raso vai saḥ*. The ripened fruit of the Vedic tree of knowledge is *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* It is full of *rasa*. And those who relish *rasa* are *rasikas,* or devotees. Only devotees can relish *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and we should taste the meaning of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* with pure devotees. *Karmīs, jñānīs* and *yogīs* can never relish *rasa* and thus attain bliss.

Your purports further explain: “In a holy place like Vṛndāvana, there are many devotees constantly engaged in chanting and hearing the glories of the Lord. If one gets the chance to hear from pure devotees in such a place, allowing the constant flow of the river of nectar to come from the mouth of pure devotees, then the cultivation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness becomes very easy. . . . In the association of pure devotees one becomes attached to hearing and chanting the glories of the Lord. In this way one can become faithful to the Lord, devoted to the Lord, and attached to the Lord, and thus one can very quickly attain full Kṛṣṇa consciousness” (*Bhāg.* 4.29.40 purport). Especially when we come to Vṛndāvana for VIHE we get such opportunities.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so kind. You have given us everything. You have given as your sublime books and association to help us understand their deep purports. How can we ever repay you?

Of course, the disciple can never repay the debt to the spiritual master, but the spiritual master may be pleased by the attitude of the disciple who tries. And how? You said that we can preach like you. And how did you preach? You preached by word and by example. We have to know how.

Though some may say the best way to glorify you is to preach, I think the best way for me to preach will be to glorify you. If the devotees and the world knew more how you served and what you said, they would benefit so much. They would do so much to try to please you.

So from my own experience and others’, I would like to record your personal struggle and instructions in Bombay. Practically, you gave your life for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rasabihārī. You preached on Their behalf and you gave us service to Them, too. How you served in Bombay and preached will be the greatest example and instruction for us and the whole world to follow.

So I want to write something to glorify you and your wonderful service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rasabihārī, but unfortunately I am not qualified. I do not have the purity to do it. On this occasion I pray to you to give me the guidance and the qualification necessary to write. And here again your books will surely help: “It is certainly not good to write literature for money or reputation, but to write books and publish them for the enlightenment of the general populace is real service to the Lord. That was Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī’s opinion, and he specifically told his disciples to write books. According to Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, distributing literature is like playing on a great *mṛdaṅga*. Consequently we always request members of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness to publish as many books as possible and distribute them widely throughout the world. By thus following in the footsteps of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī \[who wrote books\], one can become a *rūpānuga* devotee” (Cc. *Madhya* 19.132 purport).

All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your wonderful service to Rādhā-Rasabihārī!

Your eternal, fallen servant,  
Girirāja Swami

## Gopāla Krishna Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Once again we are celebrating your most glorious appearance on this planet, and again we are feeling that our words cannot express or describe your transcendental qualities and activities. No one within the three worlds can sufficiently praise your glorious activities in establishing ISKCON and spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness—a work which is going on constantly with increasing strength everywhere.

A little over five hundred years ago Lord Caitanya predicted that the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* would be spread to every town and village of the world. So you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have descended from Goloka to bring about the realization of Lord Caitanya’s prediction. The only purpose of your appearance was to explain the true meaning of *bhakti.* You also explained the importance of *jñāna* and *vairāgya.* You are an eternal associate of Lord Caitanya.

*gaurāṅgera saṅgi-gāṇe nitya-siddha kori mane*

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* it is stated,

*kali-kālera dharma kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti viṇā nahe tāra pravartana*

**“The fundamental religious system in this Age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement.”**

With the passage of time your glories are spreading more and more. Devotees are realizing the importance of constantly associating with you. This association can be realized by constantly associating with your *vāṇī,* or instructions, which you have very kindly left us in your books, letters, tapes, etc. The Bhaktivedanta Archives has been rendering a great service to the devotees by publishing your room conversations, *darśanas,* lectures, etc. Through your words you have answered every question one can possibly ask to defend the philosophy of *bhakti* and destroy *māyā.* We do not have to search for answers elsewhere. All spiritual problems can be solved by remembering your instructions. The cause of spiritual problems is deviation from your instructions. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us the real wealth—transcendental knowledge. You have shown the perfect example of how one should make the order of the spiritual master one’s life and soul. You wanted ISKCON to go down in history as a movement that has changed the direction of this world. In order to realize this, we have to become fully dedicated to carrying out your orders to print and distribute your books in as many languages as exist on this planet. Lord Kṛṣṇa’s worship has to be established in every home in every village, town, and city on this planet. Any person who reads your books will experience a change of heart and begin the quest for perfection. The whole world needs to be flooded with this treasure of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which you have very kindly given us. Only this will stop the sinful tendencies from sprouting.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, very soon the world is going to celebrate the centennial of your appearance on this planet. In years to come, this Vyāsa-pūjā day of yours is going to become one of the most important occasions in the world. Many ambitious programs are being contemplated for your pleasure. One such program is the completion of the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Pārtha-sārathi temple and Glory of India Vedic Cultural Center in New Delhi. Delhi, which at one time was known as Indraprastha, is also a place of your transcendental *līlā.* You struggled in Delhi to print your books and BTG and to start your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. In a letter to a disciple in 1971, you wrote, “Delhi is the most important city in India because the capital is established there. Mostly the population is educated.… So from the cultural point of view, Delhi is the most important city.” So now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is our duty to make your true glory known in this important city. We should re-establish the ancient glory of Indraprastha. To fulfill your desire to establish a glorious center in this city, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa has very kindly granted us a three-acre plot at a prominent location in south Delhi. After a long struggle with the agents of Māyā, who were trying to take the land away from us, we now have cleared all obstacles, and the construction of the glorious Śrī Śrī Pārtha-sārathi temple and the Glory of India project has started. A museum will depict the true Vedic heritage with the help of modern technology. The library will exhibit and focus on your works, along with the contributions of other Vaiṣṇava saints. The people of India are confused about the meaning of *dharma* and the qualifications of a bona fide spiritual master. This project in Delhi will help destroy the prevailing illusions and establish you as the real spiritual master, who took Vṛndāvana to the whole world.

Therefore, on this auspicious day I fall at your feet and beg you for mercy so that I can help complete the Glory of India project and help print and distribute your books in my area of responsibility. Only this will make you happy, and the only purpose of my existence should be to please you. Otherwise, life has no meaning.

Your most fallen disciple,  
Gopāla Krishna Goswami

## Gour Govinda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O my dearest eternal spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, O lord of my heart and soul, I lay myself in humble prostration before thy lotus feet and earnestly pray for kind acceptance of this offensive servant’s small offering on this most auspicious occasion of the holy appearance day of Your Great Divine Grace.

O my dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda:

You are immortal and eternal, remaining ever present in your nectarean preachings, and thus still awakening and inspiring even this feeble, rotten eternal servant to move on the ever-illuminated path of pure devotional advancement.

And therefore, O my dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda:

You and you alone are the invoked charioteer guiding this most unworthy disqualified servant through the battlefield of this dreadful material existence.

O my dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda:

Your nectarean, compassionate, transcendental preaching alone is the source of the spiritual strength that energizes even this very feeble, cowardly eternal servant to courageously survive with the hope to one day take part in rendering better service to your great divine cause during the remaining part of this very life.

Ofcourse, although, my dearest Śrīla Gurudev,

Your Divine Grace belongs to your devotees of this world as much as to the longing, eternal associates who seek your constant divine association in the supreme spiritual world, and even more to KṚṢṆA, THE SUPREME LORD HIMSELF, still, through relishing your divine song of KṚṢṆA PREMA, the mind, heart, and soul are freshened and swept away with the waves of pure love of Godhead.

And thus, O my dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda:

The nucleus of this very life and conception is the ever-illuminated preachings of Your Great Divine Grace.

And thus, O my dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda:

You are the unending perennial source of transcendental mellow, the unending giver of pure KṚṢṆA PREMA RASA BHĀVA that makes us alive in this life.

And thus, O great Gurudev:

Such is the strength of your high aspiring song of pure love of Godhead, such is the clarion call of your ever noble pure heart, and such is the depth of the strength of your transcendental preachings that they still resound on in every atom that we are encircled with.

And thus it is by your unending causeless mercy alone, O great Śrīla Gurudeva, that:

Your Bhubaneswar temple of THEIR LORDSHIPS is now showing the symptoms of steady progress, though it still stands far behind your holy high aspiration of becoming the very best temple, especially in the matter of singing the pure song of the divine glorification of ever-melliferous nectarean KṚṢṆA PREMA.

And therefore, O dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda:

My soul cries out at the holy dust of your divine lotus feet to please make this most feeble, unworthy servant just a musical instrument in your divine lotus hand, to be successfully used by you to help sing that great melodious song of pure nectarean KṚṢṆA PREMA and thus compassionately enable the thirsty aspiring newcomers to relish the ever-nectarean PREMA RASA and sing the song of pure love of Godhead.

All glories to Patita-pāvaṇa Parivrājakācārya His Divine Grace Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupādjī Guru Mahārāja!  
All glories to the sacred Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony of our eternal, most beloved, and most revered great spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupādjī Mahārāja!

With the ever-hungry aspiration and soul’s outcry to have that rare fortune of becoming a speck of the holy dust of your divine lotus feet, I remain  
Your eternal but most degraded servant,  
Gour Govinda Swami

## Guruprasāda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet, which has sanctified the whole universe.

I never cease to marvel at how much mercy you have given me. Yet my mind is so clouded by material desires for name and fame, which obscure the path of unalloyed devotional service. By your causeless mercy I pray that I come out of the dream state of ignorance and wake up to reality, which means becoming your humble servant.

The spiritual master directs the disciple at every stage of his development. Sometimes a disciple sees himself as a devotee, and the spiritual master reminds him that he is actually the servant of the devotee:

*jogyata vicāre, kichu nahi pai, tomāra karuṇā sāra*

Please let me always remember that any qualification I have is only coming from you. This aspect of surrendering to the spiritual master is of utmost importance. In this way you penetrate all strata of false ego and false identifications by your transcendental instructions. It is most difficult by any other means to perceive the real self.

You carry out this essential function of the spiritual master in the most expert way by using the technique given by Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. First you give us devotional service, and then you give us the means and methodology to perfect it. You once gave the instruction to let someone develop attraction for chanting and then teach him how to follow all the rules and regulations to purify his existence, thus increasing his taste.

Therefore, I beg you for the necessary qualification and intelligence to develop the tolerance and discrimination to do my preaching service for you. In this way I can try to help others take shelter of your lotus feet through this transcendental movement you have started, which is the merciful medium you have provided for me to offer my tiny service to you. And by this preaching service I may have the opportunity to receive the full mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu by your causeless mercy. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your unworthy servant,  
Guruprasāda Swami

## Harikeśa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances thrown in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, who are the personification of the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. It is through your mercy alone that thousands of formerly sinful people are now dancing like jubilant peacocks in the bliss of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is only because of you that millions of people now know about the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement and are chanting the holy name of the Lord. Your glories are immense, and they form a network of mercy that has captured this planet and is making it surrender to your desire. Please always engage me in this mission of mercy and make me a humble servant in the dust of your lotus feet.

I remember you as a personality who was indefatigable. I saw that you would take great troubles upon yourself and not be visibly affected by them. After traveling around the world with you, I could personally see your great and uncommon strength, although you were externally in a body which could not possibly tolerate such troubles.

Once we were flying rapidly throughout the USA and Canada, culminating in a long night flight to Paris. Although I was completely physically finished and could not even see straight, you were alert, fully awake, and ready to begin your preaching in this new field. I could never understand how you could be beyond jet lag. I thought that everyone in the world got jet lag, but I forgot that you were simply not of this world. Jet lag could not touch you, for your transcendental schedule was the same no matter where you were. Since you never slept for more than 2-3 hours at night and perhaps 1-2 hours during the day, you were immune from the effects of jet lag. You simply immediately adjusted yourself to the new time and took rest exactly as if you had always been there. You also took your meals at exactly the same time each day; although I could not understand how to eat at such odd hours, you were ready to eat whenever the clock said it was 8:30 a.m. or 1:00 p.m., regardless of the time of your internal bio-clock. Indeed, that internal clock seemed to be totally under your control, exactly as it would be in the body of a great mystic *yogī*.

They say that a Vaiṣṇava has great mystic power. Although I have seen you display power mystically, this amazing capacity of yours to adjust to any time, place, or situation convinced me that it is due to mystic strength only. No one else can be as effortlessly transcendental as you.

I never could understand how you could be so enthusiastic on your morning walks. I was a young man in my mid-twenties and you were an old man in your late seventies, yet you could walk circles around me while preaching a storm of pure instruction about Kṛṣṇa to your eager audience of young disciples. I was always so tired after these morning walks that I had to take rest immediately after them, while you were simply enlivened and ready to perform the rest of your daily activities. How was this so? I cannot understand, but it seems that you are simply not a person of this material world; you descended from the spiritual platform, directly from the pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Please bless me that I may someday be engaged in those eternal pastimes as your eternal servant in the spiritual sky.

How can a person preach all day and night, sometimes without a break? Of course, mundane politicians, eager to satisfy the electorate, may also perform such feats of speaking without end, but who among them can speak about Kṛṣṇa for more than a few minutes? People can speak all nonsense for hours and years when it is related to their sense gratification, but when they have to speak about Kṛṣṇa they can only speak for some minutes before they have had enough. You could speak about Kṛṣṇa your whole life without ever becoming satiated or tired. Your eyes would beam with enthused brightness when you were given the opportunity to preach for extended periods of time, especially when you could expose such rascals of modern society as the politicians, the scientists, and the so-called scholars. You never backed away from a good fight, and you reveled in the struggle to present Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the ignorant masses.

You ate very little—a few bites at breakfast and merely a handful of *prasādam* at lunch. Sometimes you would drink the water from a dob or a glass of milk. Your servants would wonder how anyone could live and be strong on such a limited diet, but your physical strength was amazing for a person your age.

In your final days you did not eat for months on end. You became so skinny that we could not tolerate seeing you, yet you were continuously writing your books. One might think that your purports would have become simpler and a bit disjointed due to your diseased and weakened condition, but on the contrary, an examination of the final chapters written by you in the Tenth Canto reveals a clarity of mind, a scholarly understanding, and a continuing perfect compassion on the living entities who would read the book. We are simply bewildered at how you could write so nicely even up to the very end of life. We pray to you that at the end of our lives we may reflect even a portion of your transcendental consciousness and determination.

No one can properly glorify you with their words, since words often fall short of expressing the true greatness of truly great personalities. But your own actions speak louder than numberless words, and therefore I have tried to present you as you are without excess verbiage. Please bless me with greater and greater appreciation of your glories and allow me to eternally serve your lotus feet, fully expecting your mercy to flow upon me without cessation. I am nothing with-

out your blessings and guidance. Please accept me and engage me in your eternal service.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you.

Your eternal servant,  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

Falling down straight like a rod, I offer my repeated obeisances at the lotus feet of His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Growing up and then growing old in Kṛṣṇa consciousness are both sublime, eternally beneficial experiences, and they are simply due to his causeless mercy. As he wrote of his spiritual master (and how much more we could say of Śrīla Prabhupāda), “If you had not come, who would have told / the message of Kṛṣṇa, forceful and bold? This gives us a life anew and fresh, … miracle done, Your Divine Grace.”

How refreshing and enlivening it is to the soul to be busy, busy, busy in your service, Śrīla Prabhupāda, without a moment left over for meaningless thoughts. As we grow in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we can only become more and more amazed by your character, your teaching, your practical counsel, for they are all constituted of pure and limitless Kṛṣṇa consciousness. To render you eternal devotional service, you who are the purest servant of Kṛṣṇa, is the least we can do. Thank you Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Trying to serve your servants,  
Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Jagadīśa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. I write this letter as an offering for your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration this year.

On behalf of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His servants, you have saved the world from darkness, a grand accomplishment that no one thought possible.

In so doing you have collected a rascal like me as a disciple. Please forgive me for still not being a good servant.

I wish to help, even if in a small way, to fulfill your noble plans to reverse the present diabolic drift of human society and to resurrect real civilization as a service to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Today in meditation I have seen that a good servant must have no motive other than to work for his master. When will I achieve that? When will my personal motives cease?

Please help me to become your good servant. Please bless me that I can set aside all selfish considerations and become one hundred percent dedicated.

Your eternal servant,  
Jagadīśa Goswami

## Kavicandra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Another year has passed, and as we grow older, time seems to move faster. You have explained that to Lord Kṛṣṇa, even the life of Lord Brahmā is like one second. So of what significance is my life? The only value is that by the mercy of your devotees, I have some chance to be connected with Your Divine Grace. As time is moving faster, it seems that I am less able to do anything useful in your service, but I can at least see that your children and grandchildren are working hard to fulfill your transcendental desires. I have some hope that I can someday begin devotional service, but as time continues to move faster, that day seems further away.

Your annual Gaura Pūrṇimā festival has just ended. As you desire, so many devotees came from various remote corners of the world and chanted, “*Jaya* Śacī-nandana!” In this way you continue to please Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura. This year’s festival featured some special highlights. First was the transcendental report of book distribution and production. Everyone was amazed by the results and by the sublime humility of those who distributed the most books. Everyone became inspired to go on working to please you in this way. Another inspiration was the new BBT building in Māyāpura. This building came up very fast and houses modern equipment for producing your books in East India. Of course, it goes without saying, but we must say, that your books are the basis of the worldwide revolution and the source of life for all Vaiṣṇava activities.

A special highlight was the Navadvīpa *parikrama*. More than five hundred men and women and children walked and camped in the holy *dhāma* for seven ecstatic days. The constant *sādhu-saṅga, kṛṣṇa-kīrtana,* and *kṛṣṇa-kathā* served to reveal the glories of the holy *dhāma* to everyone. The Māyāpura management organized everything very nicely so that everyone could concentrate on the holy name and pastimes of Lord Gaurāṅga.

Of course, the Vṛndāvana side was sublime, with daily *parikramas* and seminars. The devotees were overwhelmed by the beauty of your Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Deities. We also saw that your books are displayed in many places around the temple and sold profusely. So the many pilgrims who come daily get the topmost transcendental benefit from their visit. Bhaktivedanta Swami Marga has become a very busy road.

Now many devotees are gathering for the Kumbha Mela at Ujjain. You desired nice organization for preaching at Kumbha Mela, and that is being done. Book distribution, *harināma,* and *prasādam* distribution will go on in a big way.

*yāṅra citte kṛṣṇa-premā karaye udaya*  
*tāṅra vākya, kriyā, mudrā vijñeha nā bujhaya*

**“Even the most learned man cannot understand the words, activities, and symptoms of a person situated in love of Godhead” (Cc.** ***Madhya*** **23.39).**

Many times we were, and still are, confused by your transcendental orders. But as time goes on and we observe those who strictly follow you, we can get a glimpse of your perfection. We cannot see the mind or the wind, but we can observe its influence. You are surely a “Vaikuṇṭha man,” and a fool like myself can never appreciate your greatness. But by your mercy I can see the results of your desires and orders. I simply must pray that you always allow me to stay in the association of your genuine followers and that they will be kind enough to give me some service. Please let me try to glorify you by my mind, words, and deeds. Without a connection with Your Divine Grace, this life is not human and has no value. Your glories are perfectly sung by the *Vedas* and the *ācāryas*. I am like a fly trying to follow Garuḍa. That is a seemingly impossible task, but please allow me to try.

Your aspiring servant,  
Kavicandra Swami

## Loka-sākṣī dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Out of kindness to the suffering people of this world, Lord Kṛṣṇa has come as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In the mood of His dearmost devotee, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, and accompanied by Lord Baladeva, His brother and the original *guru* of the universe, They are relieving the distress cause by forgetting our loving relationship with Kṛṣṇa.

Appearing like a rising sun, you are part of Mahāprabhu’s plan to give everyone the ambrosial nectar of love of God, and you are the fullness of Lord Nityānanda’s mercy because you are also looking for the Jāgais and Mādhāis like my wretched self and reeling me in as a likely devotee.

There is no parallel to your kindness and love, which are the prototypes of all kinds of welfare work.

Please, my lord and savior, help me cleanse away all the *anarthas* accumulated through endless births, and engage me again in the service of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, under your eternal guidance.

Your most insignificant servant,  
Loka-sākṣī dāsa

## Madhusevita dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupada,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

I beg you to take one fraction of your transcendental time to read the words of a most neophyte disciple of yours.

Your fully spiritual person is engaged in so many perfect preaching projects throughout the countless universes that come out of the pores of Mahā-Viṣṇu’s body!

You are serving Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava in your glorious *siddha-deha* beyond the sphere of mundane limitation.

Forgive me for having thought of you as someone confined in an old man’s body. That was your *līlā*. You accepted this position only to deliver the science of Kṛṣṇa to us.

Your association is the most elevated; you know Lord Brahmā, Śrī Nārada Muni, Śrīla Vyāsadeva, Śrīla Rūpa and Raghunātha Gosvāmīs, along with Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī; you are eternally serving the lotus feet of Śrīla Vārṣabhānavī-devī-dayita dāsa, your beloved eternal father, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī.

With all these most elevated personalities you are tasting the confidential mellows of service to Lord Caitanya and Nityānanda, and the talks you exchange with them about Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa are nectar sublime.

On the contrary, I am a member of this host of beings eternally lost in oblivion. I was bound to be forever created, maintained, and annihilated in the ocean of material energy, picking through the garbage of material desires.

You are like a munificent nobleman who picks up a young ragamuffin from the street to elevate him to the standard of a decent human being.

When, for a moment, the cloud of my senseless pride leaves a small opening and I know by intuition your constitutional glory, the limited nature of my vision of you and the service I render to you makes me feel so ridiculous.

Śrīla Prabhupada, I feel so ridiculous and useless.

Your every thought, word, and action is *sac-cid-ānanda,* and Kṛṣṇa is so happy with you. He is actually dependent upon you, as He himself confirms throughout the Vedic literature.

He has bestowed upon you His *acintya-śakti,* the power of spreading devotional service even to those degraded beyond imagination.

You are manifesting the supramundane world of sound vibrations in the form of your books. They contain all the liberated entities and their relationships with Kṛṣṇa. All the previous *ācāryas* manifest themselves through you and are pleased by your preaching; therefore you are liberating millions of beings.

Śrī Śrī Māyāpura and Vṛndāvana *dhāmas’* lotus feet are being served by you, and as a result, monumental cities of love of God are being erected to give shelter to the fallen conditioned souls.

I live in fear that one day you’ll become disillusioned by my character and send me back into the streets of desolation.

To learn how to be grateful to you by following your instructions means to find my way back to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. The eternal care of the unlimited number of His loving devotees is what I aspire for.

There are so many enthusiastic devotees in this movement, but unfortunately I am a hypocrite. Your transcendental personality pervades their lives. You are in their minds, their words, and their bodies. You are teaching the supreme science of *acintya-bhedābheda-tattva* by expanding your desire to serve Kṛṣṇa through your loving disciples and followers, but the poverty of my love for you makes me negate this fact and keeps me under the grip of ignorance and passion, victimized by the six enemies of the soul. My vision is that of a *kaniṣṭha adhikārī*. I separate you from the devotees of this movement.

You have thousands of hands that serve you by distributing books, thousands of lips that serve your order by chanting the *mahā-mantra,* and thousands of minds that meditate on your desire to expand the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

You are Kṛṣṇa’s empowered representative. Please, Śrīla Prabhupada, free this deluded soul from his materialistic conceptions.

They are obstacles that impede the vision of the Personality of Servitor Godhead.

Let me see you in your followers, and let me serve them with undeviated mind.

Bless me with a taste for cooperation and humility in the family of ISKCON.

Please be affectionate to me, for without you I am lost in the world of *māyā*.

Grasping your lotus feet tight to my heart, I beg to remain your son and disciple,  
Mādhusevita dāsa

## Mukunda Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Each day when we sing *ebe yaśa ghuṣuk tri-bhuvana,* I try to realize how your qualities are appreciated throughout the universe, especially in Gokula Vṛndāvana itself, where Lord Kṛṣṇa, the original Personality of Godhead, resides. To me you once said of your Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, “He was no ordinary spiritual master.”

Some people want to be mystified by a spiritual master’s display of mystic powers. Your mystic power, Śrīla Prabhupāda, was the manner in which you lived your maxim “utility is the principle,” far beyond human capabilities.

Throughout your time with us you never stopped writing the “lawbooks,” as you once called them, for this planet’s human population, even up to the point of your departure to the spiritual world. With deep determination to execute your Śrīla Prabhupāda’s (Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s) will, you built and managed an international organization from your humble rooms, sitting on small cushions, dictating letters, and personally directing your raw recruits.

As with your translations and purports, you acted with infallible precision in all management decisions, without the help of trained personnel. You simply didn’t make mistakes, which are the heritage of the human race.

Your direction for ISKCON was also the ultimate honesty, the complete sharing of the profoundest knowledge. This is so because you gave everything of yourself, intellectually and emotionally, to communicate to the very root of each person, to the soul itself.

Your practical answers for a godless—”Use gold coins”; “Protect cows and develop local food production”; “Don’t depend on artificial necessities”; “Ban the divorce law”; “Implement *varṇāśrama*”; “Punish murderers with the death penalty”; “Outlaw abortion”; “Make *kīrtana* compulsory in schools”; “Become self-sufficient”; “Distribute *prasādam* unlimitedly”; “Expose cheating scientists”; “Preach government support for religious principles”—proved your determination to take on the whole world, providing functional solutions based on complete knowledge of Kṛṣṇa.

You constantly preached wherever you went, circling the world non-stop in ripe old age, against the advice of friends in India and elsewhere, who could not understand your transcendental vision or the urgency of your mission.

By meditating on your life and mission during your time with us and your earlier life in India, all of which reveals an eternal mission as Lord Kṛṣṇa’s greatest modern-day ambassador to the earth, we are inspired to serve you with every ounce of our energy.

Your life and teachings give us a glimpse into how great sages like Vyāsadeva and Jīva Gosvāmī produced massive quantities of flawless spiritual literature, perfectly focused on bringing about a revolution in today’s misdirected civilization. As part of this great continuum, you are a fully qualified *mahā-bhāgavata*.

This is how I perceive your mystic power, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your mission has grown in the fashion of a true miracle. During a conversation with reporters in New Delhi in March of 1976, you were asked if ISKCON was “self-sufficient.” You proudly replied, “We are feeding at least ten thousand devotees daily, but we have no fixed income.… We are traveling all over the world.… Our income, daily collection, is not less than one *lakh* of rupees, and we are spending everything.” And you established the process by saying, “So how it is going on? On Kṛṣṇa’s grace. We depend on Kṛṣṇa.”

Because of your complete surrender to and dependence upon the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa, He directly gave you the greatest facilities to successfully canvass on His behalf on the request of your Guru Mahārāja.

Your eternal servant,  
Mukunda Goswami

## Nirañjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, I beg that you please accept this humble offering and prayer unto your lotus feet.

**A Prayer for Protection**

Who am I?  
To speak from Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,  
As you did with greater purity,  
Enlightening everyone with perfect clarity,  
Instilling faith in the Supreme Personality,  
Inspiring devotion for eternity,  
And creating perfect harmony.  
I shouldn’t even dare to speak,  
But, I know that it’s my duty,  
For I was an object of your compassion.

Who am I?  
To travel and preach,  
As you did unceasingly,  
Not caring for any residency,  
Nor looking for personal charity,  
Compelled ever so compassionately  
To rescue souls lost in iniquity.  
I shouldn’t dare to imitate,  
But, by your mercy only,  
I was an object of your compassion.

Who am I?  
To adopt the renounced order,  
As you did in complete detachment;  
No attraction for family and friends,  
You were fixed in *parātma-niṣṭham*,  
And thus preached with equal vision  
To everyone without discretion.  
All I did was change my dress.  
O Prabhupāda, please keep pushing me,  
For I’m an object of your compassion.

Who am I?  
To manage the affairs of your Society,  
As you did with such authority,  
Because you commanded it by your purity,  
And those who heard could clearly see  
That you embodied Lord Kṛṣṇa’s decree  
Of full surrender to His lotus feet.  
I shouldn’t attempt to impose myself,  
But, it’s to protect your sacred mission,  
Which made me an object of your compassion.

Who am I?  
To accept worship  
As you did for our benefit,  
Not out of mundane sentiment,  
But for increasing our reverence,  
Because I’m too proud and impudent,  
And you know that’s such a detriment.  
I shouldn’t even think of it,  
But I’m trying to on your order,  
So that I’ll remain an object of your compassion

O Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
I know that I’m nothing without you.  
I did nothing to deserve your compassion.  
But since it is so expansive,  
I’m caught in your loving network,  
And thus there’s no other reason  
To adopt this imitative behavior.  
The debt that you’ve left with me  
Just won’t ever let me forget  
That I was an object of your compassion.

Please protect me!

Your eternal servant,  
Nirañjana Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the years go by it seems I’m standing still,  
Trying, yet unable, your instructions to fulfill.  
Please bless this fool to always stay at your lotus feet,  
And give me strength and courage Māyā’s influence to defeat.

As we travel around the world, we can always see  
Devotees in every country striving to set people free  
From bewilderment, illusion, anger, lust, and greed  
By distributing your books—this is the greatest need.  
They have captured your mood, these enthusiastic souls,  
Always eager to please you—that is their life’s goal.  
You never tired of preaching till the very end,  
And you continue to live with us through all of them.  
Your attitude was always very grave and sober,  
Yet you often displayed a delightful sense of humor.  
Your devotees also like to laugh and dance with joy,  
Yet are deeply devoted to the blue cowherd boy.

They rise early in the morning to chant the holy name,  
Then hear the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* describe Lord Kṛṣṇa’s fame  
In the way that you have taught, by your example divine,  
How to concentrate the mind in Kṛṣṇa consciousness sublime.

“Repeat what guru speaks; change nothing,” you have said.  
“Anyone who deviates will become spiritually dead.”  
So you left us with such wonderful transcendental instructions  
In your lectures, books, letters, morning walks, and conversations.

Your dear devotees love to recall your pastimes sweet.  
“Śrīla Prabhupāda said this, did that,” they eagerly speak.  
On hearing such extremely enlivening recollections,  
We’re more inspired to preach and render service to your mission.  
By your divine arrangement, we all can regularly meet  
At Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhav’s lotus feet,  
In ISKCON’s Śrī Śrī Māyāpura Chandrodaya Mandir,  
At the Gaur Pūrṇimā festival every single year.

And each year we’re reminded more and more of you  
As this powerful saṅkīrtana movement ever continues to grow,  
Books of every language resplendently fill your room,  
Produced by advanced devotees empowered by your boon.

So dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly hear my plea.  
I pray that in your house there’s also a place for me.  
Please help me to become from all offenses free  
So that I can render service to you and your devotees.

Thus one day I may also become a devotee.

Your worthless servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most fallen *daṇḍavats* at your feet.

For twelve extraordinary years you crossed and recrossed the world, sowing the seeds of love of Kṛṣṇa. Who can actually know the extent of your work? Wherever you went, you broadcast the seeds of *bhakti*—by your footfall, by your speech, by your glance. And wherever in the nooks and crannies of this earth your various energies came to alight, the seeds of *bhakti* scattered and spread—carried by your books, your recorded voice, your followers. To this day no one knows the breadth and depth of your work.

One day it will be known. Your greatness will become manifest. You sowed the seeds, and I labor joyfully with your followers in the fields you planted to nurse the huge harvest of love to fruition; I work so that your glories can be known. Each day we uncover new fields, discover growing testimony to you great work. Each day we mark the indomitable growth. We get a hint of the dimensions of what is to come.

I am the most fortunate person in all the worlds to have had your association and to be able even now to keep your association by following your order and doing your work. I undertake these things for your glorification.

Pleading to remain forever at your lotus feet, I remain  
Your unworthy servant,  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Romapāda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear spiritual master, savior of the whole world, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

How is one so tiny as myself to express the glories of one so great?

The event of your Vyāsa-pūjā is filled with auspicious spiritual potencies. The whole assembly of Vaiṣṇavas worldwide is receiving your transcendental blessings just by participating in your glorification on this day. We stand before you like tiny glowing lamps on an ornamental string, singing our eulogies, each of us radiating but a fraction of your full splendor. The worldwide chorus of such glowing ornamentations is but a part of a far grander festival—the festival of your brilliant, unalloyed devotion to Śrī Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu and to Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. All the three worlds and the personified *Vedas* also sing your praises, in honor of your unalloyed love of Kṛṣṇa, which is so rare in this world. And even among the stalwart *ācāryas,* the caliber and magnitude of your unparalleled compassion is as significant to all life within this entire world as one’s very life air or beating heart is within one individual body.

**All-Auspicious**

Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī gives us a definition of auspiciousness in his *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu,* and in your *Nectar of Devotion* we find it described in this way: “Actual auspiciousness means welfare activities for all people of the world.” When we take part in honoring your Vyāsa-pūjā, then “actual auspiciousness,” exactly as Rūpa Gosvāmī described it, is created or made manifest. Why is this a true conclusion? Because it was by your own heart’s unchecked desire, by your tireless dedication to the order of Guru and Gaurāṅga, by your full-life’s mission, that persons in every nation of the entire world have accepted the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness!

And you mercifully left some service for us to perform, carrying on the world preaching activity which you began.

No territorial perimeter can circumscribe your compassion. And your followers, Śrīla Prabhupāda, can continue to manifest the same “global auspiciousness” by the simple activities of glorifying you and rendering all kinds of services unto you.

A passage in the *Caitanya-maṅgala,* by Locana dāsa Ṭhākura, directly confirms your position as the bearer of all auspiciousness for the entire world. Locana dāsa Ṭhākura describes a visit Nārada Muni once paid to Kṛṣṇa’s palaces in Dvārakā, along with the ensuing conversation between him and Queen Rukmiṇī. Nārada expresses his concern for the impending perils of the Age of Kali, soon to come after Lord Kṛṣṇa’s departure from this world. He sees how the conditioned souls, devoid of *bhakti,* will become completely victimized by the degrading influences of Kali-yuga.

Queen Rukmiṇī replies that the following age will be blessed by the appearance of *bhakti* once again, by the agency of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, as well as by one of His chief generals, or assistants, who will spread *bhakti* throughout the entire world!

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

**Śakti-vistāriṇī**

Here in New York City, at 26 Second Avenue, we have begun regular glorification of your transcendental *līlā* and *vāṇī* on our Śrīla Prabhupāda Appreciation Night. And just as on your Vyāsa-pūjā day, when guests and devotees alike come together to appreciate your wonderful spiritual potencies, those same potencies come alive to be experienced once again by both speaker and audience!

My personal appreciation of this *śakti-vistāriṇī* phenomenon, or the expanding of transcendental potencies emanating from the glorification of your activities, is that all stages of *bhakti* are nourished by events featuring personal homages to you. Rūpa Gosvāmī affirms the immense spiritual potencies to be found within auspicious days like Ekādaśī, Janmāṣṭamī, etc. And the same is the case for your glorification.

I can distinctly remember the super-powerful effect of hearing your glories described, even by non-practitioners of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, during my college days—what to speak of early encounters with your loving, adoring servants and devotees!

I boldly proclaim that the whole world benefits from your Vyāsa-pūjā. It is the same kind of benefit produced by the sages in the remote forest of Naimiṣāraṇya over five thousand years ago. Not only were the sages assembled in that remote forest at that particular time benefited, likewise the local forest dwellers, by the sanctification of Sūta Gosvāmī’s narration, but the whole world, for all time, received the fruits of their chanting and hearing of the glories of the Lord. So the same is true regarding the glorification of eminently exalted Vaiṣṇavas such as you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is one concluding personal note I would like to express in this Vyāsa-pūjā homage. In the summer of 1974, one of my Godbrothers and I were duscussing the request from Your Divine Grace for a replacement for your personal servant to accompany you in your world preaching mission. This conversation took place in the Henry Street temple, and my Godbrother friend was the candidate being considered; he was asking my advice, whether or not to accept the offer. He finally declined, and I knew at the time that if I were to voice my strong desire to fill in as a replacement, serious consideration would have been given to that request. But, like a fool, I remained silent.

My service was *saṅkīrtana* leader, and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, were already putting so much stress on book distribution. So I felt that for me it was best to remain with “service in separation.”

But over and over again that Henry Street conversation has crossed my mind. “I should have taken the opportunity, presented on a golden platter, to render that personal service to Śrīla Prabhupāda! And now it is too late!!”

Now history repeats itself. I have been requested to take on a new service as assistant GBC of New York, a very confidential and important service to you and your worldwide ISKCON. Please give me the intelligence and strength to render this service to the satisfaction of Your Divine Grace and my fellow Godbrothers here in New York. I am but a fool, trying to do my small part to assist your mission. Therefore, without your blessings and the blessings of my fellow Godbrothers, I will remain but a fool, causing only disturbances. By your grace, may I have the vision to complement the abilities of local leaders and become your instrument for furthering the great mission you have entrusted to us all, your faithful followers.

And if rain-checks are allowed in the spiritual world of Goloka, please allow me your personal service after you have completed your all-merciful task of dragging me back home, back to Godhead. This is my Number One aspiration.

Your aspiring servant,  
Romapāda Swami

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances and this humble offering on your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

**Vṛndāvana Meditation**

It was one of those wonderful mornings, like all mornings in Vṛndāvana. The sun had begun its auspicious ascent in the clear sky, resting on the forested horizon. The air was crisp, heavy with the sweetness that is the pastimes of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Birds greeted the new day, their songs rising in a unified *kīrtana,* as Vṛndāvana forest’s own choir.

I was inspired by that atmosphere, and thus a resolve arose in my mind to circumambulate the town of Vṛndāvana. Paying my obeisances in the dust of the holy *dhāma,* I invoked auspiciousness by the remembrance of your name. Casting aside pretense in the form of my sandals, I set off on Vṛndāvana *parikrama,* and remembrance of you.

**Preface**

A few hours earlier I had sat in the still darkness, scrutinizing your *Bhagavad-gītā* purports. Absorbed in the realm of your transcendental intellect, I became inspired by the greatness of your books. That vision now marks my meditation.

Alone, walking the path between *āśramas,* I am greeted by the banging of sticks and loud shouts: *Ha! Ha! Ha!* Mischievous monkeys are driven away by *bābājīs,* scampering in tribes from rooftop to rooftop.

I consider your voice more effective than such efforts. Through your purports you dispel mischievous Kali from the courtyard of the heart, rendering that secluded place suitable for the appearance of the Lord.

Ramaṇa Reti is the place of Kṛṣṇa’s and Balarāma’s pastimes while tending cows. Those same brothers, having appeared as Gaura and Nitāi, are the inspiration of your pen. Saving the fallen is Their mission.

You as Their representative take up that task worldwide, while everyone sleeps in the comfort of their own *bhajan*.

**Bhagavad-gītā**

Peacocks raise a concert throughout the ancient forest, dancing in the soft sands, exhibiting their billowing plumage, their many painted eyes seeing everywhere.

Eighteen wonderful chapters, like so many eyes by which to perceive the Truth. With many eyes, as many perspectives, but in your vision they all lead to devotional service. By such knowledge, relationship in devotion to Kṛṣṇa is clear. Therefore you are truly a Bhaktivedānta.

Past Varāha-ghāṭa I turn east, to the well-worn path of the old Yamunā bed. Once that divine river flowed here, but now, due to offenses she has retreated to a distant place.
