How many editions of the *Gītā* are there? Countless! The more that are printed, the less is understood. Māyāvādī commentators commit heinous offenses to the Lord, the meaning of His words forever distant to such rascals.

You have brought back the flow of the river of transcendental knowledge, once again bathing the parched sands of the hearts of aspiring transcendentalists, presenting the *Gītā* “As It Is.”

The high walls of the Nimbārka headquarters was once a proud symbol of devotion to the Lord. Now there remains only a decayed ruin of brick and mortar, overgrown with trees and moss. Such was the condition of the *sampradāyas* before your advent.

Your purports are not dry words, but juicy nectar that inspires realized service to Kṛṣṇa, even in lands distant from here. They glorify a living tradition, the living books bringing to life the timeless culture of *bhakti.*

The domes of Madana-mohana temple grace Dvadaśāditya-tilā. That noble structure is decaying under the influence of time. *Sambandha-jñāna,* which Madana-mohanjī inspired and Sanātana Gosvāmī teaches, will continue for thousands of years through the distribution of your *Gītā*.

If due to neglect these temples of Vṛndāvana turn to dust, their glory will remain forever alive in the pages of your books.

On the bank of the Yamunā, washermen’s children play, determined to bathe a donkey in the cool sapphire-colored waters. Amid much laughter and fun, two pull the beast from the front, one kick on each side, and still a fifth stands at the back, brandishing a stick. Equally determined, stubborn to the extreme, the animal refuses to enter.

Yet you inspire legions of devotees, less than washermen’s sons, to entice donkey brains to enter the cooling rivers of transcendental knowledge and bathe in the words of Lord Kṛṣṇa. It is no mean task, but remains the steady desire of your compassion-filled heart.

**Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam**

As the decadence of Indian materialism has spoiled this country’s spiritual heritage, at Keśī-ghāṭa the Yamunā has receded, being a *ghāṭa* to sewage alone.

In the pages of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the pastimes of Lord Kṛṣṇa are alive, forever expanding within the hearts of your readers. By your grace Vṛndāvana is now manifest worldwide, beyond the purview of the local residents.

The ancient tree at Vaṁśīvaṭa, its great branches spread wide, is decorated by many colored cloths—the dresses of the *gopīs* as they bathed in the river blow. Then the Lord manifested His confidential pastime of *vastra-harana,* fulfilling the *gopīs’* inner desire to worship Him as their husband.

Confidential revelations such as these, innocently portrayed and expertly projected to the masses by your all-perfect descriptions, reveal your unsurpassed devotional poetic ability.

Lord Govinda stands by the shore of the Yamunā, illuminated by the soft glow of a full autumn moon, playing His flute and attracting the doelike hearts of the *gopīs*. Better not venture that way if you value your attachments within the realm of material enjoyment.

In the cities of the *karmīs* your words flow like a soft flute-song through the mouths of your representatives. Merchants, politicians, and atheists are drawn by the irresistible beauty of a newly discovered figure—Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Vṛndāvana-dhāma is marked with many places where the Lord disposed of yet another demon, maintaining peace and harmony within His own abode. The words of great sages like Nārada, Vyāsa, and Śukadeva are faithfully delivered by Your Divine Grace through thirty-two beautiful volumes, and the offspring of Baka, Agha, and Ariṣṭa become candidates for liberation that even their forefather could not achieve.

The sound of clanging bells and the chanting of *mantras* rise from the town. It is the *sādhana* of thousands of devotees preparing for another day that will bring them closer to their long-sought destinations.

The process of devotion practiced here for centuries has remained a mystery to the world. Misrepresented by ambitious commentators, *bhakti* had been studied only by a few scholars as a means to an academic end. Now you offer that rare science of loving devotion, traditionally transmitted to the most intimate students, to all, despite their omission of not bowing to your lotus feet.

**Caitanya-caritāmṛta**

Daily Caitanya Mahāprabhu would sit at Imli-tāla, chanting the holy names of the Lord, fathomless tears gliding down His beautiful cheeks as He gazed over the Yamunā River. Only those very rare souls who are blessed by the Lord Himself can penetrate the mystery of those tears.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* you have revealed the confidential truths about Lord Caitanya. Faithfully adhering to the words of the author, you present the Lord as the perfect teacher and practitioner of devotional service.

A *sādhu* dressed only in *kaupinas,* with matted hair and horizontal *tilaka,* passes me on the path. Those who bow and worship here aspire for goals ranging from *mukti* to *bhakti*. Clearly there are varied aspirations of the residents, indicating uncertainty in the purpose of their *sādhana*. Despite their piety and high birth, they are unaware that, without the mercy of Lord Gaurāṅga, no one can fathom the devotional secrets glorified by every grain of dust in this holy land.

Out of your causeless mercy you have clearly delineated loving devotional service, rarely achieved by Lord Brahmā and the king of heaven. Who else but one empowered by Lord Nityānanda can reveal the sentiments of Śrī Rādhā, reflected in the heart of Lord Kṛṣṇa as He adopts Her golden complexion in the form of Lord Gaurāṅga?

Past Old Vṛndāvana, near a temple of Lord Jagannātha, stands a large banyan tree where Caitanya Mahāprabhu once sat. Bowing to the Lord while taking the dust from this place, I invoke your name as a transcendental password, praying for His mercy.

“O Lord Gaurāṅga, when will my steel-framed heart melt when I chant your holy names? When will I abandon my pride that I am a great devotee? When by Your mercy will I feel myself lower than a blade of grass, taking the dust of the feet of Vaiṣṇavas as my treasure, begging for their mercy with tear-filled eyes? Please make that true very soon, for my heart is burning with the insatiable fire of lust, and I cannot tolerate the misery of separation from You.”

Whatever feeble words escape our pride-frozen lips we have learned from you. Whatever inspiration we have to serve Lord Caitanya has been inspired by you. Whatever advancement we will make in the *saṅkīrtana* movement is due to you, and you alone. Your mercy, our only asset, now forever preserved in the pages of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*.

When Caitanya Mahāprabhu came to Vṛndāvana, all the peacocks, deer, and cows approached Him with tears in their eyes, as if meeting a friend after a very long time.

Residing at Akrura-ghāṭa, the Lord would visit Vṛndāvana daily, returning for His meals later in the day. On one occasion he reached Vaṁśīvaṭa, where Śrīdhāmā had been waiting for Lord Kṛṣṇa’s return from Mathurā. Extending his golden, serpentine arms, Lord Gaura cried out, “Śrīdhāmā, I am back!”

When by your mercy will that golden Lord, all so kind, saturated with intense love of Godhead, non-different from Lord Kṛṣṇa, manifest in my heart?

The path of devotional service practiced by the Vrajavāsīs is very rare and difficult to achieve. Lord Gaurāṅga has descended to freely distribute this gift to one and all. But we are so unfortunate, we have no attraction for it. How will it be achieved? Where may we find it?

Inscribed in the pages of golden-bound volumes are the secrets you have revealed—secrets yet unknown in the cities of the demigods. If we take up the offenseless chanting of the holy names in a mood of sincere separation, while boldly participating in the propagation of the Lord’s *saṅkīrtana* movement in the association of devotees, Caitanya Mahāprabhu will manifest all devotional truths within the heart, in which greed to achieve Him has arisen, by His mercy.

As Rāmānanda Rāya fully realized the identity of Lord Caitanya, so your followers, blessed by His grace, may one day see Gaurāṅga as Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa through the medium of their sincere devotion in the mood of humble servitude.

Here is the place where Vyāsa composed the *Vedas;* their essence, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam;* and the means to taste the *Bhāgavatam, Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. What took Brahmā millennia to understand by repeated study of all things known, you have explained clearly and succinctly in a few words. How fortunate are they who with little effort can, by your grace, acquire that knowledge not to be had by wandering for many lifetimes throughout the three worlds.

**Nectar of Devotion**

Warming the cool sand on the *parikrama* path, the sun is forever smiling, daily receiving the *darśana* of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī as She worships him for the attainment of the association of Her beloved Lord Kṛṣṇa.

The path of devotional service is illuminated by the mercy of Śrīmatī Rādhikā, through the instructions of Rūpa Gosvāmī, in your summary study, *Nectar of Devotion*.

The *parikrama* is a full six miles. A short journey, but sometimes seeming like forever. Step by step we cover the distance. From *śraddhā* to *prema,* the progressive steps of devotion devoid of coverings of *jñāna* and *karma,* known as *uttama-bhakti,* in and of themselves nectar, you clearly explain, bringing us closer to our destination.

One of those inevitable thorns breaks my stride. Having walked in Vṛndāvana for years, I have learned to expect as much anywhere, at any time.

By your direction, devotees will be able to continue on the path of devotion, overcoming the thorny obstacles to devotional service.

What Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī charged as being mere sentimentality you establish to be the most erudite scientific process of purified emotional exchange with the unlimited ocean of transcendental mellows, Lord Kṛṣṇa.

We pass by Dhāvānala-līḍha Kuṇḍa, where the Lord swallowed the forest fire, saving the cowherd boys, who had taken complete shelter of Him.

You are the personified form of unconditional surrender, knowing no other refuge than Lord Kṛṣṇa for the unlimited ills of material existence. You dismissed the bewildering array of order-supplier deities of Hinduism, replacing their worship with exclusive dependence on the Lord.

As the varied flora of Vṛndāvana offer *ārati* to the *dhāma* with their fragrance, a soft wind combines the scents to perfume the air.

By the favorable cultivation of *bhakti,* the combination of *vibhāva, anubhāva, sthāyi-bhāva, sañcāri-* and *sāttvika-bhāvas* manifest *rasa.* According to one’s inherent spiritual nature, one may serve Lord Kṛṣṇa in one of five transcendental mellows, ever residing in Vṛndāvana. In this way you conclude your teachings.

**Conclusion**

The domes of Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandir become visible; our *parikrama* has drawn to an end.

When will that day come when, through your transcendental instructions, the goal of my life becomes visible before my eyes?

Paying *daṇḍavats* where I started, I pray that the process of devotional service you have outlined in your transcendental books, from *sambandha* to *prayojana,* be fully manifest in my heart.

As each grain of dust, all-desire-fulfilling *cintāmaṇi,* is worshipable, I worship each and every word you have left for us to study.

As Vṛndāvana is as worshipable as Lord Kṛṣṇa, your books are as worshipable as you. Did you not say you eternally reside in them?

As Lord Kṛṣṇa is inseparable from His beloved Vṛndāvana, I beg you for unflinching faith in your instructions and guidance . . . Faith that supersedes sensual perception, logic, and intellectual arguments. Faith that will make your words my only life and soul. Faith to take shelter at the feet of the Gosvāmīs. Faith that manifests the realm of the inconceivable. Faith that will reveal pure devotional service to Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa in Vṛndāvana-dhāma. Faith by which I may see you once again.

Your humble servant,  
Śivarāma Swami

## Śrīdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, I reaffirm my complete confidence in your ability to save all the fallen, conditioned souls in this Kali-yuga.

When I think of your dedication to Lord Caitanya’s preaching mission, your hard work and uncompromising spirit, I feel thrilled to have had your direct association for even a short time. I want to follow in your footsteps. You are my hero, my master, my guiding star.

I am most attracted by your courage—courage to fight the forces of Kali, to fight Māyā in all her forms, to establish Lord Caitanya’s mission. I would not like to minimize your effort by suggesting you had an easy time.

I humbly submit to all my brothers that your struggle was the greatest of all. You told us that Kṛṣṇa tested you by making you poverty-stricken and He tested you by giving you everything. You said that the first year in America was a constant struggle. Weekly you went to the steamship company to inquire when the boat back to India was leaving. But ultimately you never left your service to your Guru Mahārāja, although the task seemed impossible: to transform *mlecchas* into pure Vaiṣṇavas.

A disciple asked you, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, what makes you happy?” and you replied, “I am happy when I see you struggle.”

By this statement you meant that when we meet with difficulty in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we have to persevere. When we have difficulty in our family lives or personal *sādhana,* we have to struggle and persevere. Absolute faith in the simple process you gave us—that is the answer.

If a devotee falls down from the standard a thousand times, he has to get back up and battle against ignorance a thousand times. There is no need to create a new standard. We just have to stubbornly keep trying to follow the standard you gave us. We don’t need psychiatrists or New Age “*gurus*” to help us discover “the subtle psychic inner self beyond.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for telling us the truth. There is no shortcut alternative to the day-to-day execution of devotional service.

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.15.22 states, “By making plans with determination, one should give up lusty desires for sense gratification.” In your purport you comment, “One cannot give up thinking of women, for thinking in this way is natural; even while walking on the street, one will see so many women. However, if one is determined not to live with a woman, even while seeing a woman he will not become lusty. If one is determined not to have sex, he can automatically conquer lusty desires.”

The example is that even if one is hungry, if on a particular day he is determined to observe fasting, he can naturally conquer the disturbances of hunger and thirst.

Paraphrasing the politician’s wife: “You just have to say NO to Māyā.”

So, whether I am a man or wo-man (any kind of man), I have to decide my priority in life, and if I value Kṛṣṇa consciousness, if I have faith in you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, then I have to make a supreme effort to control my senses. I am completely responsible for my own situation. I can’t blame anyone else.

If I can only surrender 10% to you, then I understand that I can only expect a proportionate amount of your mercy by which I can be transcendental to the miseries of material existence.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were the perfect gentleman. You asked us to rise up and overcome our wretched past lives. You wanted us to become gentlemen, Vaiṣṇavas, men of culture. We have to struggle for that … it’s the price we have to pay to free ourselves from the cycle of repeated birth and death.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you struggled to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the real thing, in America. Now we, your disciples, have to struggle again to rebuild your mission, which we have allowed to atrophy. Now is not the time to blame any person or group for our condition. Now is the time for all sincere devotees to step forward while they still have health and life and work together to establish your glory.

Your servant,  
Śrīdhara Swami

## Suhotra Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

There is a story about a poor widow who was desperate to educate her son so that he might be successful and bring money to the household. For this purpose she borrowed money to hire a tutor.

One day that unfortunate woman listened from another room as the tutor instructed her son in geometry. “Let ABC be a triangle,” was all she heard before she angrily rushed in to rebuke the man. “I pay you ten rupees a month. For that I have been expecting you to raise my boy to higher levels of learning. But today I see that after all this time you have not been able to bring him beyond simple ABC’s! He learned the alphabet long ago. So as you are only wasting his time and my money, I order you to leave my house and never return!” So angry was she that there was no possibility of explaining to her that the ABC’s of geometry are indeed of a higher order of knowledge than she had yet experienced.

In an age of faithlessness brought about by bad experience of religious fanaticism, many so-called intellectuals react to the teachings of Your Divine Grace as did the widow to the tutor’s geometry class. They need only hear the words “God,” “devotion,” and “spiritual world” before rejecting out of hand the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness as just another type of blind faith.

How great is their misfortune! “O my Lord Urukrama,” Brahmā prays in the Third Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* “all these poor creatures are constantly perplexed by hunger, thirst, severe cold, secretion, and bile, attacked by coughing winter, blasting summer, rains, and many other disturbing elements, and overwhelmed by strong sex urges and indefatigable anger. I take pity on them, and I am very much aggrieved for them.” Spiritually numbed by their sybaritic technological culture, modern men and women wallow vainly in up-to-date prejudices against all that smacks of religion, yet are fettered like dogs or pigs by the coils of the threefold miseries (miseries caused by their own bodies and minds, miseries caused by other living entities, and miseries caused by the powers behind nature). Even as they scoff at God and His representatives, ridiculing the contradictions they find among various belief systems and mocking the simple devotion of the faithful, the coils of the threefold miseries choke off their guffawing and drag them down to swallow the karmic filth of their self-indulgences. And all the while they are pitied by the Lord’s pure devotees, who view their struggling from the transcendental platform of unrestricted consciousness.

The proud-blind fools are to be pitied because their knowledge is not knowledge at all but simply self-righteous ignorance, as the widow’s “knowledge” of the tutor’s lessons was ignorance. This “knowledge” is nothing but a burden that stains the consciousness with pride and delusion, as much as a burden of dung carried in a wicker basket upon the head stains the bearer when it rains.

The great souls—Vaiṣṇavas like Brahmā, Nārada, and Vyāsa—moved by pity for a world filled with fools, took counsel among themselves and petitioned Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s help. And so Lord Caitanya sent you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to relieve these fallen souls of the burden and stain of their modern “knowledge” (indeed, you said that exposing these rascals was your special mission). Your words, razor-sharp with crystalline realization, cut through the coils of the threefold miseries. You so kindly lift the fallen soul up from the muck of his own illusion and invite him to open his eyes and see directly the all-beautiful Lord of the senses, Hṛṣīkeśa, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. That soul who does so will surely curse himself (even as he drinks the beauty of the Lord through purified vision) for having wasted countless lifetimes in darkness speculating about the nature of the absolute, as did the blind men about the elephant.

Smilingly, you encourage the astonished soul even further: “Now that you’ve seen Him, why don’t you serve Him?” And by your example, you show that serving Kṛṣṇa is so simple, pure, blissful, and natural. And that soul who follows your example will surely curse himself (even as his senses are flooded with the nectar of Kṛṣṇa’s loving acceptance of his service) for having lain spiritually dead for eons together, his consciousness focused by Māyā upon an endless succession of pathetic material bodies that were capable of only twitching with lust and flinching with pain.

And finally you show the newly-awakened soul the greatest wonder of all, that at which the Lord Himself expresses amazement: “As chaste women bring their gentle husbands under control by service, the pure devotees, who are equal to everyone and completely attached to Me in the core of the heart, bring Me under their full control.”

All that is described above about the awakening and deliverance of the conditioned souls was manifest by you during your earthly pastimes, and continues to be manifest as your ISKCON movement expands.

Now, among those who were in some way touched by the compassion of Your Divine Grace, there are those who assert that your teachings are only the “ABC’s of the spiritual world,” which, while certainly vastly superior to the most erudite expressions of materialism, only usher the devotee onto the threshold of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. They would have us move beyond the “alphabet” taught by you to the “geometry” taught by others.

To them too I respectfully dedicate the story of the widow and the tutor, and add the following story as well.

Some herons standing in a bog saw a swan happening by. They had never seen a swan before, and their curiosity was aroused.

“Why are your eyes, beak, and feet so red?” one of the herons asked the swan.

“Because I’m a swan.”

“Well, where do you come from?”

“From the Mānasa-sarovara Lake.”

“And how are things over there?”

“The lake-water is as clear as crystal and tastes like nectar. From it grows a garden of golden lotuses. All around are islands studded with jewels where beautiful trees, plants, fruits, and flowers grow.”

The heron then asked, “But are there big snails there?”

“No,” the swan answered.

Smirking, the herons exchanged glances. Their spokesman told the swan with haughty finality, “If it has no snails, it can hardly be called a lake. From what you say it is not a suitable place for connoisseurs like us. Thank you, swan, for all you’ve told us, but don’t expect us to follow you back home!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have come to transform herons into swans. The spiritual home of the *paramahaṁsas* is revealed in your books. If we catch up your taste for serving Kṛṣṇa, we’ll be able to enter your home and serve you and Kṛṣṇa eternally. But to do that, we’ll have to become like swans and lose our taste for snails—anything, whether material or spiritual, of which you do not approve.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray in the dust of your lotus feet that I may come to the standard of pure devotional service approved by you, even if it takes me millions of years to attain it.

Your servant,  
Suhotra Swami

## Tamāl Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

The celebration of your appearance day provides all of your followers an opportunity to appreciate how they have been most affected by your mercy in the past year. For myself, the most important manifestation of your mercy has been to make me realize that when you offered me the shelter of your lotus feet, you were actually offering me the shelter of all the members of our disciplic line. This was always clear in theory, as the principle of disciplic succession is described by Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself in the beginning of the fourth chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā*. A bona fide spiritual master acts as a transparent via media delivering to the disciple what the *guru* himself has received from his own spiritual master. And conversely, whatever a disciple offers to the spiritual master is offered by the spiritual master to his own *guru* in turn. Each of the spiritual masters in the disciplic line is a vital link connecting the devotee with the Absolute Truth, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Theoretically it’s clear. You have arranged for the mercy of the previous *ācāryas* to be showered upon all of us, your followers. Unfortunately, I never fully grasped this concept. Like an infant, I reposed all my affection in you alone. Perhaps there was nothing wrong in this. A young child cannot be blamed for his ignorance. But a father certainly expects his grown-up son to know and reciprocate with all of his family members. During this past year I have noticed how much you have helped me to overcome my childishness by making me increasingly aware of our other family members. Your mercy has allowed me to finally begin to appreciate the exalted qualities of the members of our disciplic succession. But in doing so I have taken care not to become confused about my relationship with them: I always remind myself that it is based entirely upon my relationship with you. I have carefully studied all of your books many times, read your letters, and listened to all of your lectures, and I continue to do so. Now that I am also studying the works of the previous *ācāryas,* I always try to understand them in the light of what I have learned from you.

Your appearance day is the occasion for us to offer Vyāsa-pūjā. We worship you not only because you fully represent Śrīla Vyāsadeva, but also because you represent all the previous *ācāryas*. You have dedicated yourself eternally to their service. Therefore, if I speak a few words praising them, I know this will please you. As a father is pleased with the first faltering attempts of his child, kindly overlook what is bound to be imperfect. There is no way to sufficiently glorify such exalted family members, especially with such brief praise. As the sun is worshiped by the flames of a lamp, as Mother Ganges is worshiped with a palmful of her water, so I pray that all the spiritual masters in our disciplic succession will accept this humble offering. As each is unlimitedly accomplished and qualified, I will simply mention the particular attributes you helped me to most appreciate this past year.

I offer my respectful obeisances unto my *parama-gurudeva,* Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. By revealing the hidden truths of *Śrī Brahma-saṁhitā* through his English commentary, he has made it available to devotees throughout the world. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī taught through his writings, and also demonstrated personally, the ideal life of a devotee. He worshiped the transcendental *dhāmas* of Śrī Navadvīpa and Śrī Vrajamandala by establishing annual circumambulations of each based on the research and revelations of his father, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura.

Śrīla Gaura Kiśora dāsa Bābājī Mahārāja demonstrated that renunciation need not be dry or harsh if one is always absorbed in remembering the sweet pastimes of the divine couple Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Furthermore, he achieved the pinnacle of preaching success. Normally one preaches to numerous persons, very few of whom are sincere. But Śrīla Gaura Kiśora dāsa Bābājī Mahārāja reserved all of his instructions for a single empowered disciple, who could distribute that knowledge throughout the world. May he bless me with the spirit of renunciation, sweetened by the remembrance of Rādhā’s and Kṛṣṇa’s youthful pastimes.

I offer innumerable obeisances unto the lotus feet of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, the “seventh *gosvāmī*.” Lord Caitanya not only personally revealed Himself to Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, but He also revealed the exact location of His birthplace and pastime places as well. By transcendental vision, he beheld the pilgrimage of Śrī Navadvīpa *dhāma* by Nityānanda Prabhu and Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī, relating their divine conversation in *Śrī Navadvīpa Dhāma Parikrama.* Similarly, he heard the conversation between Lord Caitanya and Ṭhākura Haridāsa concerning the glories of the holy names of the Lord and recorded it in *Śrī Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi*. He wrote and commented upon numerous Vaiṣṇava literatures, which in themselves form an entire library on the science of *bhakti*. He also composed hundreds of songs in which he takes the role of a conditioned soul and humbly expresses his unworthiness yet reveals that he was blessed with the purest love for Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura accepted the best of the Vaiṣṇavas, Śrīla Jagannātha dāsa Bābājī, as his instructing spiritual master. Bābājī Mahārāja confirmed the accuracy of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda’s discovery of Lord Caitanya’s birthsite. Confirmation by a Vaiṣṇava of his magnitude reinforced the already indefatigable scriptural evidence assembled by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda to authenticate the discovery. Whenever anyone questions the location of Mahāprabhu’s birthplace, all doubts are at once dispelled by recalling how Jagannātha dāsa Bābājī, though physically invalid at 125 years of age, leaped high into the air when brought to that sacred spot. May I always be purified by this remembrance.

When the philosophical authority of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas was challenged, Śrīla Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa prayed to Śrī Govindadeva. The Deity guided him to write a commentary on the *Vedānta-sūtra* within a matter of a few hours. He named his commentary *Śrī Govinda-bhāṣya,* giving credit to Śrīla Govindadeva, who was dictating the proper conclusions into his heart. Śrīla Prabhupāda dedicated the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* to Śrīla Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa, whose commentary perfectly establishes the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava philosophy. May he bless me to always reach the proper conclusions in all my spiritual inquiries.

I pray for the eternal shelter of Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura’s lotus feet. May the dust of his feet be my foodstuff, and may his instructions water my dry, desertlike heart. He is the devoted follower of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī and has churned the mellows of *mādhurya-rasa* for the *rasika-bhaktas* to relish. His books illuminate the path of *vraja-bhakti. Mādhurya-kadambinī* describes the progressive stages of *uttama-bhakti* from *śraddhā* to *prema. Rāga-vartma-candrikā* provides guidance for those who desire spontaneous devotion. *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu-bindu, Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi-kīrtana,* and *Bhagavatāmṛta-kanikā* are like drops of nectar from the ocean of literature they summarize. In *Saṅkalpa-kalpadruma* he reveals the intimate service rendered by the *mañjarīs* to their worshipable Deity, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. Four of Śrī Rādhikā’s most astonishing pastimes with Lord Mādhava have been related in *Camatkāra-candrikā.* In fact, Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura has glorified all of the divine couple’s pastimes throughout the eight periods of day and night in *Kṛṣṇa-bhāvanāmṛta.* His *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* Tenth Canto commentary mercifully reveals all the jewellike truths which Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī carefully hid.

I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, who is a repository of Lord Gaurāṅga’s *prema.* If one is fortunate to hear or sing his *Prārthana,* one will surely attain attraction for *yugala-kiśora,* Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Because he is always situated at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurāṅga, one who achieves his mercy can mitigate all material miseries. I pray that these sweet songs always vibrate on my tongue and within my ears. Only then will I be able to give up the countless material desires which burn within my heart.

Among the poets of this world, none can ever surpass Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja. He composed *Śrī Govinda-līlāmṛta* and *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* for the pleasure of Lord Caitanya’s associates. These two works are in themselves sufficient to nourish all the followers of Lord Caitanya, past, present, and future. May I become such a follower.

I bow down before Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī, the foremost of all philosophers. Studying his *Ṣaṭ-sandarbhas* makes one’s faith in Kṛṣṇa invincible. All that Lord Caitanya taught to Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī and Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī has been systematically described in the *Sandarbhas.* Even mundane scholars praise Śrī Jīva for this contribution and marvel at his brilliant exposition of the *acintya-bhedābheda-tattva* philosophy. May my thoughts, words, and deeds be ever fortified by the strong arguments of Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī. And may we one day be blessed with an English translation of his *Gopāla-campu.*

May Śrīla Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī consider me his dependent. He is the principal Rūpānuga Vaiṣṇava, the eternal friend and follower of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī. He personally witnessed the most intimate pastimes of Lord Caitanya under the care and guidance of Śrīla Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī. Lord Caitanya gave him Govardhana as his residence and the service of Śrīmatī Rādhikā as his only engagement. His *sādhana* is the standard for all Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas. As he lay weeping on the banks of Rādhā-kuṇḍa, his heart breaking in separation from Śrī Rādhikā, his lamentations (*vilāpa*) poured forth in the form of a handful of lowers (*kusumāñjalī*). This *Vilāpa-kusumāñjalī* is the central jewel on the necklace of prayers (*stavāvalī*), which always adorn him. I shall keep this jewel as my most valuable treasure and await the time when I may reap its riches.

Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī is the dearmost servitor of Lord Caitanya. In Rādhā’s and Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes, he is Śrī Rūpa Mañjarī, the closest maidservant of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. Rādhārāṇī hides nothing from her because she has totally identified herself with the innermost desires of Śrī Rādhikā. Along with her dearmost friend Rati-mañjarī (Śrī Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī), the two render intimate, menial services to Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. What Rādhikā will hesitate to ask Lalitā or Viśākhā to do, she will demand of Rūpa or Rati. These *mañjarīs* are her very own, and they have no interest other than Her service.

When Lord Kṛṣṇa came as Lord Caitanya to taste the emotions of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, Śrī Rūpa-mañjarī descended as Śrī Rūpa Gosvāmī to broadcast the most confidential teachings of Lord Caitanya. Lord Caitanya tasted all of the *rasas,* especially the *madhu-rasa.* Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī is the *rasa-ācārya,* and his books like the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* and *Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi* are the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava textbooks on the subject of *rasa.* His dramas *Lalita-mādhava* and *Vidagdha-mādhava* bring to life the pastimes of *rasarāja* Śrī Kṛṣṇa for all those who hear them. His poetry and all his other literary works are the standards which guide the lives of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas. I pray that I may one day be identified as a genuine *rūpānuga,* for I cannot think of any other acceptable designation.

I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī, the elder brother and spiritual master of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī. The eldest of the six Gosvāmīs, he was a fatherly figure for all the residents of Vraja. His *Bṛhad-bhāgavatāmṛta* provided the inspiration for all the literatures produced later by the Gosvāmīs. His *Hari-bhakti-vilāsa* established the standards of Vaiṣṇava etiquette and worship.

No one can fully fathom the exalted position of Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu personally stated that Sanātana Gosvāmī’s body was His principal instrument for executing many of the important functions of His mission. Apart from the important books he wrote, Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī excavated all the lost places of pilgrimage in Vṛndāvana. He taught devotees how to perform devotional service in Vṛndāvana, demonstrating perfectly the behavior of one in the renounced order of life. Because he established the Deity of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana-mohana, it is by his mercy that we can establish our relationship with Kṛṣṇa. He is the ideal spiritual master of all the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas, providing them an opportunity to live in Vṛndāvana and thus make all spiritual advancement. I therefore fall prostrate at the lotus feet of Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī, praying that he will somehow make me a worthy object of his mercy.

Śrīmatī Lalitā-devī, the most important friend of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, appeared in this world as Śrīla Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī. Just as Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī would do everything by first consulting Lalitā-sakhī, so Lord Caitanya depended upon his secretary Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī in all respects. Lalitā-devī takes charge of all the unlimited number of *gopīs* who serve in the pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Similarly, anyone wishing to do service to Caitanya Mahāprabhu had to first be approved by Svarūpa Dāmodara. Lord Caitanya came to experience the highest limits of love in separation. The association of Svarūpa Dāmodara and Rāmānanda Rāya helped the Lord survive the pain of such separation. Whatever we know of Lord Caitanya’s pastimes in Purī is based on the diary kept by Śrīla Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī, which Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī expanded upon.

Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu appeared as the twenty-second spiritual master in our *paramparā.* Although He is the original Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself, He has chosen to appear in the Age of Kali as a devotee. We can know the confidential reasons for His descent by hearing from His closest associates. Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī prays,

*anarpita-carīṁ cirāt karuṇāyavatīrṇaḥ kalau*  
*samarpayitum unnatojjvala-rasāṁ sva-bhakti-śriyam*  
*hariḥ puraṭa-sundara-dyuti-kadamba-sandīpitaḥ*  
*sadā hṛdaya-kandare sphuratu vaḥ śacī-nandanaḥ*

**“May that Lord who is known as the son of Śrīmatī Śacī-devī be transcendentally situated in the innermost chambers of your heart. Resplendent with the radiance of molten gold, He has appeared in the Age of Kali by His causeless mercy to bestow what no incarnation ever offered before: the most elevated mellow of devotional service, the mellow of conjugal love.”**

Śrīla Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī has revealed the identity of Lord Caitanya, and by his grace we can understand the most confidential causes for His appearance. The following is from his diary:

*rādhā-kṛṣṇa-praṇaya-vikṛtir hlādinī śaktir asmād*  
*ekātmānāv api bhuvi purā deha-bhedaṁ gatau tau*  
*caitanyākhyaṁ prakaṭam adhunā tad-dvayaṁ caikyam āptaṁ*  
*rādhā-bhāva-dyuti-suvalitaṁ naumi kṛṣṇa-svarūpam*

**“The loving affairs of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa are transcendental manifestations of the Lord’s internal pleasure-giving potency. Although Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa are one in Their identity, They separated Themselves eternally. Now these two transcendental identities have again united in the form of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya. I bow down to Him, who has manifested Himself with the sentiment and complexion of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, although He is Kṛṣṇa Himself.”**

*śrī-rādhāyāḥ praṇaya-mahimā kīdṛśo vānayaivā-*  
*svādyo yenādbhuta-madhurimā kīdṛśo vā madīyaḥ*  
*saukhyaṁ cāsyā mad-anubhavataḥ kīdṛśaṁ veti lobhāt*  
*tad-bhāvāḍhyaḥ samajani śacī-garbha-sindhau harīnduḥ*

**“Desiring to understand the glory of Rādhārāṇī’s love, the wonderful qualities in Him that She alone relishes through Her love, and the happiness She feels when She realizes the sweetness of His love, the Supreme Lord Hari, richly endowed with Her emotions, appeared from the womb of Śrīmatī Śacī-devī, as the moon appeared from the ocean.”**

May Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu give me shelter.

Preceding Lord Caitanya in the line of disciplic succession is Lord Nityānanda Prabhu. Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura has sung,

*vrajendra-nandana jei, śacī-suta hoilo sei*  
*balarāma hoilo nitāi*

**“Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Prince of Vraja, has appeared as Lord Caitanya, the son of Śacī-devī, and Lord Balarāma has appeared as Lord Nityānanda.”**

Lord Nityānanda is thus the Supreme Personality of Godhead, as Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī explains:

*saṅkarṣaṇaḥ kāraṇa-toya-śāyī*  
*garbhoda-śāyī ca payobdhi-śāyī*  
*śeṣaś ca yasyāṁśa-kalāḥ sa nityā-*  
*nandākhya-rāmaḥ śaraṇaṁ mamāstu*

**“May Śrī Nityānanda Rāma be the object of my constant remembrance. Saṅkarṣaṇa, Śeṣa Nāga, and the Viṣṇus who lie on the Kāraṇa Ocean, Garbha Ocean, and ocean of milk are his plenary portions and the portions of His plenary portions.”**

One cannot approach Lord Caitanya unless one has a relationship with Lord Nityānanda. I therefore pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and to all of the other spiritual masters since the time of Lord Caitanya, that they bestow upon me their causeless mercy. Then only will I be able to worship the lotus feet of Śrī Nityānanda Rāma.

Advaita Ācārya is an incarnation of Mahā-Viṣṇu as a devotee. Before Lord Caitanya’s descent, He sent His respectable predecessors such as Śrī Advaita Ācārya Prabhu. Finding the world devoid of pure devotion, He fervently appealed to the Lord to come. The loud calling of Advaita Ācārya made it possible for Kṛṣṇa Himself to appear and spread the *yuga-dharma* of *nāma-saṅkīrtana.* I offer my respectful obeisances to the lotus feet of Śrī Advaita Ācārya Prabhu thousands of millions of times. Being full of compassion for the fallen conditioned souls, may He consider me, who am most fallen, a worthy object of His mercy.

Lord Caitanya is known as Mahāprabhu, while Lord Nityānanda and Lord Advaita are known as Prabhus. The two Prabhus accepted Śrī Īśvara Purī as their Godbrother due to his being so dear to their spiritual master, Śrī Mādhavendra Purī. Śrī Īśvara Purī’s fame is twofold. As a perfect disciple he completely engaged himself in the service of Śrī Mādhavendra Purī, who had become invalid and unable to move. Thus he personally cleaned up his stool and urine. For rendering the best service of all his disciples, Īśvara Purī was blessed by Mādhavendra Purī to become a great devotee. The blessings of the spiritual master are so powerful that they qualified Īśvara Purī to become the spiritual master of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Thus Īśvara Purī was equally famous as a disciple and as a spiritual master. May he be merciful to me.

Śrī Mādhavendra Purī belonged to the disciplic succession from Śrī Madhvācārya. Until his appearance, the line of Madhvācārya followed a system of worship based upon ritualistic ceremonies. Mādhavendra Purī was the first to exhibit ecstatic devotional service in conjugal love. Thus he composed a verse which is considered the most famous of all poems. It was spoken by Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī Herself, expressing Her feeling of intense separation after Kṛṣṇa left Vṛndāvana for Mathurā. Because Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu appeared in order to fully experience love in separation, Mādhavendra Purī is credited as the first in whom the seed of such devotional love fructified. It is said that only Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, Mādhavendra Purī, and Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu are capable of fully understanding the purport of the famous verse composed by Mādhavendra Purī. It is as follows:

*ayi dīna-dayārdra nātha he*  
*mathurā-nātha kadāvalokyase*  
*hṛdayaṁ tvad-aloka-kātaraṁ*  
*dayitā bhrāmyati kiṁ karomy aham*

**“O My Lord! O most merciful master! O master of Mathurā! When shall I see You again? Because of My not seeing You, My agitated heart has become unsteady. O most beloved one, what shall I do now?”**

May Śrī Mādhavendra Purī bless me in some future birth with some slight understanding of this verse’s meaning.

I bow down to the lotus feet of Śrī Madhvācārya, as well as all his exalted *sannyāsī* descendants. By their mercy, may I always be protected from the influence of Māyāvāda philosophy, which Lord Caitanya considered the greatest enemy of *bhakti.* May they also enable me to have unflinching faith in the service of the Deity.

I bow down to Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the literary incarnation of Godhead, who divided the original *Veda* into four. Thus he edited the *Vedas* to make them easily understandable for the general populace of this age. May he bless me with the understanding of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, the cream of all Vedic literatures. Freed from the vicious modes of passion and ignorance, I will then engage in genuine devotional service to Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga.

O Nārada Muni! As the Lord’s eternal associate, you are like the Lord Himself. As the Lord is able to expand Himself in innumerable incarnations, so you also do so to take part in His pastimes. To observe Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mystic potencies by which He married and lived with more than 16,000 queens in Dvārakā, you exhibited your own mystic potency and visited the 16,000 palaces of the Lord simultaneously. Such feats are not astonishing for one who is himself an empowered incarnation of Godhead. Empowered with the Lord’s *bhakti-śakti,* you can bestow pure devotional service on anyone. Thus you delivered a lowly hunter named Mṛgāri. You also have the most exalted disciples, such as Prahlāda Mahārāja, Dhruva Mahārāja, Yudhiṣṭhira Mahārāja, and Śrīla Vyāsadeva, to name a few. I know that I am not worthy of your attention. But I pray that by associating with those who are, your merciful glance will inadvertently fall upon me. In this way I will be able to easily advance on the path of pure devotion. Left to my own efforts, I am helpless. Therefore I am depending on the mercy of those who have received your mercy.

To Lord Brahmā, the first living entity born in and the father of this universe, I offer my most respectful obeisances. You are famous for your penances, for your intelligence, and for your devotion. After testing Kṛṣṇa’s powers by stealing His calves and cowherd boys, you realized your fault. Afterwards you prayed that when the Lord appeared again, you would be able to participate in His pastimes in a more favorable role. Knowing your sincerity, Lord Caitanya blessed you to be free from all pride. He arranged for you to take birth in a Mohammedan family as the most exalted of His devotees, Śrīla Haridāsa Ṭhākura.

You are the founder-*ācārya* of our *sampradāya,* the Brahma-mādhva-gauḍīya-sampradāya. Although I am most unworthy, nevertheless I pray that you allow me to consider myself a menial servant of your party. I will be very pleased to perform any service, no matter what that service may be. Please accept this request, for I do not want any other engagement in life or after death.

Having offered my obeisances unto all of the *ācāryas* in the disciplic succession, I approach their ultimate shelter, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. O Lord, each of Your devotees lovingly addresses You by a name suitable to their loving relationship with You. Thus You are Rasika-śekhara, the best of all the relishers of transcendental mellows. In the introduction to his *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu,* Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī has thus glorified You:

*akhila-rasāmṛta-mūrtiḥ*  
*prasṛmara-ruci-ruddha-tārakā-pāliḥ*  
*kalita-śyāmā-lalito*  
*rādhā-preyān vidhur jayati*

**“Let Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, be glorified! By virtue of His expanding attractive features, He subjugated the** ***gopīs*** **named Tārakā and Pālī and absorbed the minds of Śyāmā and Lalitā. He is the most attractive lover of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and is the reservoir of pleasure for all devotional mellows.”**

O Gopīnātha, please give me shelter.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, my glorification of our family members in disciplic succession is now complete. Having sung their praise and begged for their mercy, I return to that one who has shown me the greatest mercy of all. That is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I know that no one can ever give me shelter more than you have. In the form of your books, in the form of your movement, in the form of your devotees, and in the form of your personal association and service you have already bestowed far more blessings upon me than I shall ever be worthy of. But I see that you are eager to give me still more by introducing me to our venerable family members. Were it not for my relationship with you, they would certainly give me no heed. But because my father is His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, they cannot reject me. Because you are so dear to them, and because I have somehow become dear to you, they may be merciful to me. Whatever mercy they may bestow upon me, I will always know it is due to you.

Eternally fixed in your service,  
Tamāl Krishna Goswami

## Vīrabāhu dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**An Evening Lecture on Vyāsa-pūjā Day**

*tā-sabā tārite prabhu sei saba deśe*  
*yogya-bhakta jīva-dehe karena ‘āveśe’*  
*sei jīve nija-bhakti karena prakāśe*  
*tāhāra darśane ‘vaiṣṇava’ haya sarva-deśe*

**“To deliver people in regions throughout the universe who could not meet Him, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu personally entered the bodies of pure devotees. Thus He empowered living beings \[His pure devotees\] by manifesting in them so much of His own devotion that people in all other countries became devotees by seeing them.”**

“Unless one is empowered by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, one cannot spread the holy names of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* throughout the world. Persons who do so are empowered. Therefore they are sometimes called *āveśa-avatarās,* or incarnations, for they are endowed with the power of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu” (Cc. *Antya* 2.13-14).

It has been a very nice day today celebrating Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance. The occasion is always very intense due to the proximity of Janmāṣṭamī. Yesterday was Janmāṣṭamī, which is a very intense day, and then today it’s Prabhupāda’s appearance day, which makes things still more intense.

Today we heard so many nice things about Śrīla Prabhupāda, being a day that we devote in particular to meditating on him. Śrīla Prabhupāda is so important in our lives. There are many, many *ācāryas* in our disciplic succession, and we have come to learn about them. But this one particular *ācārya,* Śrīla Prabhupāda, is the most important to us because thanks to him we have come to know of all other *ācāryas*. Not only is he important to us because of this, but his importance is magnified in our lives due to how he has transformed our lives for us by connect ing with the previous *ācāryas.*

We may find out about so many people of the past from our history professor, our history teacher. World history. We also know of people of the past by means of a teacher. But this is not the kind of relationship we have with our teacher, Śrīla Prabhupāda, or with the previous *ācāryas*. It is not that kind of relationship. Because of Śrīla Prabhupāda our relationship with the previous *ācāryas* is completely different. That is something for us to consider—that the importance of Śrīla Prabhupāda in our lives is not just that he has presented to us this philosophy and the previous *ācāryas*. For example, there may also be professors in the university presenting Kṛṣṇa consciousness to their students in so many ways. Probably some of these professors may be telling their students what Kṛṣṇa consciousness is all about. But that information is not the same kind of information that we have received from Śrīla Prabhupāda. We should see this difference. There are so many aspects concerning Śrīla Prabhupāda’s personality and his relationship with us; that is one of them. Therefore today, a day that we are devoting especially to concentrating and meditating on Śrīla Prabhupāda, this is a thought that we may have in mind.

Every year, actually every day, we have this meditation on Śrīla Prabhupāda, because he is so important that every day we are forced to think of him. Of course “we are forced” is a way to speak, meaning that because Śrīla Prabhupāda is all-pervading in this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, in the ISKCON society, then it is a must. There is no way we can escape from thinking about Śrīla Prabhupāda every single day. But in particular on the day of his appearance we focus more on analyzing the different aspects of our relationship with him. For so many years we have been doing this, and by Kṛṣṇa’s grace and by his grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s grace, we will continue for many, many years and probably for many, many lifetimes analyzing his gemlike qualities.

So this year, at this precise moment we can now think about this fact, the importance of Śrīla Prabhupāda in our lives in reference to how he has presented to us this culture and all the teachers, the disciplic teachers, the spiritual masters or *ācāryas* in the line. He has presented everything in such a way that our lives became transformed and we have connected with all this culture in a very strong, powerful way. And we ourselves are able to present this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and transmit the impression we have received from Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is such a strong impression that anybody who connects or has some contact with a devotee of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s immediately becomes also touched by the same kind of energy. We are therefore very grateful.

It is a day to be most thankful to Kṛṣṇa because it is by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa that one gets the spiritual master. It is by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa that we have received Śrīla Prabhupāda. Kṛṣṇa has arranged for this lucky encounter. This kind of encounter makes the living entity lucky, fortunate, as it is explained in *śāstra:*

*ei rūpe brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja.*

This means that the living entity, *jīva,* is generally wandering all over the universe life after life. Sometimes being born here, sometimes being born there. Sometimes as a human being, sometimes as an insect, sometimes as a demigod, sometimes as a worm. *Brahmāṇḍa* means “universe”, “the universes”, “the creation.” So the living entity has been wandering. Just like we have the experience that, before coming to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, even within our small lifespan we have already wandered through so many things, jumping from one thing to another. In that way we can imagine also our jumping activities before this lifetime. How we have been going from one thing to another, from one place to another.

*Guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde.* When someone gets the mercy of *guru* and Kṛṣṇa (which we have evidently received—otherwise we wouldn’t be in this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement), then that makes that living entity *bhāgyavān*. *Bhāgyavān* means someone who possesses *bhāgya,* and that is fortune, good luck.

So we are very fortunate, and this good fortune has come from Śrīla Prabhupāda. He has made us fortunate. He has taken the care to convince us about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is a great feat because it is generally a very discouraging mission. A very discouraging one. Therefore we should appreciate very much what he has done for us. We should be very careful to appreciate it and not write it off by saying that he was a pure devotee. We should not take for granted all that he did: “Oh, well, he was a pure devotee.” Because pure devotees sometimes also become very disgusted. Prabhupāda had every reason, probably as many reasons as disciples, to become disgusted. But still he didn’t. I’m saying this with all due respect to the many exalted souls in our society. I’m speaking in general. Now that we have a little more of an understanding by Prabhupāda’s grace, by his books—mostly by his instructions coming in the form of his books, listening to his tapes, and like that—we have now a clearer understanding of the distance between a pure devotee and all of us.

Now we understand that a little bit clearer. It is just like, for instance, one’s relationship with a child. How long can you be involved with a child? Unless, of course, we are speaking of your own child, and still it is a bothersome activity to engage in. When someone is in a higher stage of consciousness, it is not very easy to deal with a lower stage of consciousness. It becomes bothersome. Of course, it is very encouraging when the person in the higher stage of consciousness sees the progress of the one in the lower stage, when he sees the effect of the teachings, the result of his effort. In this case it is very encouraging for a person in the higher stage of consciousness to continue his or her relationship with those in the lower stages. But when those in the lower stage of consciousness insist on remaining there, then it becomes a very discouraging enterprise.

Therefore Śrīla Prabhupāda was very kind. Extremely kind. He had the patience to go all the way and never became discouraged. Actually, by reading the biography of Śrīla Prabhupāda and also other books about his pastimes in the West, we can see that he had every reason to become disgusted, but still he never did so—to our great fortune. To the contrary, in his very last days he was expressing his love for his disciples and was seeing everything from the transcendental platform, beholding them as Vaiṣṇava association. In his ecstatic mood of departure, in the very intimate and personal feelings that he was experiencing in those last days, he was describing his relationship with his disciples as Vaiṣṇava association.

In one letter Śrīla Prabhupāda made a statement that is still perplexing to me in many ways. He expressed that when his own spiritual master saw that he was only making neophyte disciples he became disgusted and decided to leave this world untimely. That is a very perplexing statement, and this is not the moment to analyze it thoroughly. But briefly, at least we can understand from it that Śrīla Prabhupāda never expressed such a thing regarding his own disciples. That was not the circumstance with Śrīla Prabhupāda, because we don’t have any indication that he left out of disgust. That is not there. There are so many tapes in which we can even hear him directly speaking in his last days, and there is no indication of this sort of feeling. That is his kindness.

This reflection is not in any way to compare Prabhupāda’s disciples to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s disciples. We are not saying it’s because Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disciples are more advanced than the other disciples, and so on. This is not the statement we can make. But actually what we can say is that Prabhupāda was extremely kind.

On this day we should especially appreciate that kindness. And the way to do this is to make a very strong commitment, a very big effort on our part, to be worthy. To become worthy of this kindness—this is very important. We are to make an effort, and that means that we should make an effort not to become victims of *māyā*.

We should actually make Prabhupāda powerful by making his followers powerful. Of course, to be powerful Prabhupāda does not need us. But the meaning is this: Where is the proof of his power? That’s another way to see it. The proof of his power is the great thing that he has made—ISKCON and his followers. In this manner we should understand that we have a big responsibility on our shoulders, in glorification of Prabhupāda. If his followers are weaklings, then what is going to be his position—what are people going to think about who he really was or how powerful he was?

So we have a big responsibility, and on a day like today we should meditate and invoke his further blessings. On the one hand we should be thankful, but we have to be thankful not in a sentimental way but in a very practical way. Let’s do something for Prabhupāda. That is the idea. We must do something for him, and the thing that is most needed for us to do is that we live in the manner he was expecting his followers to live. That is his success. This would really be for the amazement of all the people—that Prabhupāda’s followers are so committed, so convinced, so determined. In this way *māyā* will be kicked away.

We should meditate on these points—our responsibility to Śrīla Prabhupāda—and request his blessings and the blessings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Nityānanda Prabhu, who have brought the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement to this world and whose mission Śrīla Prabhupāda was representing. Just like we have that poem by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura we recite every day before taking *prasādam*—*preme dāko caitanya-nitāi*. We are invoking the help of Śrī Caitanya and Nityānanda for eating and controlling our senses. And we should also request the help of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi so that we become the worthy representatives of Śrīla Prabhupāda. The worthy sons and daughters, grandsons and granddaughters, of His Divine Grace.

Just like sometimes we may be embarrassed to have a particular parent or a particular relative; sometimes we may be embarrassed that we have a particular son or daughter. The saying is there, “black sheep.” But, please, let’s not be like this. We should be the worthy representatives of Śrīla Prabhupāda so that he may be proud of his followers, and not that the people see his followers and start scratching their heads about what this is all about—Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We should be the worthy representatives so that people may admire Śrīla Prabhupāda. This is a big responsibility because now people are or are not going to admire admire Śrīla Prabhupāda by his followers. Of course, his books are there—no question about it. We all acknowledge that the people will appreciate his contribution to the world also because of his books. His literary contribution. But a most important part is his movement and his followers. Most important, because that makes the difference from everybody else—between Śrīla Prabhupāda and all other creators of movements. The difference is how well the followers can represent his ideas. Prabhupāda had certain ideals, and his success will be seen in how well his followers can live those ideals. This is very important.

So today we should meditate in that way and make this a successful item in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission—that his followers became fully convinced and become in actuality the ideal persons that he was hoping them to be. This should be our constant gift to him: to fulfill the vows and promises we have made. May every Vyāsa-pūjā day be the reminder and the witness of that commitment. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

His servants’ servant,  
Vīrabāhu dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances to the dust of your lotus feet, which are authentic lifesavers for millions of living beings who, like myself, have shipwrecked in the dangerous ocean of this material world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have not only shown us the way that leads to the spiritual world, but you have also taught us how to walk toward it.

With the distribution of your books, your mercy has reached and is still reaching to all the corners of the planet. Three hundred million people have already bought your books, and at least twice that number have read them; but for each person that has come in touch with your books or with your devotees, hundreds and thousands of people daily receive your message about Kṛṣṇa. It is like your clever system of cooking with one kettle on top of the other, in which with the heat of the first kettle, which is directly over the fire, whatever is in the other kettles is also being cooked.

You organized this movement in such a way that it is impossible to stop the expansion of the teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and of the *Vedas*. How much wisdom and how much power there are in your words! How much feeling and how much determination to please Kṛṣṇa are there in your transcendental instructions!

You have opened the path of order and hope in the midst of chaos and frustration. You have softened the hardened hearts and captivated the most sensitive.

You have invaded the enemy ground and destroyed their fortress walls. For all who trust in your teachings, their confinement in this material world has come to an end. For your devotees, the boundaries created by intellect, which command the human being to live in the mental plane, have ceased to exist.

All your disciples are like atoms of your inconceivable spiritual power. Your disciples are also powerful when they are guided by your wise inspiration. But unfortunately not all of us have the quality of faithfulness to follow you, or the intelligence to understand you, or even the spirit to serve you as you deserve.

I am one of those rebellious and irresponsible disciples, and among all, the most incompetent and ungrateful. Forgive me. I hope you will accept my repentance as a precursor to the reform of my offensive and whimsical behavior. Actually, I feel that I am not worthy of you, that I don’t deserve your help, because I do not appreciate your mercy enough. Nevertheless, despite all my faults, I understand that there is nothing better that I can do than serve and glorify you continuously; this is what will give value and utility to my activities. How much gratitude and surrender to your mission will I have to prove to pay just something of my debt to you? How many lifetimes will I have to spend tolerating the unbearable to reciprocate the least of the favors received from you? As many lifetimes as it may be, they will never be enough, but I will be ever satisfied being engaged in your divine service.

The relationship between the spiritual master and the disciple is based upon service. The spiritual master teaches the fundamental values of spiritual life to the disciple, and the disciple must be very attentive to assimilate them quickly for his own benefit and for the benefit of all the conditioned souls he may help by delivering the same learning.

The master, who is full of wisdom received in disciplic succession, has an unlimited wealth of mercy that we can benefit from. It is something as simple and sublime as a cow’s relationship with her calf. The calf has to be an expert in sucking its mother’s milk; otherwise he could get frustrated and become angry at her mother. The calf has to learn how to suck nicely to receive the benefit of the milk. In the same way, the disciple has to learn to extract the nectar of wisdom from his spiritual master so that the disciple will be spiritually nourished and feel satisfied. And just as the calf has to look for her mother to obtain its daily amount of food, likewise the disciple has to search desperately for the master, aware of his position and that of the master. If, like inexpert calves, we don’t learn the right way to extract the nectar from our spiritual master, we expose ourselves to receive kicks for our clumsiness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am one of those clumsy calves who only deserves kicks from you. In the behavior of an *ācārya* there are no activities apart from the transcendental loving service to the Lord. I humbly implore your forgiveness for the many offenses I committed and am still committing to your person—by both commission and omission—by not valuing the matchless gifts received from you out of your causeless generosity. Out of genuine love you have blessed so many conditioned souls who, like myself, are suffering unredeemed in this material world.

You have said, “To honor the spiritual master means to carry out his instructions word for word” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.24.12, purport). With this sentence you not only define the obligation of the disciple but also warn us about sentimentalism. On the other hand, I understand that if I do not honor you in the right way, by fulfilling your divine instructions, I am going to become a black stain on the white sheet of services you offer to your spiritual master.

I pray that my goal will be to become your slave. That my thoughts will be pure, honest, and wise. That my words won’t consist of anything but praises to your holy movement. That my deeds will match your noble ideas of rescuing forever those who are drowning. That I will not lack intelligence to understand the glory of your sacred mission and the way of dovetailing myself to it. That I will not lack strength to fulfill your orders and preach your message.

Let me see trials as favors, and let me, from this moment, give up my ingratitude so that wherever I may be I will always fulfill your command.

Your eternal servant,  
Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

**Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Reveal to me  
The enticing form  
Of illusion’s calm  
Before she’s a storm.

Give me the strength  
To withstand the power  
Of material desires  
That submerge by the hour.

When dissensions arise,  
Provide me with fuel  
To cope with each matter  
And keep my head cool.

Offer me light  
And dismiss the dark.  
Make my path clear,  
Leave your foot mark.

I slip and I fall—  
Outstretch your cane,  
For I do not long  
For rebirth again.

Do not reject me,  
Keep me afloat.  
With mental balance,  
I’ll remain on your boat.

I exist upon ego—  
Do keep it at bay,  
Through the power of purport  
And all that you say.

Māyā is big.  
I am so small.  
You make her shrink  
In the presence of all.

Give me your mercy  
And more and more.  
How much do you give?  
How much do you store?

Protect me from wrong.  
Set me on track.  
Keep me on course  
Until I am back.

Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate giriṁ*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

On Vyāsa-pūjā Day one should endeavor to glorify the spiritual master, who is the direct representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the literary incarnation of Godhead. It is the duty of every disciple to constantly worship and glorify his spiritual master, but especially on the appearance day of his spiritual master the disciple must glorify him in a very special way.

Although I am most unqualified and unworthy, still as a menial servant of yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let me try to fulfill this grave responsibility.

*kali-kāler dharma kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*

**“The fundamental religious system in the Age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement.”**

The potency of your ISKCON movement continues to show itself even though you have not been physically present with us now for the past fifteen years. This proves that Kṛṣṇa’s mercy was with you from the very beginning and continues to be with you through the society you established twenty-five years ago. You have transmitted the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu so purely and perfectly that every day, even now, countless living entities are being attracted and joining this great *harināma-saṅkīrtana* movement. To see new devotees with young, bright-looking faces jubilantly dancing in *kīrtana* and serving their individual spiritual masters so eagerly is testimony to your genius and your expertise, and a display of the continuing potency of your movement. How can we ever repay you for this wonderful gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness?

Many of us who have now taken up the task of serving as initiating spiritual masters are feeling the heavy weight which you bore single-handedly for so many dependent souls. We are unable to compete with your achievements, nor should we ever think one day we will ever come close. We can but turn our glance toward Your Divine Grace and seek your soothing shelter as we endeavor in our small ways to expand your great society of ISKCON.

Now, as we approach your centennial year of 1996, we have the greatest opportunity to glorify you. It is a once-in-a-lifetime opportunity you are giving us. I pray that you will be kind upon us and enable these celebrations to establish you as the unequalled and unparalleled commander in chief of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

We have heard from the *śāstras* how in this Age of Kali the ninth cycle of 500-year periods will bring the *yuga-avatāra*. And so it was that around 4,500 years into the Kali-yuga, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu advented Himself. The *śāstras* also foretell that from the tenth cycle, a golden period will begin. Śrīla Prabhupāda, by traveling twelve times around the world within a short span of eleven years, by establishing more than one hundred temples, initiating some five thousand disciples, translating and distributing so many wonderful Vaiṣṇava books on the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and continuing to do this and more through your dedicated disciples and grand-disciples, you have actually ushered in this golden age. Thus you are the ambassador, the great luminary, for this new Satya-yuga, where your books will serve as lawbooks for humanity for the next ten thousand years.

I cannot fathom your glories. Surely they will one day form part of yet another canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam—*the Thirteenth Canto.

The future looks very, very bright. Slowly but surely your ISKCON society of devotees is maturing. Although you have always been the perfect master, the perfect teacher, and the perfect guide, we have not always been the perfect disciples. I can only beg for your forgiveness for my numerous offenses, blunders, and shortcomings. The path you have given, the path you have shown, is perfect. We simply need to follow it strictly, and thus we will become your perfect disciples.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, if you hadn’t come and done what you did, the whole mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu would have practically been undone. More and more devotees from other *maṭhas* are acknowledging your timely and unequalled contribution.

I pray that we, your disicples and grand-disciples, can work more closely with one another so that we can assist you even more in continuing to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, whatever good may be in us or whatever good we may have done is simply due to your mercy. You once said that you created our good fortune.

Please continue to shower this mercy and bestow your blessings upon us so that we may become your worthy children. Please give me the strength, the courage, and the wisdom to follow in your footsteps and to serve you, life after life.

Your unworthy servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated and humble obeisances at your lotus feet!

Every year my limited and small realization of your incomparable achievements increases by your grace. How you spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, writing so many books, letters, and essays, delivering countless lectures and instructions, initiating so many projects, taking such affectionate care of your disciples and followers, etc. Each year I am more amazed. Lord Kṛṣṇa was so kind to send you to this filthy pit of material existence, especially to these Western countries, where nobody else before in history dared to go. In a lecture you delivered in 1969 in Los Angeles, you said you had been sent by the Lord out of His compassion for us fallen souls. I would like to quote the whole passage of your class:

So, somehow or other it has begun, the blessings of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, as he wanted me, he desired me. So because he desired, my … I am not very expert or educated or nothing extraordinary, but only thing is that I believed in his word. That is … You can say that is my qualification. I believed cent percent in his word. So whatever success is there, it is just due to my firm faith in his instruction. So I am trying to follow. And by his grace, you are helping me. So actually, the responsibility will now depend on you. I am also old man. I may pass away at any moment. This movement should go on, you see, this movement started by Caitanya Mahāprabhu, then accelerated by Gosvāmīs, and then again Viśvanātha Cakravartī. And when … Just like whenever there is … *Yadā yadā hi dharmasya glānir bhavati*. When there is some slowness, either Kṛṣṇa Himself or some representative comes to give it a push. So you should know it, that this movement should be pushed on. \[Lecture, 1969, Los Angeles\]

That push, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is required to fight successfully the destiny of Kali’s age. That push is a symptom of love and compassion and not a symptom of passion, as some less-intelligent persons might believe. Nowadays people are flattering each other for their own motivated benefits, but that will not help anyone. The material energy will not let go of anybody, even if he is trying hard by some clever means, as long as he remains attached to it. That hard knot of attachment can only be severed by one who, out of love and compassion, pushes the attached soul to let loose.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you started your movement in that mood of love and compassion for the suffering souls, not making any compromise to cater to the self-destructive desires of the conditioned soul. Please protect and guide your devotees and this movement in such a way that we might continue to push ourselves and others in a loving and affectionate way so that we finally return home, back to Godhead. You knew perfectly that the sufferings of the living entities in this world are simply unnecessary: *anārthopaśamaṁ sākṣād bhakti-yogam adhokṣaje.* You explain in your purport to this verse in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that Vyāsadeva saw the all-perfect Personality of Godhead with His different energies and specifically observed the unwanted miseries of the conditioned souls, who are bewildered by the external energy. And at last he saw the remedial measure for the conditioned souls, namely, the process of devotional service, which begins with the process of hearing and chanting the name, fame, glory, etc., of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

There is another verse in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that explains that by giving aural reception to the Vedic literature, one at once develops the feeling for loving devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, and that this devotional service extinguishes the fire of lamentation, illusion, and fearfulness. Therefore you translated and wrote your commentaries on the Vedic literature so tirelessly. You were so happy when you received the book distribution scores from all over the world, and I am sure that you are still happy to receive them. In another class you mentioned:

We are requesting everyone: “Please chant, chant, chant.” And they are replying: “Can’t, Can’t…” This is their misfortune. This is their misfortune. So still it is our duty to make all these misfortunate, unfortunate creatures fortunate. That is our mission. We therefore go in the street and chant. Although they say “Can’t,” we go on chanting. That is our business. And somehow or other we push on some literature in his hand. He is becoming fortunate. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, 1973, Los Angeles\]

Despite so many obstacles, you showed us how to be determined in pushing on your movement. With your permission, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I would like to give some related quotations from some of your lectures:
