This secret of success, people do not know, neither they can understand. They are not fortunate enough. But it is our duty to push on this movement by superior order. Anyone who will take advantage of this movement, he’ll be saved from this repetition, birth and death. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, 1973, Stockholm\]

And within this dangerous condition of the human society we have to push on Kṛṣṇa consciousness. How difficult it is! Just imagine. But still, it is going on. But so many obstacles. So many obstacles. But we do not care for these obstacles. We must push on. This is our determination. Therefore it is called *tīvrena bhakti-yogena. Tīvrena*. There may be so many obstacles, but you must push on your movement. *Tīvrena bhakti-yogena.* That is our duty. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, 1974, Bombay\]

So very difficult task. The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, to push on, requires great patience, perseverance, tolerance. But Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s order, *tṛṇād api sunīcena taror api sahiṣṇunā / amāninā mānadena kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ …* So those who have taken to Kṛṣṇa consciousness preaching work, they should always know that things are not going to happen so easily. Māyā is very, very strong. Very, very strong. But still, we have to struggle against Māyā. It is a declaration of war with Māyā. Māyā is keeping the living entities under her control, and we are trying to save the living entity … That is the difference. *Kālo vaśī-kṛta-visṛjya-visarga-śaktiḥ.* This *śakti, visarga-śaktiḥ,* is very, very strong, but it is under the control. Although it is very, she is very, very strong, but she is under the control of Kṛṣṇa. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, 1976, Māyāpur\]

We therefore advise our disciples to strictly follow the regulative principles; otherwise the most important movement for the upliftment of humanity will be hampered due to dissension among its members. Those who are serious about pushing forward this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement should remember this and strictly follow the regulative principles so that their minds will not be disturbed. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.14.35, purport\]

Often I am thinking how to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, despite my lack of any qualification. Still by your grace there is a spark of sincerity in my heart to push on your movement. Let me therefore demonstrate my actual love for you by strictly following the regulative principles, pushing on your movement in cooperation with my Godbrothers, and trying to inspire others also in pushing on your movement.

Your eternal servant,  
Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhaktivikāśa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

This year, at Vyāsa-pūjā, I am offering this to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda: a section in a book I have compiled called *A Beginners Guide to Kṛṣṇa Consciousness.* It is called: The Importance of Śrīla Prabhupāda

The honorific title “Prabhupāda” is properly used for designating those very great spiritual masters who have made an outstanding contribution of literature and /or preaching to the world. Examples are Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Prabhupāda.

When members of ISKCON speak of “Śrīla Prabhupāda,” they refer to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is also correctly addressed as “Śrīla Prabhupāda,” for he occupies a unique position in the religious history of the world.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.5.11) Śrīla Vyāsadeva states that the *Bhāgavatam* is “meant to bring about a revolution in the impious lives of this world’s misdirected civilization.”

Learned Vaiṣṇava scholars have discerned that this statement of Vyāsadeva’s must refer to the preaching mission brought by Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. It was he only who, five thousand years after Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s compilation of the *Bhāgavatam,* wrote his Bhaktivedanta purports on *Bhāgavatam.* Śrīla Prabhupāda considered these purports his most important contribution for the revolutionary respiritualization of the entire human society, which had become plunged into the darkness of materialism.

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu also predicted that His holy name would be broadcast in every town and village of the world. *Ācāryas* of His *sampradāya* predicted that the spread of Kṛṣṇa consciousness would usher in a Golden Age within the age of Kali that will last fourteen thousand years. And in *Śrī Caitanya Maṅgala,* Locana dāsa Ṭhākura predicted that a great *senapati* (general) would appear in order to spread Lord Caitanya’s message very widely. That confidential task of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world was entrusted to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

*Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* confirms that only one who is specifically empowered by Kṛṣṇa can infuse Kṛṣṇa consciousness into the hearts of the masses.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, a great Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* who appeared in the nineteenth century, predicted that “Very soon a great personality will appear who will spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.” That great personality is clearly His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura also said that the greatness of a Vaiṣṇava can be understood by seeing how many nondevotees he can convert to Vaiṣṇavism. Ordinarily, to bring even a highly qualified person to Kṛṣṇa consciousness is considered a very difficult task. But Śrīla Prabhupāda was so much empowered by Kṛṣṇa that he went among the most unlikely candidates—the hedonistic youth of the Western countries—and made devotees by the thousands.

No one can fully understand the extraordinary task Śrīla Prabhupāda performed. He went alone among persons with no standard of Vedic culture. They had been brought up in a society that vigorously promoted meat-eating, illicit sex, gambling, and intoxication. They had no idea of how to receive a *sādhu*. They were in almost every way totally disqualified as candidates for spiritual life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda not only went among such people, but he gradually managed to train many of them to such an extent that they are now accepted everywhere as first-class Vaiṣṇavas and preachers, qualified to impart Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others.

There were certainly many Vaiṣṇavas in India who were devoted, learned, and renowned. However, the fact remains that it was Śrīla Prabhupāda alone who was sufficiently qualified to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Only he had sufficient faith in the instructions of Lord Caitanya, the order of his spiritual master, and the holy name of Kṛṣṇa to seriously attempt spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness outside India. Only he had that much compassion and vision to preach the message of Lord Caitanya to those who most needed it. Only one of the topmost confidential devotees of Kṛṣṇa could perform such an extraordinary task. His unparalleled achievements make it clear that he occupies a unique position in the history of Vaiṣṇavism.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was empowered to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in a very practical and straightforward way, just suitable for the modern world. Without changing or compromising the teachings of Kṛṣṇa consciousness even slightly, he presented its esoteric truths in a clear and intelligible manner, suitable for both the scholar and the layman simultaneously.

Śrīla Prabhupāda personally oversaw the expansion and development of ISKCON. He set up the programs that were to form the basis of ISKCON’s continued expansion: production and distribution of transcendental literature, *harināma-saṅkīrtana* parties, temples and *āśramas, prasādam* distribution, transcendental farm communities, *gurukulas,* preaching to scientists and intellectuals, etc. Śrīla Prabhupāda personally gave detailed directions in every aspect of Kṛṣṇa consciousness: how to worship the Deities, how to conduct *sādhana,* how to preach, how to wear a *dhotī,* how to cook for Kṛṣṇa, how to chant *mantras,* and so on.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is thus the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON. Whatever standards and instructions we have in ISKCON come from him. Thus Śrīla Prabhupāda will always remain the main *śikṣā-guru* for all members of ISKCON.

Both scripture and tradition offer various approaches to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. But followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda execute Kṛṣṇa consciousness just as he showed them. They know that Śrīla Prabhupāda, as a faithful follower of his *guru* and the previous *ācāryas,* presented Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the best way for the modern age. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s success is itself proof that his endeavors are approved, ordained, and blessed by the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself. Śrīla Prabhupāda gave certain instructions that are absolutely necessary for initiated disciples to follow if they at all want to claim to be serious devotees or genuine followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda. For instance: Śrīla Prabhupāda demanded that initiated devotees rise by 4:00 a.m., attend *maṅgala-ārati,* chant a minimum of sixteen rounds of the *mahā-mantra* every day, and scrupulously follow the four regulative principles.

All such standards that Śrīla Prabhupāda clearly defined are standards to be followed in ISKCON. A rightly situated, successful follower of Śrīla Prabhupāda is simply a faithful standard-bearer. He does not try to change or interpret the standards and programs given by Śrīla Prabhupāda, for he knows that what Śrīla Prabhupāda has given us is perfectly complete and completely perfect for the respiritualization of the entire human society—not only now, but for the next ten thousand years.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept my clumsy efforts to glorify you. Please empower us all to glorify you more by widely spreading knowledge of your greatness, along with that of your beloved Lord Kṛṣṇa. Please bless us all to never lose sight of your wonderful lotus feet. Please forgive me for still making so many offenses in my bumbling efforts to please you. Most of all, I beg you, shouting desperately, that however much of a nonsense rascal I am, please never let me go.

Offered by this nasty pinprick in the dust of your lotus feet,

Every desiring you mercy,  
Your eternal servant,  
Bhaktivikāśa Swami

## Bīr Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet. While meditating upon your unlimited mercy towards this fallen *jīva,* I was overcome with appreciation for the rare gift that you have given us, the gift of pure devotional service.

In your *Nectar of Devotion* you tell us that pure devotional service is rarely achieved. We often fail to realize how rare pure devotional service is, and we take it for granted due to our being complacent in your association. We forget that having your association is the greatest gift, forget about your exalted position, and forget that by your mercy you are engaging us also in pure devotional service, even if we are not fully purified at present.

How is this possible? How can a conditioned soul such as myself engage in pure devotional service? I can only engage in pure devotional service by offering my service to you, and by you offering my service to Kṛṣṇa. If I don’t follow this procedure, then Kṛṣṇa will never accept my offering. I pray to never forget this simple point.

In many places the revealed scriptures inform us about the rarity of pure devotional service. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* it is stated, *janma-koṭi-sukṛtair na labhyate:* “Pure devotional service in Kṛṣṇa consciousness cannot be had even by pious activity in hundreds and thousands of lives” (Cc. *Madhya* 8.70).

Not only is pure devotional service rare, it is extremely difficult to achieve. It can not be achieved by severe austerities and penance, by observing *brahmacārī* life, being a strict householder, being a *vānaprastha,* or even taking *sannyāsa*. *Rahuganaitāt tapasā na yāti na cejyayā nirvapanād gṛhād vā na cchandasā naiva jalāgni sūryair* (*Bhāg.* 5.12.12).

The *Mundaka Upaniṣad* informs us, *nāyam ātmā pravacanena labhyo na medhayā na bahunā śrutena:* “One cannot become self-realized simply by having an academic education, by presenting lectures in an erudite way, or by being an intelligent scientist who discovers many wonderful things” (*Bhāg.* 7.5.32, purport).

But that which is difficult, if not impossible, to achieve becomes easy by your mercy. *Vedeṣu durlabham adurlabham ātma-bhaktau* (*Brahma-saṁhitā*). You state in your purports to the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that “Actual knowledge of transcendental bliss can be bestowed upon anyone by a pure devotee” (*Bhāg.* 5.12.12, purport).

So how do we get this knowledge bestowed upon us? By smearing upon our bodies the dust of your lotus feet. *Mahīyasāṁ pāda-rajo- ‘bhiṣekam* (*Bhāg.* 7.5.32). *Mahat-pāda-rajo-’bhiṣekam* (*Bhāg.* 5.12.12).

What does this mean—to smear upon our bodies the dust of your lotus feet? We can take this statement as applying to both your *vāṇī* and your *vapu*.

As far as the *vāṇī* is concerned, we must take your instructions and desires as our life and soul. Not that we should just try to get away with the bare minimum—sixteen rounds and four regulative principles—but that we should endeavor to become first-class servants, that is, servants who understand the desire of the master and act accordingly even without being asked.

Serving your *vapu* or form can also be done, even though you have in one sense left the planet. We can serve a *mūrti* of you, we can render service at your *samādhis,* we can meditate upon doing personal service for you, or at least we can chant *japa* to a picture of you.

During this last year you have so kindly allowed me to meditate upon doing personal service for you, which is enabling me to fulfill my long-cherished desire to be your personal assistant. Please permit me to continue this.

If we rub the dust from your feet on our bodies in the above-mentioned ways, realize our subservient position in relationship to you, and have the simple faith that by following your instructions all goals will be achieved, we will be able to purify our existence, and Kṛṣṇa will allow us to enter into your association eternally in the spiritual world.

Your servant,  
Bīr Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

## Dhanurdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet, along with this humble offering on your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

**Prabhupāda Rasa**

To attain the goal  
Of love of God,  
Pure service is wanted  
To Prabhupāda.

As *rasa* with Lord  
Has *bhāvas* five,  
Same feelings with you  
Keep us alive.

The first *bhāva’s* called  
Steady ecstasy,The basic feeling  
Of service to thee.

This permanent mood  
Remains in the heart,  
Needs an excitement  
For real love to start.

Prabhupāda’s qualities,  
And his servants’ too,  
The compelling forces  
Of love for you.

Twenty-six qualities  
And your soft heart  
Excite *guru-bhakti*,  
You know the art.

Seeing your men,  
Some fifty years old,  
We think of your *līlā*,  
Courageous and bold.

Love of Śrī Guru  
Has been excited,  
But Prabhupāda *rasa*  
Not fully lighted.

Love in the heart  
Remains quite shallow  
Until service comes  
And actions follows.

By risks in service  
For the cause of Gaur,  
Mellow with master  
Deepens much more.

Rasa still absent  
Despite our prayers,  
Unless our service  
Brings us to tears.

But if your order  
Becomes life and soul,  
Dancing and trembling  
Will gladden our soul.

Oh, when will I feel  
Deep separation,  
A love that stops  
Body sensation?

Now almost *rasa*,  
The topmost emotion,  
But not yet ripened  
Until there’s commotion.

Endurance, fatigue,  
Apprehension,5Difficult preaching,  
Sometimes tension.

By serving you  
With strong emotion,  
We have all the parts  
Of pure devotion.

Before I stop  
This glorification,  
I simply beg  
With strong lamentation.

I’m wicked, unqualified,  
The biggest offender.  
I beg for the *rasa*  
Of your service to render.

Your servant,  
Dhanurdhara Swami

*The Nectar of Devotion* describes that transcendental mellow, or *rasa,* has five symptoms, or components, as follows:

1\. *sthāyi-bhāva:* continuous ecstasy, or our dominant emotions toward Kṛṣṇa in one of the five *rasas;*  
2\. *vibhāva:* causes of ecstasy, such as the Lord’s qualities or His devotees;  
3\. *anubhāva:* subsequent ecstasy, or bodily symptoms or actions by a devotee in expressing ecstatic love;  
4\. *sāttvika-bhāva:* existential ecstasy, or intense bodily manifestations of love, such as trembling or becoming stunned;  
5\. *vyabhicārī-bhāva:* aggressive ecstasy, or intense transitory feelings, such as anxiety, fatigue, or apprehension.

## Dhanvantari Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Persistent, determined, and illusioned, avoiding all sorts of liberation processes that came up until now, the conditioned living beings came to the fourth and last step in this cycle of ages.

Heroes of this great marathon of illusion, now they breathe and enjoy what their ignorance and bad *karma* selected as the “great prize.”

*prāyeṇālpayuṣaḥ sabhya kalāv asmin yuge janāḥ*  
*mandāḥ sumanda-matayo manda-bhāgyā hy upadrutāḥ*

**“O learned one, in this iron age of Kali men have but short lives. They are quarrelsome, lazy, misguided, unlucky, and, above all, always disturbed” (*****Bhāg.*** **1.1.10).**

The bad qualities reserved to the people of Kali-yuga are no longer restricted to the pages of the scriptures, but they have come out of them and penetrated our closest fellows. And admitting it frankly, they reveal themselves even in ourselves.

O Prabhupāda, how terrible it is to live like this!

Which hope can come out of our darkened hearts?

At this point of advancement of the Age of Kali, even Lord Caitanya and His associates have left the world. Even yourself, the most brave and kind devotee of the Lord, are no longer here. All seems inauspicious to me.

Feeling so desolate, without any other shelter, I declare myself the most dependent servant of yours and pray to Your Divine Grace to allow me to follow your instructions, which will qualify me to cultivate the seed of devotion, the *bhakti-latā-bīja,* and develop Kṛṣṇa *prema-bhakti,* despite the fact that I am an authentic Kali-yuga man.

Your neophyte servant,  
Dhanvantari Swami

## Dyutidhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In the awakened state one see’s you as that great saintly Vaiṣṇava who gave everything as an offering to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Wherever you went, whomever you spoke with, you were always preoccupied with Kṛṣṇa. Dancing for Kṛṣṇa, chanting for Kṛṣṇa, writing for Kṛṣṇa, preaching for Kṛṣṇa, offering all and everything for His pleasure. Who can imagine your pure state of sublime consciousness, seeing the Lord directly and also within the facets of His diverse energies? You epitomize Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, the enlivened state of pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness wherein one always has great intense love of Godhead and is infatuated in devotional service.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, to see you and hearing of your activities and pastimes is to also see Kṛṣṇa in His feature of pure compassion, always eager to reciprocate in devotional service. You wanted the unused light turned off, you wanted the *lakṣmī* accounted for to the *paisa*. You saw everything as rare and special, being but a form of the Lord’s energy. When the reporters interviewed you and your disciples, a disciple said that God guided you. You said no, He personally spoke to you.

How fortunate we have been to tread in the shadow of your footsteps. You have never compromised and have ruthlessly smashed the illusions of *māyā*. Pounding with your mighty pen, you have traversed the earth defining and defending the real understanding of God. So many men have had to lay down at your lotus feet and surrender, their false egos fatally wounded, their pride and arrogance defeated.

When you slay *māyā* with love of God, you release that pure captive and return him to his original blissful state. When he’s unchained again, he also revives his original *bhakti-śakti* and wants the same for others.

They questioned, How is this man going on beating his drum, his *bhakti-mṛdaṅga*? But you replied that you would never die, you would live forever in your books and in the hearts of your disciples.

You gave everyone the sweetest thing that the heart always hankers for. You gave yourself freely and selflessly, and you never grasped for name or fame. Of course, your fame is sung throughout all the fourteen worlds. The name “Śrīla Prabhupāda” is synonymous with the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, the movement that historians will note changed the course of history.

As you wrote in your book *Light of the Bhāgavat,* the living entities in a pond don’t realize that every day the water is gradually evaporating. Similarly, the world in general does not realize how every day another *jīva* is granted *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* by Your Divine Grace. Your teachings are found everywhere, and if a person knowingly or unknowingly tastes but a drop, his material life will wither and die. But this kind of death is not ordinary death, for in place of his material life will spring up a new life of unbounded joy and spiritual energy, giving life anew at every second and unfolding the incomparable sweetness of Kṛṣṇa *rasa*.

The disciple prays to the spiritual master to please engage him in devotional service because the spiritual master is tasting the ever-increasing waves of ecstasy at every moment. You are the bona fide representative of Śrī Hari and Lord Caitanya, and one should be eager to serve your lotus feet at every moment, aspiring to do that which always brings you pleasure. It is not at all difficult to follow your instructions, for simply by aural reception all that is inauspicious in the heart is gradually reduced to nil.

Coming in the line of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you emphasized chanting, dancing, and feasting. You said that those who are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa are the most fortunate, and that if one can benefit from chanting for fifteen minutes, then why not chant for fifteen hours?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our only hope is to receive your mercy, and if you keep favoring us with your mercy and kindness, then there will never be any darkness that can defeat us. You are our great leader and champion, and as you rush into the face battle against Māyā, let us go forward behind you chanting your sublime glories. You are our eternal shelter.

Śrīla Prabhupada *kī jaya*!

Your servant,  
Dyutidhara Swami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my fallen obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

*yānra citte kṛṣṇa-premā karaye udaya*  
*tāṅra vākya, kriyā, mudrā vijñeha nā bujhaya*

**“Even the most learned man cannot understand the words, activities, and symptoms of a person situated in love of Godhead” (Cc.** ***Madhya*** **23.39).**

If even the greatest scholar cannot begin to fathom the depth of your personality, of what use is my puny attempt? Still, I feel I have found a clue to your amazing dedication in the words of Karabhājana Muni. And because you are the personification of Lord Caitanya’s mercy, ŚRĪ-GAURA-KARUṆĀ-ŚAKTI-VIGRAHĀYA, I certainly feel correct in employing verses meant for Mahāprabhu in the glorification of Your Divine Grace.

Śrī Karabhājana says: *māyā-mṛgaṁ dayitayepsitam anvadhāvad:* “Out of sheer mercifulness you chased after the fallen, conditioned souls, who are always in pursuit of the false enjoyment of illusion, and at the same time engaged in searching out your own desired object, Lord Kṛṣṇa” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 11.5.34).

Being in the mood of Prahlāda Mahārāja (*tvad-vīrya-gāyana-mahāmṛta-magna-citta*), wherever you stay you are fully absorbed in thoughts of Kṛṣṇa’s glories and activities. But your only concern is for the fools and rascals who are making elaborate plans for material happiness and maintaining their families, societies, and countries. Indeed, Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī glorifies you when he says, *kṛpāmbudhir yaṁ para-duḥkha-duḥkhī:* For yourself you know no unhappiness—you feel unhappiness only on account of the suffering of others. This is certainly the predominant theme of your descent in the material world.

You not only “chased” us down, but you tricked us and practically blackmailed us into engaging in devotional service. This was the unlimited extent of your mercy: you made us surrender when we had no desire to. Or rather, being the topmost *mahābhāgavata,* you knew our innermost longings much better than we ourselves did.

For one such as I, steeped in the laws and limitations of the mundane world, the motivations of your transcendental activities remain mysterious and magical. This “frog in the well” has only the polluted example of himself to relate to. Yet even though I am lethargic, greedy, envious, and offensive to Your Divine Grace and your followers, you still appear in my mind and in my dreams; and beyond my wildest expectations, you even exhibit some satisfaction with my paltry service.

Please help me to learn to “fan the spark” in others and encourage them in devotional service, as you taught us. I beg you to keep chasing after this wayward and stubborn son. Only your *uttama*-vision can see any hope in my future. Please don’t give up and kick me away. (Of course, even your kick is purifying, as you once stated regarding Lord Kṛṣṇa.) It is your disgust and neglect that I most fear, for your mercy is all that I am made of. So, as the moon does not withhold its shining from the courtyard of even a crooked person, I request you to similarly not deny your transcendental mercy to me, although any service I have rendered you was mixed with selfish motives.

Your pitiful disciple,  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Giridhārī Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

In 1973 you wrote to one disciple, “The spiritual master lives forever by his divine instructions, and the disciple lives with him. Because I have always served my Guru Mahārāja and followed his teachings, I am now even never separated from him.” Time and time again throughout your books, letters, and tapes, you stressed this simple but essential truth: “The spiritual master is present wherever his sincere disciple is trying to serve his instructions.”

For one as dull, foolish, and undevotional as myself, realization of this *vāṇī* principle is very difficult to attain. Despite my attempts to sincerely follow your instructions and render you service, I still fall far short of feeling your presence at all times, in all places, and in all circumstances.

Seeing me in this pitiful condition could have been the reason that you mercifully engaged me in organizing your Philippines Padayātrā. This engagement was so totally absorbing that I was actually able to have a glimpse of what it might be like to feel your constant presence, guidance, and inspiration. Beyond this, I was able to witness the unfolding of a wonderful plan you had conceived to bless the people of the Philippines with *harināma,* book and *prasādam* distribution, and the *darśana* of Śrī Śrī Nitāi-sundara Gaura-naṭarāja, accompanied by Their pre-eminent servitor, Your Divine Grace.

Like the hand of the puppeteer bringing his puppets to life, your desire actuated our movements in making all preparations for your plan to be executed. Factually, everything was being done by you. It was your desire that Padayātrā be held in every town and village, it was you who inspired and directed us, and finally, it was you who rode regally on the Lord’s chariot, guiding our every step.

After months of preparation, the long-awaited day finally arrived. Over fifty devotees gathered together from different parts of the Philippines, and also present were two of your dearmost servitors, Tamāl Krishna Goswami and Lokanāth Swami, all to be led on a glorious procession by Your Divine Grace.

As conchshells resounded in all directions, the devotees carefully placed your beautiful form on a velvet-covered palanquin and carried you to a colorful Padayātrā cart waiting outside. After you were seated on your *vyāsāsana,* all the devotees spontaneously chanted, “*Jaya* Prabhupāda! *Jaya* Prabhupāda!” and the Padayātrā was on its way.

For eight days you continuously led us in chanting and dancing ecstatically through the streets of Manila. By your mercy, thousands heard the holy name, took *prasādam,* and received your transcendental books!

You not only inspired and directed our *saṅkīrtana* efforts, but, as expected, you were also the top preacher. Seeing your *mūrti,* hundreds of people spontaneously asked who you were and wanted to know more about your life and teachings. Directly empowered by you, old and new devotees alike spoke your glories like madmen. The conditioned souls were moved, lifetimes of sin were destroyed, and hearts were melted.

After Manila, in fulfillment of Lord Caitanya’s prophecy, you led the Padayātrā to the provinces. People in small towns like Balagtos, Malolos and Calumpit were completely amazed. They had never even heard Kṛṣṇa’s name before, what to speak of seeing twenty devotees enthusiastically chanting, dancing, and distributing *prasādam*! We thus saw you working miracles in their lives.

Your Philippines Padayātrā glories don’t stop here. Rather, you will expand these glories manyfold by pushing us to organize an ongoing Padayātrā four to five months every year. Thousands more will get the chance to take *darśana* of your golden form and be blessed by your all-compassionate glances. We are eagerly awaiting the many wonderful preaching adventures you have in store for us.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me some small realizations about your unlimited greatness and all-pervading nature. Your divine qualities and glorious activities are unfathomable. Only by continuing to take shelter at your lotus feet will I gradually become eligible to understand more.

Please bless me with the ability to follow all of your instructions perfectly, grant me the privilege of serving your devotees, and allow me to serve you in any way you desire, birth after birth.

Begging for you mercy, I remain  
Your insignificant servant,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

Roaming Māyā’s kingdom,  
Disguised in human form,  
Hellbent sense enjoyer,  
Never thought about reform.

Then came special mercy,  
As Kṛṣṇa ripped away  
All my so-called assets  
And sent me on my way.

“Where to go? What to do?”  
A prayer that seemed in vain.  
A boat without a rudder,  
Adrift in Māyā’s domain.

The answer finally came,  
As Kṛṣṇa spoke through you,  
But I could not admit  
That what you said was true.

I stood proudly and proclaimed,  
With Māyā’s flag unfurled,  
“Serving God does not compare  
To the pleasures of this world.”

So I kept on with my nescience,  
Truth choosing to ignore,  
Till Māyā beat me down so hard  
At last I cried, “No more!”

As I crawled into the temple,  
I said, “I’ll tell you true:  
Serving God is not my mood,  
But I’ve nothing else to do.”

Just the thought of sense control  
Put shivers through my spine.  
Bt then, what had I gained from sin?  
So I chose to toe the line.

To my complete amazement,  
Your mercy worked on me.  
I felt a kind of joy  
I never thought could be.

I knew then that your guidance  
Would be my life and soul.  
To help you in your mission  
Would be my life-long goal.

But still I bowed to Māyā,  
And thus I didn’t heed  
Your plea to sell your books,  
To save a world in need.

Although your books were stockpiled,  
My mind kept telling me,  
“*Saṅkīrtana* austerities  
Are not your cup of tea.”

But by your strong desire  
I was finally swept away:  
Book distribution fever  
Became the nectar of the day.

Thus again you showed me  
That what I’d thought was pain  
Was only pleasure.  
My fears had been mundane.

I’ve had to learn that lesson  
Time and time again.  
You beckon, then you save me,  
My master! My friend!

I beg you, please keep pushing,  
No matter how I fight,  
No matter what it takes.  
Someday I’ll see the light.

Your helpless servant,  
Guṇagrāhī dāsa Swami

## Hanumātpreṣaka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The spiritual master is like a piece of window glass. It is very difficult to glorify him because he is by nature transparent. He is the medium by which we see beyond the impoverished walls of this dying slum-dwelling that is our mind and senses. By his mercy we can see the clear blue sky of devotional service to Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who are always engaged in Their ecstatic, overwhelming pastimes of exchange of emotional love in the spiritual world.

Our dear Mother, You are very dear to the Supreme Lord in all of His moods as Śrī Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu, Nandalāl Śyāmasundara, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and the beautiful Lord Rāmacandra. Our life and soul is to remind You of His love for You and His infinite concern to deliver You from the hands of these evil materialists. Please pray to our Lord and master that we never forget that we are nothing more than insignificant particles of dust at the lotus feet of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, and that we may be able to serve You properly by being inspired more and more with the proper understanding and love of the lotus feet of His Divine Grace.

Your most insignificant servant,  
Hanumātpreṣaka Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you on this most auspicious occasion of your appearance within this world!

Each year at this time I sit to reflect and compose my offering to you. Thoughts come, words flow, and I put them down on paper. But one page and one day alone seem inadequate to express my love; it will be more appropriate if I can offer my life to you.

Each year I come to appreciate more the gifts you have given me. As time passes, all that is familiar in this world gradually fades, yet Kṛṣṇa consciousness remains fresh, and with it my hopes to attain your grace. Birth, death, old age, and disease are no longer unfamiliar; I see them the world over, wherever you send me to preach. With the realities of material life often present, I remember your words to us: “I have been everywhere and seen everything. My advice to you is to take *sannyāsa*.”

However, I am unable to always see as you say. Though I repeatedly hear, I remain foolishly attached. Nevertheless, you kindly arrange to instruct me. This year when little Viśākhā broke her neck and lay in the hospital bed, she was informed she would never walk again. On the phone she told me, “Why should I lament? I’m spirit soul—this body is only a frame. Besides, one day I’ll be back with Kṛṣṇa again.” I wonder at your grace that a child may have such realizations and instruct me in the same.

When I visited her for the first time after the accident, her face glowed and shined. “I’ve had many realizations,” she said.

“But you’re only twelve years old!” I thought.

Glancing over at your picture, she said, “I can understand that before the accident I was frivolous and wasn’t making the best use of my time. Now I want to become serious and take shelter of Kṛṣṇa.” I prayed for the same.

In the rehabilitation ward, as the doctors struggled in vain to teach her to move, she collapsed like a rag doll on the mat. With pity and compassion they raised her up, only to be amazed at the smile on her face. “Everyone here is in the bodily concept of life,” she said to me. “Therefore they can only lament.” Such is your mercy that we might live in this world without regret.

As I took her in the wheelchair back to her room, we laughed at the foolishness of the materialists, who only hanker and lament. But I felt we were laughing at me; an older devotee with less realization than that of this child.

As we turned the corner, she looked up and said, “But we can save them if we preach.” Her words were the purports I’ve read in your books, but her realizations were different than mine. What is this great mercy of your’s that transcends even time!

That evening I brought my Deities at her request. She stared at them a long time, not casually, as I often do. With deep feeling she said, “I missed Them a lot.” And I thought, “Why can’t I?”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for your instructions from the mouth of a child. I’ve had many realizations now. You know, “Before the accident I was frivolous and wasn’t making the best use of my time. Now I want to become serious and take shelter of Kṛṣṇa.”

Your eternal servant,  
Indradyumna Swami

## Īśvara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you.

On this most auspicious occasion of your divine appearance in this world, please let me try to compare you to Prahlāda Mahārāja. No doubt you have all the great qualities of the *mahājanas,* but I think your similarity to Prahlāda Mahārāja is unique. He is the perfect example of dedication to and confidence in Kṛṣṇa: he had to face so much opposition from demoniac forces, but still he remained so determined in his devotion toward Kṛṣṇa—so fearless, so surrendered, so compassionate. Out of his kindness he wanted to deliver all the fools and rascals and did not care for his own comfort. And most special of all, due to his intimate relationship with the Lord, he could directly approach the all-powerful Supreme Lord, Śrī Nṛsiṁha-deva, when no one else would dare to go. His pure devotion pacified the Lord. In the same way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you conquered the Lord by your love, and you have the power to give Him to us.

Being completely confident of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mercy and protection, you showed us the way to get free from illusion. You taught us to always pray to Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva so that He would protect your ISKCON movement, as well as our own lives, because you knew He would never deny what you ask from Him. So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am eternally grateful for your divine quality of compassion and for having shown us the path of the *mahājanas*. Thank you very much unlimited times for giving us your dear Prahlāda Mahārāja and the whole Kṛṣṇa conscious world.

I beg to always remain under the shelter of your compassionate lotus feet.

Your fallen servant,  
Īśvara Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Because my office is next door to that of Draviḍa Prabhu, who compiles your Vyāsa-pūjā book, I get to read some of the offerings early. And so it is that I’ve read the offering by His Holiness Indradyumna Mahārāja.

As a result, I feel that whatever I might have planned to say—some neat rhymes, some learned quotations—would by comparison be trivial and superficial.

It is by your grace that those who sincerely follow you are blessed with advanced understanding, and the ability to express it purely.

Their Kṛṣṇa consciousness is evidence of your greatness.

If I can always be engaged in serving your lotus feet in the company of such devotees, I’ll consider myself a most fortunate person.

With my head at your feet,  
Your servant,  
Jayādvaita Swami

## Krishna dāsa Swami

My dear eternal spiritual master,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is truly wonderful to see how you are slowly spiritualizing the whole world. Therefore kindly allow me to speak of your wonderful spiritual potency.

Your spiritual potency is so great that throughout Eastern Europe the old communist countries are not only undergoing political revolution, but they are also undergoing a spiritual revolution by means of the secret weapon you have given to the devotees—book distribution. The many surrendered souls who are carrying out your instructions in these countries are employing this weapon very expertly, making sure that as many people as possible are affected by it.

Your spiritual potency is so great that Lord Jagannātha is bestowing His merciful glance on more and more people every year as Rathayātrā processions are introduced in more and more cities throughout the world. Even in very troubled places such as Punjab in India, you do not spare your mercy but allow everyone to take Lord Jagannātha’s *darśana* by using your devotees to arrange Rathayātrās.

Your spiritual potency is so great that Padayātrās are now being organized in more and more countries throughout the world, thus ensuring that the chanting of the Lord’s holy names is carried to every town and village, as predicted by Lord Caitanya.

Your spiritual potency is so great that I stand back in amazement as I personally witness the turning into reality of your vision of Mauritius becoming the first Kṛṣṇa conscious country in the world. Not only have the last few years seen an entire village be renamed as Vṛndāvana and the two roads leading into that village renamed as Hare Kṛṣṇa Road and Vṛndāvana Road, but in the last year another road in another village has been renamed as Bhaktivedanta Swami Lane.

Your spiritual potency is so great that you are liberally distributing your mercy to one and all without discrimination, and all we have to do is to cup our hands to receive it. Many wonderful devotees throughout the world have surrendered themselves to you, and they are very fortunate because by your spiritual potency you are engaging them in Kṛṣṇa’s service to spiritualize the whole world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be merciful to me, as I also stand with my hands cupped to receive your mercy. It is only by your mercy that I too can be engaged in your service and thus ensure that this valuable human birth isn’t wasted.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is a fact that you left the holy land of India at the advanced age of seventy just for the sake of the fallen souls of the world. What most people forget, however, is that toward the end of your *līlā* on this planet you were actually over eighty years old! But you showed by your own personal example how not to waste this human form of life by preaching vigorously until the very last moment, having little or no regard for your own body.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have not yet reached the age of seventy, but my body is already giving me so much trouble, forcing me to become body-conscious and less efficient in my service to Kṛṣṇa. Please bless me so that I may learn from your own example how to dedicate my mind, body, and soul in the service of Kṛṣṇa. Just as your preaching became more and more vigorous with the passing of each day, similarly, let my preaching also become more and more vigorous as eternal time eats away the remaining days of my life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, if you will permit me, please allow me to make a special plea to you on behalf of all the devotees in the UK *yātrā*. For several years now, Bhaktivedanta Manor has been fighting a legal battle with the local council to keep this important place open to the public. Despite the tremendous expertise, skill, and stamina with which many wonderful devotees have fought with the council, it appears that the fight is going against us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please forgive our offenses, for it can only be due to our offenses that we are now facing this grave situation. If this weren’t the case, then it is unlikely we would be facing such a calamity, because as you have repeatedly pointed out to us, “Purity is the force.” But somehow we are always offensive to you and Their Lordships.

Bhaktivedanta Manor is very dear to me personally because it was there that I first met you. In that first meeting you showered your mercy on me, with the result that I gave up my householder life and joined ISKCON. Bhaktivedanta Manor is also very dear to all the devotees who have given some service to it throughout its many years. It is also the place where you made your last visit outside India. So historically it is a very important place to ISKCON, and it is a place of pilgrimage for the devotees.

So, on behalf of all the devotees, I would like to beg you to please petition Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda, Śrī Śrī Sītā-Rāma-Lakṣmaṇa-Hanumān, and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi to forgive us for our offenses and to give us the necessary strength and intelligence to find a solution to our problem, and keep Bhaktivedanta Manor open to the public.

Begging to render some menial service at your divine lotus feet, I remain  
Your aspiring servant,  
Krishna dāsa Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

For the last few years we have been talking of Padayātrā in 100 countries as a centennial celebration offering. Last year we came up with a tentative list of many other projects using 100s or multiples of 100 as offerings. To help us keep focused on the centennial goals, we have framed the plan in the symbol of a ten-petaled lotus. In this presentation, the “100s” form only one petal of the lotus. I would like to give you a brief account of all these petals.

**The Lotus of Activities**

**I. Offerings in multiples of 100**

The first category deals with our efforts to achieve substantial success in key areas of ISKCON as an offering to you. We are encouraging goals using the 100 figure or multiples of 100. Most of these goals could be achieved over the four-year campaign period.

• 100 countries hosting Padayātrā.  
• 100 scholars honoring you.  
• 100,000 subscribers to BTG.  
• 100 restaurants worldwide.  
• Your books published in 100 languages.  
• 100,000 congregational members worldwide.

The list goes on and on.

**II. Increasing Prabhupāda Consciousness**

This petal deals with increasing our consciousness of you. This is not only an end in itself but a necessary foundation for successful fund-raising and organization of all other activities.

Here are some specific ideas for doing this.

• *Līlāmṛta* classes once a week  
• Distribution of photos, stickers, posters, calendars, etc., with your picture on them  
• Seeing and hearing your lecture audio /video tapes  
• Distribution campaign of your *Līlāmṛta*  
• Special book distribution for your pleasure  
• Increased understanding and appreciation of your books  
• School competitions  
• Movie and video showings

**III. Appreciation and Recognition of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Naturally we want you to be recognized all over the world by government officials, scholars, and VIP’s. To accomplish this, an internationally coordinated effort is required.

Here is the outline:

1.) National and local governments

a) Rename public areas in your name

• Streets

• Parks

b) World leaders should honor you. This should be done on TV and  
through other news media.

c) Stamps and coins with your likeness should be issued by as many  
countries as possible.

d) Installation of monuments, plaques, etc., honoring you should be  
done by public officials.

2.) International

a) U.N. recognition of your accomplishments  
b) International awards for you

3.) Celebrities and VIP’s

a) Favorable famous personalities like George Harrison should step forward and honor you.  
b) Vegetarian or New Age celebrities should honor you

4.) Scholars and literati

a) Book reviews of your books  
b) Special seminars about your literary and philosophical achieve-  
ments

**IV. Prabhupāda Memorials and Institutions**

We must plan to establish or complete the various memorials and institutions, as outlined here:

1\. Your *samādhis* in Māyāpur and Vṛndāvana  
2\. Your birthplace—Calcutta  
3\. Pastime places—memorials  
a) India and abroad  
4\. Museum in major cities  
5\. Temple memorials—renovating quarters

**V. Prabhupāda Publications and Productions**

There are a number of special publications that should be published for this event. Initially, we are planning editions of the *Līlāmṛta* for India, and separate *Līlāmṛta* books for different cities, as well as a Pictorial Biography

Audiovisual presentations  
1\. Documentary feature film  
2\. TV serial  
3\. Dramas depicting your pastimes  
4\. Musical productions

**VI. Prabhupāda Special Events**

In addition to major celebrations that may be organized at suitable times, attention must be paid to the celebration of your pastime events.

1\. Vyāsa-pūjā and disappearance festivals celebrated around the world with great fanfare.  
2\. Festivals at important places of *līlā* on special dates  
a) Boston  
b) Haight-Ashbury  
c) Tompkins Square Park  
3\. Festivals in each city revolving around your *līlā* there.

**VII. Commemorative Paraphernalia and Memorabilia**

As with any function of this size, there are numerous opportunities to produce various souvenirs for publicity and fund-raising purposes. We should study how this was done with events like the Olympic Games and try to implement an organized program with the help of devotee and Life Member businessmen.

Some ideas include:

1\. A commemorative coin  
2\. T-shirts, caps, pens, etc.  
3\. Limited-edition artwork

**VIII. Major Public Events and Programs**

Now we come to the BIG SHOW that we want to put on in 1996, to make the whole world sit up, take notice, and join in. These programs have to be substantial enough that the world media and the public will take definite interest. They should also clearly communicate your message and inspire deep appreciation.

These major programs will be our primary method of celebration, and will also involve the participation of the general public.

One of these events—most likely the Calcutta celebration—should provide a sort of high point of the whole program and culminate with a grand finale—Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Book Marathon in December 1996.

Here are the ideas so far:

1\. World Saṅkīrtana Tour

You spoke of a World Traveling Saṅkīrtana Party, with a team of preachers. This tour would try to accomplish this with the world’s best preachers, *kīrtanīyas,* actors, etc., traveling together and holding festivals in conjunction with the local devotees.

a) A world tour by our leading preachers  
b) 1,000 devotees, Nāma Hatta members  
c) Two chartered Jumbo jets  
d) Carrying your *mūrti*  
e) Travel all over the world for 100 days one year  
f) Huge festival in capitals and main cities  
g) Impressive cultural performances (theater, music, etc.)

2\. World Holy Name Day

3\. Bhaktivedanta Institute Conference—Calcutta ‘96

4\. Series of conferences and festivals throughout the year

5\. Blitz of mass media

6\. Global Padayātrā—one-week simultaneous Padayātrā throughout the world

7\. ISKCON International Convention—Gaura Pūrṇimā Festival

8\. The Calcutta Celebration of your Vyāsa-pūjā will feature the following events:

a) 100 countries Padayātrā procession  
b) *Prasādam* for entire city of Calcutta  
c) A BI conference  
d) Gītā Jayantī campaign  
e) Pastime *parikrama* (Harrison Rd., Rādhā-Govinda, etc.)  
f) Worlds biggest birthday cake

**IX. ISKCON Rejuvenation**

You told us that we will show our love for you by how well we cooperate. We believe we must pay attention to strengthening ISKCON as a unified preaching force. That will not only please you but is essential if we are to achieve our goals.

Some areas to focus on are:

a) High spiritual standards among all members.  
b) Understanding your specific instructions for each country  
c) Establishing *siddhānta* on contentious issues, e.g.:  
• *Gurus* in ISKCON  
• Women’s role in ISKCON  
• Relations with non-ISKCON Vaiṣṇavas  
d) Understanding *varṇāśrama* in ISKCON  
e) Publishing an ISKCON constitution  
f) Raising the standard of management and administration

**X. Reuniting Prabhupāda’s family**

Your centennial celebration presents a unique opportunity for us to bring your disciples and grand-disciples closer to their spiritual father, and thus closer to their brothers and sisters as well.

This can be achieved by:

• Organized reunions  
• Publishing a directory and newsletter  
• Encouraging remembering personal instructions  
• Encouraging contact with inactive disciples  
• Creating programs for active involvement  
• Counseling for healing and guidance

It is better to talk less and work more, for action speaks louder than words. Leaders as well as devotees in general seem to be getting serious about this celebration. This is evident from the fact that the GBC has formed a global ministry for coordinating your centennial.

In terms of both quality and quantity, we hope to make this celebration the most spectacular event of the century. We don’t just want a flash-in-the-pan celebration. But we want to perpetuate your glorification.

Broadly speaking, there are two ways of appreciating you. One of them is through your achievements: number of temples opened, number of disciples initiated, number of books translated, etc. When devotees talk of these achievements, people listen, become amazed, and develop appreciation for you. However, if they are to turn into your true followers they need to hear of your personal qualities, like compassion, gravity, humility, expertise, tolerance, and patience.

Jesus Christ is not famous for his achievements. He never opened any temples. However, he is famous for his compassion and other spiritual qualities. Therefore, for the world to recognize and understand who you are, we need to stress both of these aspects during your centennial.

Now one and all we are begging at the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa that He may give us the necessary intelligence and assistance to glorify you in the grandest manner possible.

Thank you for engaging me.

Your insignificant son,  
Lokanāth Swami

## Mahānidhi Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On arriving in Boston on the 17th of September, 1965, you composed a Bengali prayer petitioning Lord Śrī Krsna to deliver the fallen souls. Śrīla Prabhupāda, with your divine lotus hand you concluded that poem by writing, “Signed—the most unfortunate, insignificant beggar, A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami . . .”

From a position of absolute humility as Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī’s pure loving servitor, you felt yourself devoid of fortune, insignificant and penniless, but we, being bankrupt of *bhakti,* saw you as the most significant and benevolent philanthropist giving the precious fortune of Rādhā’s and Kṛṣṇa’s service to poor souls like ourselves. Although in the poem you described yourself as “unfortunate,” we gradually realized that you possessed the inconceivably valuable fortune of the surrendered service of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī’s divine lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were very careful about discussing your relationship with Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, but you once revealed to a Godbrother, “My *guru* is Rādhārāṇī, and Rādhārāṇī, my Gurudeva, has ordered me to preach in the West. Dear Kṛṣṇa, being my friend, You must help me because my Gurudeva will be satisfied. Your business is also like that—to please Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. So You must come to help me, because I am going to carry out the order of Rādhārāṇī.”

A week before arriving in Boston you felt separation from Rādhā-Dāmodara and Śrī Vṛndāvana as the ship smoothly plied the seas. While your softened heart was swimming in the ocean of *rasa,* you composed a “Prayer to the Lotus Feet of Kṛṣṇa,” wherein you disclosed the secret treasure, the cherished prize, the most fortunate gift of the Gosvāmīs—the service of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. You stressed this point in the poem’s refrain: “I emphatically say to you, O brothers, you will obtain your good fortune from the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa only when Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī becomes pleased with you.”

When accepting the renounced order of life a Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava assumes the title “Tridaṇḍī Bhikṣu.” *Bhikṣu* means beggar. All devotees are beggars, from the *bhaktas* to the *bhikṣus*.

Then you taught us to beg Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, “O Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, O energy of the Lord, please engage us in the service of Kṛṣṇa.” We begged at the lotus feet of Śrī Harināma. We begged Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu and the Pañca-tattva to please empower us to distribute these wonderful books and receive donations. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we begged you for *dikṣā,* and we begged Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda to bless us with attachment and love for Their service and servants.

Every morning we beg for service, and at night we beg that you forgive our offenses. We are crying and begging you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to please reveal your divine form and ours as servants of the servants of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us your service, for without it we are insignificant. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please engage us at the lotus feet of Śrī Rādhā—for that most significant fortune we are beggars.

Signed—a most unfortunate, insignificant beggar,  
Mahānidhi Swami

## Mahā-Viṣṇu Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You are glorious because you understood and took to heart the order of your Guru Mahārāja, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Aṣṭottara Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja. Your Guru Mahārāja’s desire was that the books containing Lord Caitanya’s message be published in the English language and that the preaching go on boldly far and wide. You have pleased him by striving to fulfill his desire, and on the strength of your sincere following of his order, the whole ISKCON movement is springing up from the seeds of your transcendental books in every country and clime. Yet you only reluctantly have agreed that if any credit is due to you it is because you simply tried to please your Guru Mahārāja.

No, your faithful disciples, led by Harikeśa Mahārāja especially, are following solidly in your footsteps and trying to inundate the world with your books by all means. Your grand-disciple heroes are truly rallying to their master’s calls to distribute your books more and more. It is so inspiring, and in this way the success of ISKCON, come what may, is assured. Transcendental book distribution *kī jaya*!

But on this, your glorious 96th Vyāsa-pūjā day, I feel despondent. The point was always so simple, but I could not see the wood for the trees! My disease is that I have not pleased you properly. I am very sorry that after so many years’ “preaching,” so few Nepali books are published and distributed.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, try to forgive me and give me your blessed kicks to make me single-minded—book-minded—so that we will shortly see, for your centennial celebration and before, all your shining books shedding mercy on the Himalayan people.

Your most fallen and unqualified servant,  
Mahā-Viṣṇu Swami \[the younger\]

## Nava-yogendra Swami

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Kṛṣṇa. Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who are very merciful upon the fallen souls like myself.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, today is your appearance day. Yesterday was the anniversary of the appearance of the cause of all causes, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who came in order to deliver the message of *Bhagavad-gītā* so that fallen souls can understand the real meaning of human life. But people are so fallen that they forgot the message of *Bhagavad-gītā* and again became too conscious of their bodies. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your beautiful and wonderful translation and purports of *Bhagavad-gītā,* enabled so many people around the world to understand the real meaning of human life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the true follower of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and you are very dear to Him. In the *Gītā* (18.68-69) the Lord says, “For one who explains this supreme secret to the devotees, devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end he will come back to Me. There is no servant in this world more dear to Me than he, nor will there ever be one more dear.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, Śrī Kṛṣṇa is yours, and if you like you can give Him to me also. If you don’t like, I can’t do anything. A fallen soul like myself must have your mercy. Without your mercy, I will simply weep. Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly be merciful upon this fallen soul so that I can save myself from going to hell. I am already in hell—this material world is a hell, a place of miseries. Therefore your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is the only hope.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I sincerely want to be a little instrument in your great ISKCON, though I am devoid of any good quality. Out of your causeless mercy, kindly allow me to do so. Please give me strength, intelligence, and proper understanding so that I can be a good instrument. Kindly make me more humble than a blade of grass and more tolerant than a tree, devoid of all sense of false prestige, and always ready to offer respects to all the Vaiṣṇavas.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your most fallen disciple of all,  
Nava-yogendra Swami

## Paraṁ-gati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*titikṣavaḥ kāruṇikāḥ suhṛdaḥ sarva-dehinām*  
*ajāta-śatravaḥ śāntāḥ sādhavaḥ sādhu-bhūṣaṇāḥ*

**“The symptoms of a** ***sādhu*** **are that he is tolerant, merciful, and friendly to all living entities. He has no enemies, he is peaceful, he abides by the scriptures, and all his characteristics are sublime” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **3.25.21).**

On this holy day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, O my *guru,* millions of words are insufficient to describe your transcendental qualities. Your infinite mercy ever expands itself in so many practical ways.

During a class you gave in New York in March of 1966, you said, “Now here I am sitting \[in\] New York, … the world’s greatest city, but my heart is always hankering after that Vṛndāvana.… I am not happy here.… I shall be very happy to return to my Vṛndāvana. \[Then why am I here?\] Because it is my duty.… Because I am ordered by superior, my spiritual master.”

Since the very beginning of your movement, you showed the importance of serving the mission of the spiritual master with one’s life and soul. The people try to escape the harsh reality of this world, buying illusion in the big marketplace of so-called happiness at high prices. They are hypnotized by shopping centers and all kinds of fantasy worlds. Thus in this great phantasmagoria, they delude themselves even until death.

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate giriṁ*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of all fallen souls, whose mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.”**

You came to end this foolish dream, to show the reality of the spiritual world. In one of your purports in the Sixth Canto (6.5.14) you say, “In our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, fashionable persons are taught to adopt one fashion—the dress of a Vaiṣṇava with a shaved head and *tilaka*. They are taught to be always clean in mind, dress, and eating in order to be fixed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

*yathā kāñcanatāṁ yāti kāṁsyaṁ rasa-vidhānataḥ*  
*tathā dīkṣā-vidhānena dvijatvaṁ jāyate nṛṇām*

**“As bell metal is turned into gold when mixed with mercury in an alchemical process, so one who is properly trained and initiated by a bona fide spiritual master immediately becomes a** ***brāhmaṇa*****.”**

O my dear eternal father, you worked so hard to protect this precious gem, ISKCON, the only hope for the future of human society.

To follow seriously your instructions in every respect, to distribute your books, to preach vigorously to make new devotees—all these things will help maintain ISKCON strong and united to push on your mission.

I humbly pray for your protection so I may continue to serve the servants of your servants and in this way maintain the dust of your lotus feet on my head.

Your eternal servant,  
Paraṁ-gati Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Within your heart is the spiritual world, where Kṛṣṇa and His transcendental associates perform their eternal *līlā*. The Lord has kindly sent you to teach us, the conditioned souls of this material world, how we can join His association through service. Out of your devotional love you have created the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, ISKCON, and you have explained to us that serving Kṛṣṇa means serving this movement.

Seeing the defects in this movement or in its devotees is not difficult for such expert faultfinders as us. But by your training we are also learning to appreciate ISKCON, the only movement that is doing anything of lasting value. And if

this movement has any problems, because we appreciate you we will try to rec-

tify them. By working with such full appreciation, we can advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

The more we appreciate the enthusiasm, conviction, and patience of ISKCON’s devotees, big and small, the sweeter our relationships become, and the sweeter our lives. The best way to learn how to appreciate this movement and its devotees is to meditate on how you appreciate them, and how this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement was created and is expanding on the basis of your loving appreciation. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give me even a drop of such appreciation.

Your servant,  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Puruṣatraya Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so great, so exalted. You are incomparable within these three worlds. Since I am insignificant, I cannot precisely evaluate your actual greatness; I can only visualize some traces of it. But I am convinced that you possess unlimited qualities due to your closeness to Kṛṣṇa. Still, to me you appear like an iceberg, which shows only a small fraction of its colossal body. Indeed, I doubt whether there exists on this earth an advanced soul who can understand you in full, since one’s spiritual attributes can only be properly measured by another on the same plane or higher.

II

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, how wonderful are the qualities present in you! Your personality is so extraordinarily enchanting, so charming! Although you are physically absent, we can feel a continuous flow of bliss coming from you through everything related to you—your *mūrti,* your books, your tapes, your Deities, your rooms. For us you are an inexhaustible source of delight, inspiration, and safety. You are our only shelter. On the other side are the rascals, those who, upon hearing your glories, are reluctant to accept you as you are. Thus they remain perplexed, bewildered, and ill-disposed toward you.

As for myself, in my smallness, my appreciation of you is quite limited. I know practically nothing about you. Nevertheless, of one thing I am positively sure: You are the savior of this world. You came to this world with a specific purpose, and your accomplishments were impeccable and unprecedented. This world is not the same after your passage here; you have changed the face of this planet. Your Kṛṣṇa consciousness overcame all barriers of race, fanaticism, sectarianism, and provincialism, and it is irrevocably established in the core of the society for the next ninety-five centuries. This is fantastic! Fabulous! All this is due to your own merit—no doubt the reward for being the best of the devotees of the Lord.

III

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, who are you? You came here, gave Kṛṣṇa to this fallen world, and all of a sudden you left. Whoever you are, we are sure that your coming here was not imposed by karmic laws. You belong to a much more vast context, beyond the limits of the kingdom of Māyā. In the midst of the degradation of this Kali-yuga, you brought transcendental knowledge, immaculate devotion, pure life, all auspiciousness, and hope to a rotten world going inexorably down to hell. You are the hero of this Kali-yuga.

Actually, this iron age is an extraordinary one indeed. Some special and

unheard-of events have already marked this particular Kali-yuga, the twenty-eighth in the cycle of *yugas* of this day of Brahmā. Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is *svayaṁ bhagavān,* the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself, was personally here just a few days before Kali-yuga started. His sublime eternal abode, Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma,* was established right here on this earthly planet. The Vedavyāsa for this present Kali-yuga, Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana Vyāsa, excelled all other Vyāsas in previous *kalpas.* The narrator of his masterpiece, the beautiful *Bhāgavatam,* was the purest and wisest personality in the universe, Śukadeva Gosvāmī. And furthermore, later on, Kali-yuga became extremely glorious when the *yuga-avatāra,* the most munificent one, the *bhakta-rūpa,* Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, appeared in this world. How sublime is Gaura-līlā! At that point the *saṅkīrtana* era, the Golden Age, was inaugurated.

Then, to complete this succession of transcendental events, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, came to this world. You are the executor of Śrī Caitanya’s *yuga-dharma* mission. You are the inheritor of this immeasurable and invaluable spiritual legacy. You are the genuine representative of Śrī Kṛṣṇa through the glorious lineage of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* coming down from Śrīla Vyāsadeva and Śukadeva Gosvāmī. All the things you have accomplished—your successful preaching, your victorious Bṛhat Mṛdaṅga, your superb literary work, your worldwide community of devotees—are not a fortuitous achievements or automatic results from some accumulated *puṇya* or something like that. NO. Everything draws us to see you as a precise instrument in Kṛṣṇa’s hands, a strategic and infallible agent in a divine plan to promote *kṛṣṇa-prema* in modern society.

IV

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so merciful, so magnanimous. Your compassion for the people of this world has no limit. You have the *śakti* to transform lives. Look at my case: I was immersed in an ocean of misconceptions, my character marked by arrogant pseudo-intellectualism, shameless hedonism, cynical agnosticism, and an irresistible attraction for the impermanent. Out of ignorance I was creating profound and sinful *saṁskāras* in my mind. Had you not come, I would now be just one more among millions of monsters of the Kali-yuga. But under your command, this process of degradation came at once to an end.

You saved me. You literally gave me life. You gave me the eyes to see the horrible phantasmagoria that is this world. You gave me a new brain for realizing that there is no possibility at all of happiness in this world You sowed the *bhakti-latā-bīja* in my heart and introduced me to the realm of the name, form, qualities, and pastimes of the Supreme Personality o Godhead. You are my only protection and only hope.

Your eternal debtor and servant,  
Puruṣatraya Swami

## Rāma Govinda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Your Divine Grace wrote in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (9.13.11, purport), “The Supreme Personality of Godhead can be seen or unseen according to His own transcendental desire; similarly, a devotee, being *jīvan-mukta,* can be seen or not, as He chooses.”

**Twenty Years Ago**

Twenty years ago, for the first time you came to bless the Mexico City temple, to fulfill Lord Caitanya’s prediction by establishing your ISKCON mission.

Your causeless mercy has no limits. The first time I meet you, even though I was only three weeks old in ISKCON, you gave me my first initiation. In this way you granted me your eternal association.

Therefore I owe an eternal debt to you and your ISKCON mission. The only way to pay it back is to eternally serve you within ISKCON, helping you to fulfill Lord Caitanya’s prediction.

At that time I was blessed very freely with your divine association—by walking every morning with you, by going with you on all your preaching programs, and so on. I had the chance to place your shoes on your lotus feet at all the programs, and in this way I had the mercy of being very close to your lotus feet, which are the source of our inspiration.

And as our real father, you gave us ISKCON centers all over the world, a house in which to live together and serve Śrī Kṛṣṇa, in which we can hear, chant, remember, serve, worship, pray, and become His servant. Then when we become fixed in pure devotional service we can become friends with Śrī Kṛṣṇa or develop a conjugal relationship with Him.

I was dreaming of getting a good friend, a good teacher, a good guide, but by meeting you I found more than I was dreaming of, for you are a perfect and transcendental person, a real friend, a perfect teacher (*ācārya*), a real guide, a bona fide *guru*. As a perfect *ācārya,* you show how to live and how to preach. As a bona fide *guru* you give us the holy name of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the *mahā-mantra,* along with the *arca-mūrti* and service to the Vaiṣṇavas. As a real guide, you show us your life example and give us your books, tapes, and letters. And to protect us you give us your ISKCON mission.

I drive fast to the airport,  
Getting closer to the sidewalk,  
Getting late to receive you  
As devotees exclaim in loud voices,  
“Śrīla Prabhupāda is here!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda is here!”  
You had arrived in our lives.

Getting fast out of the car,  
Running behind the devotees,  
I fall flat on the sidewalk.  
You sit on your suitcase,  
Resting your chin on your cane,  
Beaming a bright and loving smile.  
I arrived late to meet you, but  
You arrived on time in my life.

You show your Vaiṣṇava compassion  
By allowing us to eat your remnants.  
We offer you a garland  
Around your neck,  
Sandalwood paste  
On your forehead.  
Let me collect eucalyptus  
Twigs for your teeth

By remembrance of your lotus feet,  
By remembrance of your life,  
By remembrance of your teachings,  
By remembrance of your preaching,  
By remembrance of whatever you wrote,  
By remembrance of whatever you say,  
All the obstacles go.

By our seeing your picture,  
By our serving your ISKCON,  
By our traveling and preaching  
All over the world,  
You are always with us.  
You are my eternal Father.  
You are my eternal Guru.  
You are my eternal Master.  
You are my eternal Friend.

You are my hero.  
You are the person  
Who I was looking for.  
You are all that I have  
In my life.

Humbly presented to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by this lowly sannyāsī,  
Rāma Govinda Swami

## Rohiṇī-kumāra Swami

Most merciful guru,  
What can I do for you?  
I pass the whole day through  
Thinking of you.  
What should I do?  
Let me serve you!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda!

I fall at your two feet,  
Admitting my defeat.  
I sit below your seat  
Serving your feet,  
Hoping to meet  
Your mercy so sweet.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda!

You opened my two eyes  
To recognize the lies  
Of foolish men’s ill tries  
To enjoy their lives,  
But the body dies  
With heart-rending cries.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda!

The soul is just a part  
Of God’s infinite heart,  
But I was not so smart.  
Your love was a dart  
That pierced my heart  
And taught me the art.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda!

When will come that great day  
When everyone will say  
Love of God is the way,  
That we can all stay  
In ecstasy’s sway?  
You showed us the way.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda!

My heart is full of fears,  
My eyes brimming with tears,  
And buzzing in my ears—  
A day seems like years  
Since you joined your peers  
In heaven’s frontiers.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda!

My heart is pained and sore.  
Alas! I have naught more  
Than memories so poor.  
I fall on the floor  
As dead as a door.  
I see you no more!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Does anybody care  
About life’s real affair?  
Or are they unaware?  
Myself, I can’t bear  
That you went elsewhere.  
I want to go there!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Now let me be quite frank:  
You are the one to thank.  
When everyone else sank  
In sin dark and dank,  
You came here to yank  
Us to the shore’s bank.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda!

I hope you will allow  
That I may serve you now.  
Although I don’t know how,  
At least I can bow  
While making a vow  
To do so somehow.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your eternal servant, humbled at the thought of your greatness and mercy,  
Rohiṇī-kumāra Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Today, on your divine appearance day, please be so kind as to accept this *guru-pūjā.* You are eternally on the other side of the ocean of birth and death, and on the other side of the river Vaitaraṇī. But I know that at the same time you are very, very close to us, watching our attempts, guarding, supporting, and helping us. That is your potency. It is due to your being intimately connected with Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

**The Preparation**

First we prepared everything for your *guru-pūjā*.

We took a fresh bath in surrendered preaching service to you. We dressed in the new cloth of following all of your instructions. Then only will you want to see us.

We have cleaned the temple of the heart by intense chanting and *hari-kathā*. And we have decorated it with tasteful arrangements of different preaching programs for your service. Today we have used the cultural preaching decoration. The different programs are like mango leaves, banana trees, and fruits which attractively decorate the temple.

Then we acquired the materials for worship and gathered them together:

Incense, ghee lamps, clear water, handkerchief, flowers, *cāmara,* and peacock fan.

Because it is Kali-yuga, the fragrant flowers in the countries—nice new *bhaktas*—were not so easy to organize; they are rare in this Northern part of the world. But today you will be amazed to find many of them. You gave us the clue how to find them: book distribution.

**The Guru-pūjā**

This *guru-pūjā* will be special. We want to be absolutely concentrated and attentive. Before starting it we have meditated deeply about our eternal relationship with you as your humble and loving eternal servants. We desire really to become free of the bodily conception of life.
