No more hankering for name and fame; envy toward others we want to exchange for selfless service and appreciation of our Godbrothers’ service.

And we know that the *kīrtana* has to be good. If I sing on my own, just thinking how I will sound, that will not be pleasing, because with such a desire I will not think of my Godbrothers. Such *kīrtana* will be a disharmonious one. You said, “Your love for me will be shown by how much you cooperate.”

Now we start:

The *pujārī* is a very dear Godbrother who knows your every desire. You turn from the Deities and take *caraṇāmrta;* then you smile at us, who are a little nervous in your presence, to encourage us …

“*Jaya* Prabhupāda! *Jaya* Prabhupāda! *Jaya* Prabhupāda! *Jaya* Prabhupāda!”

Like a swan you glide toward the *vyāsāsana* and sit down. The *pūjā* starts. Many effulgent devotees have gathered, and many offer you fragrant garlands. These are the different projects you have asked them to do. And then one devotee brings the *candana,* the golden sandalwood paste, which consists of how the devotees take responsibility in your mission to relieve you of the headache of management.

Today the devotees mercifully asked me to sing. As the familiar melody starts, I make sure to meditate on each line. As I sing, short flashes of gratefulness appear in my mind:

“The lotus feet of our spiritual master are the only way by which we can attain pure devotional service.”

*You can give us the great treasure of devotion; it cannot be had by any other means than your mercy. Please make me qualified.*

“I bow down to his lotus feet with great awe and reverence.”

*Please accept my unworthy self in the cooling dust of you lotus feet.*

“By his grace one can cross the ocean of material sufferings and obtain the mercy of Kṛṣṇa.”

*If you are satisfied, the material ocean of misery becomes as small as the water contained in the hoofprint of a calf—easy to cross over. And Kṛṣṇa shows His mercy so that “impossible” indeed becomes a word in a fool’s dictionary.*

“My only wish is to have my consciousness purified by the words emanating from his lotus mouth.”

*As soon as I am in contact with your vāṇī, life begins to fill my consciousness, and I can see clearly what to do—and also get the strength to do it.*

“Attachment to his lotus feet is the perfection that fulfills all desires.”

*Whenever I see myself as your most humble servant, things mysteriously fall into place, obstacles disappear, people we preach to and devotees cooperate.*

“He opens my darkened eyes and fills my heart with transcendental knowledge.”

*That will continuously be so … I am a great fool, so please always chastise, correct, and guide me.*

“He is my lord, birth after birth.”

*I will never leave you, and you have promised to never leave me.*

“From him ecstatic *prema* emanates.”

*Please make me more eager for this gift of prema, so that I can receive it.*

“By him ignorance is destroyed.”

*In my mind the picture of Vyāsadeva is appearing, the one in which he holds up the sunlike Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam and dissipates the darkness of this age—both externally and internally.*

“The Vedic scriptures sing of his character.”

All throughout the *Vedas,* the spiritual master is praised as the representative of God, but more specifically all statements referring to people outside India taking up the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness directly hint at you (for instance: *kirāta-huṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā—Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.4.1).

“Our spiritual master is the ocean of mercy, the friend of the poor, and the lord and master of the devotees.”

*Śrīla Prabhupāda, your words—“We have to save them”—come from real compassion. And you clearly take command of you disciple’s life: “Do as I have shown you.”*

“O master, be merciful unto me. Give me the shade of your lotus feet.”

*Although I must be a botheration for you, please do not kick me away. Please take my insignificant service and please guide me.*

“Your fame is spread all over the three worlds.”

*Certainly you are the talk of the intelligent residents of the universe.*

“I seek shelter of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are the savior of the most fallen souls.”

*Should an insignificant soul called Śacīnandana Swami be the only exception?*

Today, as a helpless beggar I am requesting you to please give me the strength to make this *guru-pūjā* a living reality every day—from now to eternity.

Your eternal servant,  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Make Me Dance**

One of my favorite lines from the few poems that Prabhupāda wrote is “Make me dance, make me dance, O Lord, make me dance as You like.” Prabhupāda declared himself a puppet in the hands of Kṛṣṇa, and he asked Kṛṣṇa to please fulfill the actual meaning of the name Bhaktivedanta. That poem wasn’t written as the musings of a reclusive scholar. It was written onboard the *Jaladuta,* a freighter with one Indian-style passenger cabin. The one passenger, A. C Bhaktivedanta Swami, had no money, yet he was striking out for America. The poem was written by a preacher who, although fearless, appeared to be momentarily overcome by his first sight of an American city. Even Lord Kṛṣṇa hesitated when He faced the Aghāsura demon. Śrīla Prabhupāda didn’t hesitate in his purpose or even waver in his determination, but he realized and admitted his helpless situation to Kṛṣṇa, his friend.

Prabhupāda was always turning to Kṛṣṇa, but it is natural for a devotee to turn even more to Kṛṣṇa when he is in a difficult situation. Nanda Mahārāja also did this. When Nanda Mahārāja was returning from Mathurā, he began to fear that something may have happened to Kṛṣṇa, his baby in Gokula, so he began to think of Kṛṣṇa very intensely and chant the holy names. Similarly, Prabhupāda’s Kṛṣṇa consciousness was intensified on that boat-crossing as he suffered heart attacks and seasickness. He expressed himself intimately to his spiritual master and to Kṛṣṇa in poems.

The cry “Please make me dance” is from the heart. Just as a poor man cries out, “Give me money,” so the preacher who is faced with the tremendous task of preaching to foreigners, to people who have demoniac habits, also cries out, “Please make me dance. Please fulfill the purport of my name, ‘devotion and knowledge.’” It took an extraordinary combination of devotion and knowledge to make even the slightest impression on these proud and dirty-minded Americans who Prabhupāda saw that day with his first glimpse of Boston, Massachusetts.

And how would he do it? Prabhupāda found the clue in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. In his poem he selected several verses describing in a systematic way the Lord’s working within the heart of every conditioned soul for his betterment. This takes place, according to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* when one hears the glories of Kṛṣṇa. The modes of nature, which grip each person’s heart, are then broken up, and liberation is possible. By recalling these verses, Prabhupāda took heart for the task of preaching in America. He gathered his determination just before officially entering the country through immigration in New York City, when the boat docked at its final destination. Although outwardly he may have appeared to be an immigrant lost in the foreignness of a new country, inwardly he had gained resolve, as expressed in his poem. He was already fully surrendered to the task and awaiting Kṛṣṇa to direct his steps.

“Make me dance!” This is not only a desperate cry; there seems to be celebration in his voice. He wants to dance in ecstasy for Kṛṣṇa! He wants to be Kṛṣṇa’s puppet. Lord Kṛṣṇa did make Prabhupāda dance in America. We saw his stately *kīrtana* steps in the storefront at 26 Second Avenue and his dancing in Tompkins Square Park, his “Swami step” before the students at Stanford University and in Golden Gate Park in San Francisco, at Ohio State University and in public halls in London . . . He danced along with the energetic steps of his disciples in the hand-to-hand rings of bounding hippies at Golden Gate Park. Actually, all the dancing during ISKCON *kīrtanas* was but a response to Prabhupāda’s hand gesture inviting them: Get up and dance!

And if we think of the word *dance* in a broader sense, as ecstatic, spontaneous celebration, as movement with rhythm and grace, then we can see that Kṛṣṇa made Prabhupāda dance in many ways. His writing is a dance of the spirit liberated from matter. His expressions were filled with light-heartedness or with the heaviness of *guru* that only a pure devotee can deliver. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja also used the image of dancing when referring to this writing. He said that his words were like dolls under the puppet master, and that they had danced to their full satisfaction throughout the chapters of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*.

It is also significant that Prabhupāda referred to his work as a “movement.” This doesn’t refer to political agitation with lobbying and marches on the Capitol; it refers to the dancing in *kīrtana* in the streets, and the movement of gathering numbers of disciples all over the world to create a favorable eddy against the current of Kali-yuga disasters. Prabhupāda’s movement is like the dance of a young cowheard boy upon the many, many hoods of a poisonous serpent.

Prabhupāda is still engaged in his movement, still dancing for you and me, and waiting for us to join the dance. To hold back during this dance means to miss the whole purpose of human existence. Prabhupāda is gesturing: “Get up and dance, just surrender to Kṛṣṇa, do as I am doing. Do the Swami step back to the spiritual world with me.” Let us take courage in Prabhupāda’s words, “If You have brought me here to dance, then please make me dance as You like.”

Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Smita Krishna Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*dharmasya tattvaṁ nihitaṁ guhāyāṁ*  
*mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthaḥ*

**“The solid truth of religious principles is hidden in the heart of an unadulterated self-realized person. Consequently, as the** ***śāstras*** **confirm, one should accept whatever progressive path the** ***mahājanas*** **advocate.”**

This is the second half of a verse Caitanya Mahāprabhu cites in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā,* Chapter 17. We are fortunate to be under the shelter of your lotus feet and thus able to accept the progressive path that you have shown us. We also know this verse:

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

You had full faith in your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī. Therefore you could do what others could not. Faith can move mountains, and by your unflinching faith in your *guru* and Kṛṣṇa you put the mountain of ignorance covering the world into motion, removing it.

Why was such faith possessed by you and not others? It is hard to say, but what is clear is that your faith manifested some remarkable miracles. You were competent to realize the true glories of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta. Thus you had a true, thorough faith. Your preaching success came at the end of your manifested pastimes in this world. In the beginning, in many eyes you were just a householder doing some business. You were not in the front line at first. But you had that faith in your *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, a faith that was so strong that as a result the tide of the world is now changing. Why do some have more faith than others? In this case you apparently were fully able to realize the true position of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, and because of his position as *mahā-bhāgavata* and yours as a worthy recipient, his *śakti* and mercy were transferred to you.

The mercy is there for everyone but some take it and some do not.

*vīta-rāga-bhaya-krodha…*

You were known as Abhaya Caraṇāravinda because you were free of fear due to your complete faith. Free from attachment, fear, and anger. Free from desire for fame, profit, and distinction, you just wanted to execute the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, and you did it. You knew that it should be done and could be done, and you did it with success beyond imagination. That was lucky for me and lucky for the whole world.

I heard one story about your time as householder in the forties. Some of your *sannyāsī* Godbrothers were visiting your house, and there was a discussion on how the prediction that Lord Caitanya’s name would be heard in every town and village could possibly come true. You said, “Some fool with implicit faith in his *guru* would accomplish it.” One of the *sannyāsīs* looked at you, and then you said to him, “Do not worry, it won’t be you.” Later that *sannyāsī* Godbrother, unaware of your preaching success, met one of your disciples somewhere on the streets of Calcutta and came to understand that you had done what you did, beyond the imagination of the other Godbrothers.

You had faith. On this auspicious day, I bow down in the dust of your lotus feet and pray that all my misgivings may be eradicated by your mercy, so that my faith in you may grow strong and one day, if Kṛṣṇa so desires, I may be able to do something that will please you in terms of devotional service and preaching to the conditioned souls. If just by your mercy I at least could chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* with full faith, then my desertlike heart would blossom with wonderful love for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. You are an ocean of mercy. Please be merciful upon me.

Your insignificant servant,  
Smita Krishna Swami

## Somaka Swami

Dear and beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I beg you to accept my most humble and respectful obeisances prostrated in the dust of your lotus feet.

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvamanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**“\[The Supreme Lord said:\] One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.”**

You are the representative of God, the Supreme Personality, the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, who, full of compassion for the conditioned souls, comes to rescue them from conditioned life. With your words and example you show the way to go back to Godhead. You are the greatest *ācārya* of modern times:

*yad yad ācarati śreṣṭhas tat tad evetaro janaḥ*  
*sa yat pramāṇaṁ kurute lokas tad anuvartate*

**“Whatever action a great man performs, common men follow. And whatever standards he sets by exemplary acts, all the world pursues” (Bg. 3.21).**

In the purport you say, “People in general always require a leader who can teach the public with practical behavior. A leader cannot teach the people to stop smoking if he himself smokes. Lord Caitanya said that a teacher should behave properly even before he begins teaching. One who teaches that way is called *ācārya,* or spiritual master” (*Bhāg.* 11.17.27).

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day I would like to thank you because you appeared in order to become our great *ācārya,* and thus you have given, along with knowledge and wisdom, your great and unforgettable example. Simply by following your instructions and your example, the whole world can be saved.

Your love for Kṛṣṇa always was completely manifested in each one of your activities from the early moments of your life. When your were small you worshiped Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and organized a Rathayātrā with the neighborhood children, just as Lord Caitanya did. Thus even in your childhood you engaged the conditioned souls in *harināma-saṅkīrtana* and the service of the Lord. Each one of your words and activities was always full of love for Kṛṣṇa, and you not only showed love for Kṛṣṇa but also love for all living entities. Your effort to free all the living beings from the claws of Māyā has no match in history. Your literary contribution is colossal, and the most wonderful thing is that you, although a giant, have presented yourself in such a simple and humble manner.

Your tireless production of literature was so intense that teams of devotees had to be engaged to keep up with you in book production. Yet you still found time to receive guests, to advise your disciples, to answer letters, and to give nectarean *darśanas.* With everyone you had a personal relationship; you never were miserly in giving yourself to others. Your love for all was amply visible. Although you acted perfectly according to etiquette, you were beyond all dictates of mundane codes of morality; instead, each of your acts and words was dictated by your intense love for Kṛṣṇa and for the conditioned living beings.

In your *pranāma mantra* it is said that you came to the West because your spiritual master ordered you to defeat the Māyāvāda philosophy. You not only were very expert in defeating philosophically the Māyāvāda philosophy, but you also defeated the Māyāvādī relationships, because your way of relating with everybody was never diplomatic, cold, or distant. Rather, your warmth and love were always perceived, and we all felt you to be our eternal father and well-wisher. Even the nondevotees always saw in you a kind well-wisher.

You also showed us how to be very simple and tolerant. I’ll always remember the days that you were so merciful by visiting us and giving us your holy and saintly association for a week in 1974, in the then recently opened temple of Rome. (We had opened it a few months before your arrival.) It was a small temple, we were few devotees, and the majority of us were new and inexperienced. The service we offered you—I lament it a lot now—was very much lower than mediocre. We gave you a room just in front of the kitchen because of the limited space and so that you would not have to climb too many stairs. How many times did my hair stand on end when the kitchen devotees would let a pot fall or bang the kettles together! What’s more, the preparations we offered you were never well prepared. In fact, they were so bad that one day you went into the kitchen and prepared the noon offering yourself, and naturally you did it noiselessly and left the kitchen as clean and shiny as a mirror—and of course the preparations were more than delicious. And to add to our clumsiness, the last day of your stay in Rome, while we were riding back from a trip, I was so clumsy that I crashed your car and you had to go back to the temple in a guest’s car while I fixed up the formalities of the accident. A few hours after this last clumsiness, we were on the stairs chanting in farewell, and you came out of your room, stopped before going down the stairs, and looked at all of us. We were all dancing, and you joined your hands together and said, “Thank you all, and excuse me for the discomforts I caused you.” You smiled as you looked at us with your glance full of compassion and mercy.

It’s so marvelous that when you were present one could constantly feel your great spiritual size and weight, because you are a spiritual giant, a colossus, but no one ever felt drowned or crushed or uncomfortable. Everyone felt spiritually uplifted, whether he was a devotee or a nondevotee, noble or plebeian, friend or enemy. Therefore, when I remember you, I always think of a verse of the *Ṣaḍ-gosvāmy-aṣṭaka: dhīrādhīra-jana-priyau priya-karau.* “The six Gosvāmīs were dear to both devotees and ruffians.”

On this auspicious day, I thank you for creating ISKCON. I also thank you for staying with us in your unmanifested form to pilot this transcendental boat of ISKCON, because the day of your disappearance Māyā attacked us very strongly, and with every attack of Māyā the ship was shaken, and it looked as if it were going to sink. But you always piloted the ship and inspired us so that we would get through the storm, and by your mercy we are still afloat, although we have some scars to remind us of the wounds due to the shaking of the boat.

On this day I ask, O beloved father and master, that you bestow your mercy on us and help us develop a fragment of your transcendental qualities, that you help us become simple, humble, and free from desires for false prestige, glory, and worship, that you help us develop truly loving relationships that go further than the formalities and norms, that your mystic power of being heavy without being overbearing may fill us up with the desire to save all living entities while remaining humble and detached, and particularly that you never reject me although I am such an unworthy servant.

By your books and your instructions I can feel your presence, but I always feel the separation from your person, from seeing your fast and light walk, from witnessing you defeat all the materialistic and atheistic arguments, from experiencing your very heavy and at the same time sweet and light personality, from seeing your contagious smile and your glance full of compassion and mercy, from hearing your voice: “Simply chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy; thank you very much.” ISKCON is you and you are ISKCON, and with our immaturity we were shaking your boat and sometimes we still shake it. Therefore I conclude this humble attempt at glorification on your transcendental appearance day by joining my hands in *pranāmas* and saying. “Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and forgive me for the shakes.”

Your insignificant servant,  
Somaka Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

All glories to Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet. In the *Hari-bhakti-sudhodaya* (13.2) it is said:

*akṣṇoḥ phalaṁ tvādṛśa-darśanaṁ hi*  
*tanoḥ phalaṁ tvādṛśa-gātra-saṅgaḥ*  
*jihvā-phalaṁ tvādṛśa-kīrtanaṁ hi*  
*sudurlabhā bhāgavatā hi loke*

**“O Vaiṣṇava! To see you is the perfection of the eyes. To touch your holy feet is the perfection of the body. To vibrate your holy qualities is the perfection of the tongue, for it is very rare to find a pure devotee within this world.”**

You are such a pure devotee, a *mahā-bhāgavata,* that is so rarely to be found. But foolish as we were, we didn’t realize just how fortunate we were to have had an opportunity to see you and touch your lotus feet and eat the remnants of *prasādam* left by you. This was your causeless mercy: even though we were unqualified, still you gave us this rare opportunity, and for that we are eternally benefited.

For in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.10.41 it is stated:

*sādhūnāṁ sama-cittānāṁ sutarāṁ mat-kṛtātmanām*  
*darśanān no bhaved bandhaḥ puṁso ‘kṣṇoḥ savitur yathā*

**“When one is face to face with the sun, there is no longer darkness for one’s eyes. Similarly, when one is face to face with a** ***sādhu,*** **a devotee who is fully determined and surrendered to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, one will no longer be subject to material bondage.”**

This point is confirmed in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 22.54):

*‘sādhu-saṅga’, ‘sādhu-saṅga’—sarva-śāstre kaya*  
*lava-mātra sādhu-saṅge sarva-siddhi haya.*

**“The verdict of all revealed scriptures is that by even a moment’s association with a pure devotee, one can attain all success.”**

So by your mercy we have every hope of our eventual personal success in this great struggle with the Lord’s illusory energy. But how can we hope to benefit those souls who didn’t get that rare opportunity? There are so many sincere souls who have circumstantially missed your personal association. Are they deprived of your mercy? Or, in other words, how to proceed with or carry on Lord Caitanya’s movement when we don’t find any such rare, pure devotee in this world?

Fortunately, you foresaw this eventuality and prepared us for it. The lesson from the history of the Gauḍīya Math after the passing of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura was never lost on you. I personally observed your deep concern about this problem of succession from as early as 1969. You carefully created and spent much time and energy developing a workable Governing Body Commission. And in your last will you made it clear that ISKCON, under the authority of the GBC, was how you wanted to extend your mercy to the vast number of sincere souls who would be coming in the future. Can anyone doubt this? Isn’t this the simple truth? So let us therefore take this opportunity of your Vyāsa-pūjā day to reaffirm our loyalty to your ISKCON Society and to redouble our effort to see that the standard of this Society, as given to us by you, remains intact.

Your hopeful servant,  
Trivikrama Swami

## Umāpati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

This morning started out like an ordinary morning. I mixed some *tilaka* and water in my left hand, the way Śrīla Prabhupāda showed me many years ago. I dipped the fourth finger of my right hand in the mixture and started to put it on my forehead, when a sudden thought brought me back to 26 Second Avenue. I was still putting on *tilaka,* but I remembered how Śrīla Prabhupāda used to help me with it.

Putting on tilaka is a kind of worship: decorating the body with the holy names of the Lord. But I—and many others, I’m sure—tend to rush through it. I dab a few marks on my body, mumble a few prayers, and finish getting dressed.

But putting on *tilaka* was special this morning. Suddenly, I felt the touch of Prabhupāda’s finger on my forehead and saw his face looking at me intently.

How can I describe the touch of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s finger? Most fingers just feel like fingers, but Prabhupāda’s finger on my forehead was different. I could feel delicate flesh covering a firm bone. The touch was gentle, but purposeful and strong.

Putting on *tilaka* was an unfamiliar effort in those days, and it often turned into a mess. I remember Hayagrīva sitting there looking helpless, yellow water running through his fingers onto the floor. “Do not waste it,” Śrīla Prabhupāda said. “It is precious.”

Sometimes I would watch like a child when Śrīla Prabhupāda put on his own *tilaka*. “Do you think you could wear *tilaka* on the street?” he once asked me. I declined. I wasn’t brave enough. But a few of us would come in the evening to try out the little waterpot and the ball of *tilaka.*

I don’t remember how many times Śrīla Prabhupāda helped me put on *tilaka,* but he helped me again this morning. And once again he showed me what a fool I am. How could I think any morning is ordinary when it starts with Vaiṣṇava *tilaka*? And how could I think I have ever put on *tilaka* without his help?

“Does your spiritual master ever touch your forehead?” I would always laugh at this question and pity those poor souls looking for cheap ecstasy at the touch of a finger.

But today I learned that a true spiritual master can also touch his disciple’s forehead. It happened to me. But there was no electric thrill, no blinding flash. Just Vaiṣṇava *tilaka*.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Umāpati Swami

## Varṣāṇa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

As preparations to celebrate your appearance begin, a vivid and meaningful remembrance appears in my mind, as it has for the past twenty years:

It is Janmāṣṭamī, 1972. The hills, which no longer belong to any nation or state but to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vṛndāvana-candra and their pure devotee, seem to stretch up and out in anticipation of a joyous day. Devotees have worked day and night to make everything ready for your coming, simultaneously with our efforts to greet Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s wonderful appearance day. The sanctity that follows the advent day of Bhagavān is shared by the appearance of His pure devotee.

How appropriate that you took birth on Nandotsava, the most blessed event, for that was the day five thousand years ago when the auspicious tidings were proclaimed throughout the land: “The son of Nanda, Prince of Vraja, is born!” You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, appeared on this glorious announcement day to echo that same message of hope, faith, and joy.

On the seventy-sixth anniversary of your appearance, as we wait on the farm for your arrival, the sun rises over the reddish horizon, illuminating the beauty of the fields, forests, and pasturing grounds. You are coming from the spiritual world to personally walk and talk with us. Bhūmi and Dharma, our cow and bull, graze peacefully, confident of a lifetime of protection, basking in the warmth of the brilliant rising sun. Devotees have painted and decorated them with colorful designs and flowers and gold for their horns. They appear more happy than ever before.

The devotees gather to see you come into sight around a curve in the winding road. All burst into spontaneous *kīrtana—*jumping, dancing, clapping, chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. Everyone watches as you pass by Bhūmi and Dharma, gazing at them with affection, a huge smile brightening your face. That smile is precious, for it confirms Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure, and, seeing it, all the devotees become more ecstatic than ever. It seems as if all the darkness and evil of Kali-yuga just suddenly vanished by some extra special mercy of the Lord.

Actually, this is the fact. Your mission in this world marks the fulfillment of a longstanding prophecy and spiritual legacy. Long ago, Bhakti-devī predicted it. Not so long ago, Lord Caitanya inaugurated it. In the year of your birth, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura revived it. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī then expanded it, and you, his disciple, fulfilled it.

As we prepare to celebrate your appearance now, twenty years later, we see how things have changed. The words of Vidarbhī, the faithful disciple who laments the departure of her *guru,* become more and more meaningful since you have departed: “O best of Kings, please get up; please get up. Just see this world surrounded by water and infested with rogues and so-called kings. This world is very much afraid, and it is your duty to protect her.” And so we wait for you to arrive again around the curve in the road, though we know you still protect us.

The cow and bull, Bhūmi and Dharma, have already passed away. Perhaps they are with you again. Life on the farm changed so much that, although you had asked us to stay, we finally had to leave. Kali’s agents have captured some of our leaders. Māyā’s allurements have captured others. Some gave up your shelter, seeking something “higher.” What could be higher than to assist you in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement?

When you left this planet, history repeated itself. Having come to this world on the order of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, as His representative, you certainly established the principles of religion, which are revealed only through the *līlā* of the Lord’s pure devotee. Yet so quickly, rogues and nondevotees took advantage and immediately began to introduce unauthorized principles, disregarding or misrepresenting the lawbooks you gave to guide us for the next ten thousand years.

And yet, despite the pain of separation from you, and all its consequences, we are still feeling your guidance in a special kind of way. The dark night of our soul ended when you appeared to our vision with the torchlight of knowledge. Our attachment to this world was severed by your sharp sword of detachment. The knowledge you presented brought the best of speculators to their knees, and the books you left us have changed the course of history. You opened for us the door to the spiritual world, giving us a glimpse of sights, sounds, forms, and activities so sweet. You united even the most diverse elements under the banner of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Saṅkīrtana. You are the intercessor through whom all our inspirations come and through whom all our aspirations return to their proper source—Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. You are the commander of all the devotees.

Now that you have returned to your real home, where anything is possible and nothing is impossible, we pray for one benediction on this auspicious day of your appearance—that along with serving you and glorifying you, we can some day again see you, chanting Jaya Rādhā-Mādhava and then delivering your Bhāgavata Dharma discourses. To again see you amidst all the clamor of empires and nations, gently delivering the peace formula from the hilltop temple as Bhūmi and Dharma graze happily in the pasture below you—such would be the perfection of my eyes.

Now that the puppet master has stopped pulling the strings, our words cannot continue. And yet, we know you are still present with us—as certain as ever. Without you at the helm, we would all have perished in the ocean of material life, beset by its storms and devoured by the ferocious aquatics lurking outside your movement and shelter.

As memories of your presence flood my mind, many regrets begin to surface as well. How I wish I could have served you better. It is only by your mercy, ever present before us, that transcendental knowledge remains ever sublime and true. And thus, through your *vāṇī,* I know you are still with us. You live in your instructions, and your followers live there with you. Devotional service culminates here and now. In serving your mission, we feel your presence, no less divine through the passing of ages.

I feel your presence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and yet I feel in many ways that I am still standing at the spot on the road where I stood looking so intently, as if I could make you appear just by looking long enough. I go through all the activities each day brings, in so many different places, but my heart remains where I stood waiting for you to round the curve. It seems to me I will not move from there again until I see you before me, and when that day comes, I will serve you better than I did before.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, savior of the fallen souls, please protect us, please guide us, and never let us stray from the shelter of your lotus feet.

From the servant of your servant,  
Varṣāṇa Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

Geepers. Those sincere offerings in previous years’ Vyāsa-pūjā books are so full of devotion, analogies, allegories, and anecdotes that it makes me hide my head in shame. I could never write anything that comes close to expressing the devotional sentiments of these devotees. A Godsister from North Carolina wrote in last year’s book, “We beg that our attachment to you goes beyond affectionate sentiment. Let us be firmly attached, without a drop of deviation, to both the spirit and letter of your orders.” Wow. That’s deep. No wonder I’m useless, forever missing my days living at Bhaktivedanta Manor. What about their offering last year? “Indeed,” they wrote, “the sun never stops shining on an ISKCON temple.” Fantastic Vyāsa-pūjā offering. Who wrote it, anyway? In terms of words, service, spirit, sentiment, and firm attachment, I have nothing to compare with these devotees. Still, I have to try to offer something; otherwise people will think I blooped and Prabhupāda will be displeased with me.

Prabhupāda! In exchange for everything you gave to me, I have nothing of value to offer you. I am useless and wretched. I am poor, materially and spiritually, and my *dhotīs* have holes in them. Since I have nothing of value to offer you, please accept my head as a pillow to rest your lotus feet. If I have ever helped anyone become Kṛṣṇa conscious, trained someone in Vaiṣṇava etiquette, or initiated anyone as a disciple, I pray they may become eternal servants of your lotus feet.

As I walk through a small village in South India ten years ago, my *sannyāsa tridaṇḍa* in hand, some local residents came out in awe and forced me to sit down. Although I struggled to escape, they forcibly washed my feet and performed *ārati*. Then they inquired from me how a person born in the West like me had become such a great saint and taken to the life of a Vaiṣṇava *sannyāsī*. With astonishment in their eyes, they heard me describe how I was not unique in any way. In fact, I told them, there were thousands of devotees just like me all over the world. “How is this possible?” asked one of the local villagers. “Who is responsible for this miracle?” asked another.

My story is not unique. I am not some kind of saint who, although born in a fallen land, somehow manages to live the life of a Vaiṣṇava. My story, and the story of countless thousands of others like me, is all due to the inconceivable mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Had Prabhupāda not appeared in Calcutta in 1896, or not left Vṛndāvana and crossed the ocean by boat at the age of seventy, or had he lost hope and returned to India before establishing the first temple in New York, all of us would have dried up, shriveled, and died. If Śrīla Prabhupāda had not accepted me as his disciple, would I ever have found shelter anywhere? Would I not have passed from this life in total misery?

When you compare me to other disciples and followers, I’m not even worth mentioning. Most regrettably, I have nothing to offer except thanks and a small request. Please let me thank you, Prabhupāda. All glories to you! Now let me request that although I am unworthy, unknown, insignificant, shameless, and insincere, please cast your glance at this lowly creature and bless me one day to achieve causeless, undeviating devotion to your lotus feet, birth after birth. I know it’s not really such a small request, but what can I do? I know that Kṛṣṇa doesn’t give pure devotional service very easily, but if I get it from you, there’s some hope for me. This is my prayer. Please accept.

Your beggar disciple,  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Adelaide

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
you have given us Kṛṣṇa  
because you are His pure devotee  
and can deliver Him to the fallen,  
who, by the laws of nature, are disqualified  
from approaching His lotus feet.

The proof of your transcendental potency  
is there in your pastimes on the Bowery,  
where you succeeded in planting  
Kṛṣṇa consciousness  
in the hearts of drop-outs and drug addicts.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
you are compassion personified  
because you wanted everyone to serve Kṛṣṇa,  
even the man with the toilet paper.  
And when your disciples disappointed you,  
or even deserted you,  
you always remained their ever well-wisher,  
the open-armed father of prodigal sons and daughters.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
humility ornaments your transcendental qualities.  
You are a gentle lamb among the devotees,  
but in the jungles of Kali-yuga you are  
like Hanumān, expanding your stature  
and manifesting as a ferocious Bengali tiger.

You devour foolish Māyāvādī nonsense,  
and mental speculators all panic and flee.  
The scientists go underground like rabbits,  
and the camouflaged do-gooders change  
their colors like chameleons.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
you are the crest jewel of the brāhmaṇas.  
At the Mantra Rock Dance, in airport lounges,  
and even in a hippie camp,  
the Supreme Brahman you established.  
By tying microphones with sacred threads,  
the perfection of pure goodness you did display.

These qualities represent only a drop in the ocean of your transcendental qualities. We glorify them so that we may qualify to join the ranks of real Prabhupādānugas, some day.

Your servants in Adelaide, Australia.

## Ahmedabad

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our worthless prostrations. All glories to your divine lotus feet, which are constantly purifying different universes immersed in the most deplorable Kali-yuga atmosphere.

Just as Lord Gaurāṅga engaged the whole world in chanting the holy name of Hari under the plea of a lunar eclipse, so you appeared among the festivities of Janmāṣṭamī on Nandotsava Day—reminding us how Kṛṣṇa consciousness is joyful and easy to perform. You brought festivities into everyone’s lives. Your program is simply “chanting, dancing, and feasting.” You proudly said of your disciples, “Before they were hippies; now they are happies.”

Indeed, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the basis of all happiness (*su-sukhaṁ kartum avyayam*). Lord Caitanya told Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī, *kṛṣṇa-nāme ye ānanda-sindhu-āsvādanabrahmānanda tāre āge khātodaka-sama:* “Compared to the ocean of transcendental bliss which is tasted by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* the pleasure derived from impersonal Brahman realization is like the shallow water in a canal.”

Your auspicious appearance day is a reminder that you have extended an eternal invitation to all the fallen souls of the world to return to the kingdom of God and regain their eternal joyful life. Your appearance is the ray of hope in the otherwise dark, miserable Kali-yuga world.

To a foolish, materialistic person it may seem as though you appeared in Calcutta like any other child, grew up, earned a degree, became a manager in

Dr. Bose’s laboratory, started your own business, and at the end came to spiritual life. But an enlightened person can see that through all these seemingly ordinary activities, a powerful spiritual personality was emerging—one who would be *jagad-guru*. The activities of *nitya-siddha* devotees are all spiritual. Every moment of your life was like a stepping-stone to the ultimate victory of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Like Lord Matsya, who first appeared in a little waterpot and then grew bigger and bigger until He ultimately saved Vedic knowledge and the saintly persons from the waters of devastation, you appeared in the house of the great Vaiṣṇava Śrī Gour Mohan De and grew “bigger and bigger” until you crossed over oceans to establish your Kṛṣṇa consciousness mission.

While instructing Sanātana Gosvāmī, Lord Caitanya declared, “An actual incarnation of God never says, ‘I am God’ or ‘I am an incarnation of God.’ The great sage Vyāsadeva, knowing all, has already recorded the characteristics of the *avatāras* in the *śāstras*. . . . By two symptoms—personal characteristics and marginal characteristics—the great sages can understand an object. Bodily features, nature, and form are the personal characteristics. Knowledge of his activities provides the marginal characteristics” (*Caitanya-caritāmrta, Madhya* 20.354-57).

The same criteria apply to the *śaktyāveśa-avatāra*. Lord Caitanya says (Cc. *Madhya* 20.374), “As explained in the Eleventh Chapter of *Bhagavad-gītā,* Kṛṣṇa has spread Himself all over the universe in many personalities through specific powers known as *vibhūti*.” He also says (Cc. *Madhya* 20.368),

*śaktyāveśa dui-rūpa—’mukhya’, ‘gauṇa’ dekhi*  
*sākṣāt-śaktye ‘avatāra’, ābhāse ‘vibhūti’ likhi*

**“Empowered incarnations are of two types—primary and secondary. The primary one is directly empowered by the Supreme Personality of Godhead and is called an incarnation. The secondary one is indirectly empowered by the Supreme Personality of Godhead and is called** ***vibhūti.*****”**

From the above statements it is clear that you have been specifically empowered by Kṛṣṇa to render Him the most intimate service of spreading His mission.

Lord Caitanya told Sanātana Gosvāmī, “Your body has been offered to Me. So now it is My property. How can you take it back to commit suicide? Through your body I have to accomplish so many tasks and establish Vaiṣṇava *siddhānta*.” When Śrīla Haridāsa Ṭhākura heard this, he said, “O Sanātana, you are so fortunate. The Lord considers your body to be His personal property.”

Similarly, O Śrīla Prabhupāda, Lord Caitanya considers your body to be His personal property. Otherwise, how could you flood the world with Kṛṣṇa consciousness?

Once you were reading your *Kṛṣṇa* book when you said, “Kṛṣṇa Himself has written this book. Otherwise, how could I write such a nice book?”

How can we ever enumerate the things you have done for us? You have given us the eternal, joyful holy name, you have given us clear knowledge of the Absolute Truth—a precise spiritual peace formula. You have taught us how to lead a decent human life. Spiritual success does not come from mundane piety. Rather, it comes from the blessings of the *mahā-bhāgavata*. The symptom of receiving your blessings is proper understanding of the spiritual *siddhānta* and intense desire to preach this sublime message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

If it were not for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we would have been lost in the dense forest of material life, and worse still in the network of spiritually corrupt *apasampradāyas*. You carefully lifted us out of our ignorance and placed us at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. Even though we feel hopeless against Māyā’s insurmountable forces, new hope and inspiration have arisen in our hearts. By your mercy we may be able to cross over this ocean of birth and death.

Please accept our humble prayers on this auspicious day and keep us afloat in the boat of your transcendental lotus feet.

Your humble servants in Ahmedabad, India.

## Albettone

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

*cakhu-dān dilo jei, janme janme prabhu sei,*  
*divya-jñān hṛde prokāśito*

You have given the world many wonderful books, which are full of transcendental knowledge. By your ecstatic purports many conditioned souls are coming to your ISKCON and changing their lives. This is the only hope for human society—to be approached by a *saṅkīrtana* devotee and receive one of your books from him or her. This is your desire—that every disciple should know the art of book distribution.

*Yogaḥ karmasu kauśalam*. If you are pleased to give me your valuable blessings, I could learn this art and please you. It is not possible to satisfy Kṛṣṇa directly. But you are His pure devotee, and if you are satisfied, then Kṛṣṇa is satisfied.

Though I’m a devotee, it would be better for me to say I’m trying to be a devotee by engaging in the valuable service of *saṅkīrtana*. In the beginning I met Ādi-bhūta Prabhu. He was on the street in Munich, distributing magazines. He was dressed as a devotee, and I asked him if he could accompany me to the temple. He said he would do so after he was finished distributing. I insisted, and so he brought me to the temple. I entered and was very attracted by the cleanliness and the good scent of incense. Many walls contained large photos of you; you seemed to look at me with eternal gravity. The devotee gave me *prasādam,* and afterward I attended noon *ārati*.

From that day I have remained with your disciples. The next day the temple president told me that I had to distribute magazines. After one hour I came back frightened. Then I stayed with devotees and it was easier. Soon I was on traveling *saṅkīrtana* in a van, on the first party in Germany. From the very beginning, by the mercy of your instructions, you accompanied us in those delicate days. Those times appear like just yesterday. I was very encouraged to read your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*.

I’m feeling greatly indebted that you allowed me to enter and know ISKCON and to remain and serve you.

On this auspicious day please bless me so I may learn how to help ISKCON and Radio Kṛṣṇa Centrale; help me to give all my energy, without sparing any for sense gratification, in offering all of myself for distributing your books.

You have many disciples who are expert in serving you, such as Harikeśa Swami, who has inspired countless devotees in book distribution.

I myself am so limited and weak, but when I see the great achievements of your disciples, that makes me more hopeful and enthusiastic for fighting.

I pray to Kṛṣṇa that I can serve you more and more.

All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Mahā-patita-pāvaṇa!

Your aspiring servants in the Hare Kṛṣṇa Cultural Center, Albettone, Vicenza, Italy.  
(written by Duyama dāsa)

## Almviks Gard

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

You are the most exalted devotee because you spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the whole world. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is a river of nectar called *amṛta-dhuni*. It is said that one who does not taste that nectar is forced to drink poison. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for saving us from willingly drinking the poison of material life. On this occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we offer to you a palmful of *amṛta-dhuni* in the form of your words quoted from a conversation you had with disciples in your room in Māyāpura on February 16th, 1977. The conversation went as follows:

**Prabhupāda:** So electric is not working? Just see. In the evening, we…, people want light. No light.

**Jayapatāka:** It works the whole day. Only in the evening it goes off.

**Prabhupāda:** Whole night also. Only evening, when you require it … \[*laughter*\]. India’s material advancement is artificial. They are not fit for it. In America, so long, no electricity every day? People would become mad. There would have been a revolution. Huh? Is it not?

**Hari-śauri:** Yes. They had a blackout in New York once for an hour or so.

**Prabhupāda:** Yes, I know that. I was there at that time.

**Hari-śauri:** Oh, you were there?

**Prabhupāda:** Not hour. It was four hours. And fortunately I was not out on the street. I was in my place. That accident took place just after a few days of my arrival, 1965. One gentleman, he, I know, he brought me some candle. I had no candle even. Simply I was sitting in darkness. What can be done? But Kṛṣṇa sent him with some candle. Yes. \[*chuckling*\]

**Hari-śauri:** Nine months after that incident they had a record number of births…

**Prabhupāda:** Yes.

**Hari-śauri:** … in New York.

**Prabhupāda:** What can they do in darkness? \[*laughter*\] That is the only engagement in darkness.

**Jayapatāka:** They could have chanted Hare Kṛṣṇa.

**Prabhupāda:** No … even Hare Kṛṣṇa people. *Nāviviktāsano bhavet, mātrā svasrā duhitrā vā*. It is strictly forbidden: “You should not sit alone even with your mother, sister, or daughter, what to speak of wife.” *Balavān indriya-grāmo vidvāṁsam api karṣati:* “The senses are so strong, even learned persons, advanced, they also become victimized.” *Balavān indriya-grāmaḥ*. *Balavān* means very strong. When the … It is forbidden even with mother, sister, or…, and daughter. That is the only capturing instrument in the material world. *Maithunyam agāram*, prison house of sex, this material world. It is a prison house, but locked up by sex. The ordinary prison house, they are locked up by guards, and here the locking process is sex. The words are selected in the *Bhāgavata*. They are thinking free. \[*laughs*\] They are imprisoned, locked up. And everyone falsely thinking, “I am free. Our nation is free. I am free.” What nonsense free? You are locked up already. That he does not … Now this word is used, *maithunyam agāram ajñaḥ*. He has preferred to remain in this prison locked up by sex because he is rascal. *Maithunyam agāram ajñaḥ*.

**Pradyumna:** Where is that, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

**Prabhupāda:** In the… Find out, Fifth, Fifth Canto, Fifth Chapter. *Bhāgavata* is the only guide. Read *Bhāgavata,* repeatedly whole life. Safe. Always. Always, whenever there is time, read, read, read.

*anarthopaśamaṁ sākṣād bhakti-yogam adhokṣaje*  
*lokasyājānato vidvāṁś cakre sātvata-saṁhitām*

Vyāsadeva has done this *sātvata-saṁhitā. Anartha.* To live in this material world is *anartha,* unnecessary. Hm? You have got it?

**Pradyumna:**

*yadā na paśyaty ayathā guṇehaṁ svārthe pramattaḥ sahasāa vipaścit*  
*gatā-smṛtir vindati tatra tāpān āsādya maithunyam agāram ajñaḥ*

**Prabhupāda:** *Ajñaḥ*.

**Jayapatāka:** Which verse?

**Pradyumna:** It’s Canto Five, Chapter Five, verse number seven. “Even though one may be very learned and wise, he is mad if he does not understand that the endeavor for sense gratification is a useless waste of time. Being forgetful of his own interest, he tries to be happy in the material world, centering his interests around his home, which is based on sexual intercourse and which brings him all kinds of material miseries. In this way one is no better than a foolish animal.”

**Prabhupāda:** Yes. *Maithunyam agāram ajñaḥ*. The selection of words in *Bhāgavata* are, from even a literary point of view, perfect. *Padaṁ padaṁ yad vipadāṁ na teṣām. Bhavāmbudhir vatsa-padaṁ paraṁ padaṁ padaṁ padaṁ yad vipadāṁ*

*Na…* This is literary. *Samāśritā ye pada-palla-plavam.* Again … *pallavam plavam. Anuprāsa*. This is literary, *anuprāsa*. *Samāśritā ye pada-pallava-plavam mahat-padaṁ puṇya-yaśo murāreḥ. Padam. Bhavāmbudhir vatsa-padaṁ paraṁ padaṁ padaṁ yad vipadāṁ na teṣām*. Just see literary arrangement. And full of meaning. This is *Bhāgavatam*. Any way you study, from literary point of view, from knowledge, from philosophy, from social, every—perfect. Therefore *lokasyājānato vidvāṁś cakre. Vidvān*. One who has learned, Vyāsadeva, *vidvān,* the first-class learned person. The *sātvata-saṁhitām*—for the devotees, Vedic cream. *Nigama-kalpa-taror galitaṁ phalam.* Nobody can be like Vyāsadeva; he’s incarnation of Nārāyaṇa. Where is such scholar throughout the whole world? Is there?

**Hari-śauri:** That man from Delhi said that he thought you were an incarnation of Vyāsadeva.

**Prabhupāda:** Yes. Any devotee in literary career, he is to be understood… Just like our Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura. He is described as incarnation of Vyāsadeva because Vyāsadeva wrote *Bhāgavatam* and he wrote *Caitanya-bhāgavata*.

**Pradyumna:** Vyāsa-pūjā.

**Prabhupāda:** Vyāsa-pūjā. *Vyāsa-prasādam.* Unless one is blessed by Vyāsadeva, he cannot write transcendental literature.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for purifying us with the nectar of *amṛta-dhuni*.  
(written by Yogīndra dāsa)

Your servants in Almviks Gārd, Sweden.

## Argatala

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In His teachings to Śrīla Rūpa Goswami (Cc. *Madhya* 19.151), Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu said:

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

By the mercy of the *guru* and Lord Kṛṣṇa, one can obtain the seed of *bhakti*. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the most merciful personality. It is only by your unlimited blessings that we can receive a glimpse of devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Without your blessings we would have still been wandering in this cosmos, not knowing the purpose of life, like the clouds of the autumn season. Our humble prayer is that somehow or other we can follow the instructions and footsteps of Your Divine Grace.

Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura sings beautifully:

*tāṇḍera caraṇa sevī bhakti sane vāsa janame janame hāya, ei abhilāśa*

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please shower your divine blessings so that we can worship your divine lotus feet birth after birth.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

The devotees of ISKCON Agartala, India.

## Auckland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate giriṁ*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master. By his mercy a lame man can cross over mountains and a dumb man can compose eloquent poetry.”**

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You instructed everyone to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy, so we are chanting and becoming more and more happy. Because you have definitely solved all our problems, we aspire to please you in any way we can. This is our desire, and if we are successful, then that alone will be the perfection of our existence, now and forevermore.

However, we find many seemingly impassable obstacles, such as our own selfishness, our false egos, our attachments—unlimited, immovable obstructions. When we experience your tolerance of these lowly traits in us, we are surprised and profoundly moved. Never have we known such depth of compassion and character! Yet even though we experience the pleasure and realization of connecting with Kṛṣṇa by following your instructions (our minds and senses are satisfied and conquered), still we don’t surrender . . . but do you reject us? NO, your tolerance is real, unwavering, and unfathomable! So, our steel-framed hearts are softened again by your glance.

In you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we find all good qualities; so much so that every year new books are written describing your effulgent characteristics; so much so that today hundreds and thousands of transcendentalists all over the planet have gathered simply to hear and chant about you in great ecstasy. Such oceanic greatness is found only in genuine *sādhus,* or saints, whom the Lord chooses to send to bring His wayward, suffering devotees back to Him. Their transcendental qualities are described throughout the scriptures. For example:

*titikṣavaḥ kāruṇikāḥ suhṛdaḥ sarva-dehinām*  
*ajāta-śatravaḥ śāntāḥ sādhavaḥ sādhu-bhūṣaṇāḥ*

**“The symptoms of a** ***sādhu*** **are that he is tolerant, merciful, and friendly to all living entities. He has no enemies, he is peaceful, he abides by the scriptures, and all his characteristics are sublime” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **3.25.21).**

In the purport you inspire our understanding: “A *sādhu,* as described above, is a devotee of the Lord. His concern, therefore, is to enlighten people in devotional service to the Lord. That is his mercy. He knows that without devotional service to the Lord, human life is spoiled. A devotee travels all over the country, door to door, preaching, ‘Be Kṛṣṇa conscious. Be a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Don’t spoil your life in simply fulfilling your animal propensities. Human life is meant for self-realization, or Kṛṣṇa consciousness.’ These are the preachings of a *sādhu.* He is not satisfied with his own liberation. He always thinks about others.”

As I write this, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I think about you and feel grateful for your love, and I feel inspired to become at least a little like you. As I struggle to engage my lust-ridden intelligence in glorifying you, the great *mahā-bhāgavata* devotee of the Supreme Lord, I derive great inspiration to apply your instructions in my daily life more seriously, and I also realize some of your greatness! Such worship of the pure devotee is more pleasing to Kṛṣṇa than direct service to Him.

You continue your purport: “\[The *sādhu*\] is the most compassionate personality toward all the fallen souls. One of his qualifications, therefore, is *kāruṇika,* great mercy to the fallen souls.… The *sādhu* has the thankless task of impressing upon them the importance of devotional service.… A *sādhu* behaves with all conditioned souls for their ultimate relief from material entanglement. Therefore, no one can be more friendly than a *sādhu* in relieving a conditioned soul.…”

Such qualities are also manifesting in your committed followers, even without your physical presence. Neither the power of your personality nor your pure, direct, and intimate connection with the Supreme Personality of Godhead are limited by time and space. Pure devotees of Kṛṣṇa live always in sound, and the results of applying their instructions is living proof of their mystic, nonmaterial potency! Perfected *aṣṭāṅga-yogīs* may expand themselves nine times, but *bhakti-yoga* is the highest rung on the *yoga* ladder, so the unalloyed *bhakta* (devotee) expands himself unlimitedly through sound vibration. Therefore we are feeling your presence (in separation) now as much or more than before, both in the association of devotees who have taken your instructions seriously, and in our own connection with you through the same medium of sound.

It is said that the pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa has all mystic powers, and your continued presence with us in your ISKCON organization is a clear example. Many people ask us, “Who is the new leader of your Hare Kṛṣṇa movement?” “Who will take over from Bhaktivedanta Swami?” But your true followers don’t feel that you have left them, and in fact you haven’t. Your potency remains, undiminished—you are a spiritual person, not an ordinary man—and in fact the influence of your Kṛṣṇa consciousness, your pure love for Kṛṣṇa, is spreading, spiritualizing the whole planet, more and more through your various expansions!

Therefore others are being charmed by your touchstonelike personality through your dedicated followers. They are becoming attracted to Kṛṣṇa, they are experiencing the subsidence of the fire of lust and pain in their own hearts and lives, and the desire to serve God, *guru,* and the devotees is awakening! Here in New Zealand, whether it be the mayors of the towns where your Padayātrā travels, or the favorable businessmen on the city streets who happily accept books and give us donations, or the inmates of a local prison near New Varṣāṇa (one has become a devotee), the result of association with you is manifesting. People’s hearts are changing, melting! The proof of your purity, your mystic power, is there in the results.

A tree is known by its fruits. The branches, twigs, flowers, and fruits of the “Bhaktivedanta tree” are spreading the sweet scent of your merciful lotus feet to every nook and corner, reviving the sleeping souls and attracting them back home, back to Godhead! Please bless us once again that we remain connected (strongly attached) to your ISKCON tree and become pure in representing Your Divine Grace.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your aspiring servants in Auckland, New Zealand.  
(written by Ātmānanda dāsa)

## Austin

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet.

On August 12, 1971, you gave some of your disciples this instruction: “Make our Austin center a grand success.” You further instructed on February 4, 1972, “So because there is such good possibility for preaching, especially in the university, then I am recommending that some men must remain there and work very hard to get a permanent center.” So now here it is two decades later and there are six of your followers here who are hoping that we can please you by fulfilling your desire for an Austin center.

You have so kindly bestowed upon us the opportunity to preach to the conditioned souls here in Austin. There is a major university here, the University of Texas, with almost 50,000 students, and an Indian community of 300 families. This is also the state capital of Texas, one of the most important states in America. So the prospects here for doing some tangible preaching work are very bright.

Even though we are most unqualified, by your grace we are seeing a gradual, steady increase in the preaching results—in our weekly campus *harināma* program, in our weekly Love Feast program, and in our own Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We are begging your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we can continue on in this way, progressing more and more, day by day.

We are now sincerely trying to carry out your order. We know that with the order of the spiritual master comes the potency to carry it out. Therefore we know that it is going to happen. There will unfold a grandly successful ISKCON center here in Austin. Our desire is simply that we can be your instruments to assist you in bringing it about.

We are simply falling down in the dust of your lotus feet and begging your mercy again and again and again. Although we are most unqualified, you have brought us all here. We are helpless, but your instructions have the full power of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa behind them: *mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim / yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam:* “I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of the fallen souls. His mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.” Therefore we know that even though we are incompetent, it is possible that by your blessings we can accomplish wonderful things here on your behalf. You are simply wonderful, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And you have given us this simply wonderful instruction, by which we can become simply wonderful and make all the inhabitants of Austin also simply wonderful.

You are the puppeteer, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Therefore we beg of you to please make us dance, make us dance, make us dance as you wish to make your Austin center a grand success.

Your insignificant servants striving to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Austin, Texas.  
(written by Saṅkarṣaṇa dāsa)

## Barcelona

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Today is the anniversary of your auspicious appearance on this planet, and therefore it is a special day for praising your transcendental qualities.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day we think of you with fond devotion, cook huge quantities of foodstuffs for you, offer homages unto you, and dance before you in *kīrtana*.

Also, today is the day when we most intensely meditate on your sweet voice, your smile, your gravity, and the enthusiasm you are forever giving us to enable us to stay fixed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and continue on the path of perfection.

When our meditation on you increases, our respect and love for you also increase, and our hearts swell with intensified love for you.

And so this day is also a great opportunity to remember Your Divine Grace’s instructions, which are, without the least doubt, the most important factor in the life of any aspiring devotee in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

Our debt to you is very, very great, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We were all in illusion due to the influence of this mundane world; we were so attached to our so-called enjoyment. We were completely infected with the disease of illusion, like the worst of lepers. The *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* describes the great devotee Vāsudeva Vipra, who was cured of leprosy by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. The only thing the Lord wanted in return was that he preach the instructions of Kṛṣṇa and free all human beings from birth and death.

And so, by this one story we can learn that the only real way we can attempt to repay the debt we all owe you is by preaching this wonderful philosophy to the masses of people.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, this special favor of yours upon us—your instruction to push on the preaching of this movement—is hereditary, and so in this way throughout all time the whole planet will feel love and affection toward you, for you brought us all the holy name of Lord Hari:

*mahāprabho kīrtana-nṛtya-gīta-vāditra-mādyan-manaso rasena*  
*romāñca-kampāśru-taraṅga-bhājo vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Taking shelter at your lotus feet, we beg to remain  
Your servants in Barcelona, Spain.  
(written by Sādhya-sreṣṭha dāsa)

## Baroda

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this, your holy Vyāsa-pūjā day, we pray to you to please grant us the following benedictions:

Please bless us so that we may ever remain strong in our daily *sādhana-bhakti*. You told us that our *sādhana*—chanting sixteen rounds of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* on beads and attending *maṅgala-ārati, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, and *guru-pūjā—*would give us spiritual strength. You said that this was the difference between our movement and that of other so-called spiritualists.

Please bless us that we remain strong in this *sādhana* and not cheaply consider ourselves advanced devotees who are beyond these important instructions of yours. In one of your poems you prayed to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Lord Kṛṣṇa, saying that you were a fallen soul and were facing crores of rebirths in this material world. This is your ideal example of humility. Kindly bless us with just a drop of such humility, so that we do not become victims of Māyā through her *ahaṅkāra* feature.

Please bless us that we may continue to have the association of devotees who have captured your mood of constant preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Let us not think that being a *bhajanānandī* is a higher engagement than preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Please help us guard against becoming like the so-called advanced devotee whom Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura described in one of his poems.

Please bless us to become free from faultfinding, which is a diversion from pure devotional service. May we constantly remember this statement of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s: “When I am so full of faults, how can I see the faults in others?”

Please bless us to be free from “groupism,” which is based on mundane considerations only, not on the topmost vision of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who sees all living entities as potential devotees and blesses them with *śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtana.* No one is outside the rays of this all-merciful benediction moon. Please bless us so that we may never be carried away by our so-called qualifications and in this way destroy the unity of this all-important *saṅkīrtana* movement.

*Yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādaḥ.* Please, please bestow your mercy on us unfortunate souls who have fallen into this ocean of sense gratification and repeated birth and death. Your lotus feet are our only hope.

Your humble servants in Baroda, India  
(written by Vāsu Ghoṣa dāsa)

## Belfast

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

At the moment there is a government election taking place here. All the political parties have presented their various manifestos for the future of the country. Politics here in Northern Ireland is much different from that on the British mainland, in as much as the main issue concerning the voting public here is how to bring about peace. Everyone talks about the violence of the last twenty years.

True, it has been particularly violent, but nobody seems able to come up with a proper solution. Quite often God is talked about—churches organize mass prayer days and try to help relieve the pain and suffering—but to no real avail. The terror groups always revert to the gun and the bomb.

You have explained to us on many occasions how the laws of *karma* work. Someone’s being killed is a direct result of the victim’s taking another’s life in the past, and so people must suffer continuous death and disruption. So many animals are being slaughtered for economic development, especially cows: Ireland is trying to be the number one supplier of cow flesh to the rest of Europe. No one seems particularly concerned or overly perturbed at the thought of all the innocent cows being sent to slaughter, despite the fact that the cow also has its parents who are weeping at the loss of their offspring. Humans so foolishly think that only they can feel loss, not considering the great loss endured by other sentient beings.

People often turn toward religion when they are feeling the need for their spirits to be elevated, during a troubled period, or if suffering undue poverty, but only rarely to seek knowledge or love of God. Strangely enough, much of the population here practice some form of religion quite ardently, yet still they lack real compassion.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, while you were with us you taught with compassion, treated all of us with immeasurable kindness, and gave every moment of your time for the upliftment of mankind. You wanted to see us transcend the clinging grip of the three modes of material energy and overcome the lower animal natures we were born with. You wished to see us freed from hatred, envy, and lust, the very forces that are driving the conditioned souls toward their destruction.

You have given a manifesto for peace in your purport to the *Bhagavad-gītā* verse beginning *bhoktāraṁ yajña-tapasām:* “\[People are\] trying to be lords of all they survey, but actually they are dominated by the material energy of the Lord.

. . . One can attain perfect peace only in complete Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

This is what you have taught us and left for the world to see in the form of your transcendental books. Within the pages of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Bhagavad-gītā, The Nectar of Devotion, Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* and all the other books you produced during your short time with us on this planet can be found full instruction on how to attain the everlasting peace that so many of us claim to hanker after.

If only we could develop the slightest fraction of the faith in Kṛṣṇa consciousness that you possessed, we might well be able to begin putting things right in this greatly troubled place. But first we have to learn to appreciate your mercy and kindness toward us. Somehow we, your most undeserving followers, must appreciate fully your message of peace and devotion to Kṛṣṇa that you have left with us. Then maybe we will be able to spread a little of that light and comfort of Kṛṣṇa consciousness around to our fellow man.

Begging for your mercy in the form of a particle of such faith, and praying to remain engaged in your service, we remain  
Your servants in Belfast, Northern Ireland.

## Belgrade

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
All glories to the activities you performed, and  
All glories to your transcendental books!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

As it’s impossible to repay one’s debt to the spiritual master, so it’s impossible to glorify the spiritual master enough. It’s really not possible to do so, because his glories are unlimited. Therefore, in this offering we can only briefly mention some of your glories. The only way we can get your mercy is through your disciples who have sincerely carried out your orders and divine instructions in your books.

We are all your grand-disciples here, and our main handicap is that we don’t have any of your direct disciples to be permanently present here to always remind us of what you are and what our debt to you is. It would probably be very difficult to completely understand your transcendental greatness as a pure devotee if you had not written your transcendental books, which perfectly explain all the knowledge that is needed to become a lover of Kṛṣṇa. Therefore, it is our great good fortune that we can read your books and in this way understand all about you. From your books we can understand what you wanted us to do. So, even though not physically present, you are definitely fully present in your books. Otherwise, how would it be possible that your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement continues to spread all over the world, even after your departure? That it is spreading confirms the fact that the pure devotee is not different from what he speaks. Your divine words can induce everyone to begin his devotional service, and after this they can unlimitedly inspire the devotee who reads them with all attention and devotion.

It is your unlimited mercy upon all the conditioned souls that you wrote these books and that you acted all the time according to the teachings that you have given in your books, establishing yourself as the topmost *paramahaṁsa* devotee of the Lord. Your books, which this offering is glorifying, are not different from you. Your glories remain to be eternally chanted throughout the three worlds.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and to your divine books, which speak only about Lord Kṛṣṇa and how to serve and love Him!

We are a young *yātrā* developing into a more organized center. At the same time, Māyā is always there to stop all those who are not sincerely engaging in Kṛṣṇa conscious activities. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us never forget the instructions you have given us in your books. Don’t let sentimentality water down the clear meaning you have given. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, allow us to eternally serve your lotus feet and the feet of our spiritual masters and in this way remain fixed in the mission of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya by distributing your books, preaching the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, chanting the holy name, distributing *prasādam,* and in all respects following in your footsteps.

Your servants in Belgrade, Yugoslavia.

## Bhayander

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Your appearance day is here,  
When you are especially remembered,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

We remember, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
that you are nondifferent from the Lord,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

You appeared in the material world  
the day after Kṛṣṇa’s appearance day,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

In your absence the world is crying;  
Your instructions we are following,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

Before you our life was wasted—  
No devotion, no faith, reality untasted,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

No faith, no direction,  
Unsteady mind, only confusion,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

We try to remember your instructions,  
O eternal guru,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

By your divine grace  
The darkness of ignorance disappears,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

Please make us follow your instructions.  
Give us this benediction.  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

Our life, our heart and soul,  
You are our only goal,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

Our enthusiasm to glorify you,  
Our eternal guru, only increases,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

O universal teacher,  
Please give us your shelter,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

We have only our service to offer,  
With our minds, bodies, and words,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

There is no greater pleasure than  
Remembering you on your appearance day,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

When you are remembered,  
All things become possible,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

From our eyes tears are coming  
As we glorify you,  
O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our beloved master.

Your servants in Bhayandar, India.

## Bhuvaneswar

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Kindly accept our prostrated *daṇḍavat pranāms* at your lotus feet on this auspicious occasion, when your innumerable devotees are glorifying Your Divine Grace. We, some of your sons and grandsons, are also trying to join our voice with those of your elevated devotees.

The last year, 1991, was a glorious year for the devotees of ISKCON in general and the people of Orissa in particular. Why? Because last year saw an ISKCON temple added to the list of the temples in this temple city, Bhuvaneswar. Your long-cherished desire was fulfilled fifteen years after you had laid the cornerstone of the Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Temple here at Bhuvaneswar.

In the year 1977, when Your Divine Grace was present at the Bhuvaneswar, one of your lady disciples asked you when you would be coming again to Bhuvaneswar.
