There is even one full-time devotee of the *saṅkīrtana* movement in Tunisia! When she sees your filmed biography, *Your Ever Well-Wisher,* she cries out of happiness. In Morocco people like *bhajana* and *prasādam*. In Algeria people like to read the philosophy. I can see the future: Because Your Divine Grace desired that the Arabic countries take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, Kṛṣṇa will fulfill this desire. It is just a question of time.

May your mercy touch all conditioned souls. May your smile attract all attention. May your books go to every home. May your temples be a shelter to all of us. May your wishes be fulfilled. May your mercy touch a conditioned soul like myself.

Your humble servants in the Arabic countries.  
(written by Gaura-bhakta dāsa)

## Australian Food for Life

Please accept our most respectful obeisances.

All glories to your Vyāsa-pūjā celebrations!  
All glories to your disciples!

Please engage us in your missionary activities, especially your Food for Life mission, which you indicated was your “secret weapon.”

During an evening *darśana* in Māyāpur on the 15th of February, 1977, the following conversation took place:

**Prabhupāda:** Anyone who comes to see the *ārati,* at least two *purīs,* little vegetable, *halavā* should be given. *Prasādam* distribution should go everywhere. Who said that this is my trick? Who was…?

**Hari Śauri:** He was just saying last night.

**Prabhupāda:** Oh, somebody…

**Hari Śauri:** Secret weapon.

**Prabhupāda:** \[*laughs*\] Yes.

**Tamāl Krishna Goswami:** Secret weapon, yes.

Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are also seeing this *prasādam* distribution as your secret weapon. Trying to firmly establish our Food for Life service within this meat-eating culture of Australia is not an easy task. However, by your prayers and blessings we are making substantial progress, not only in the matter of increasing public acceptance of our devotees, but also in improving our own attitudes as well.

You have kindly asked that we all cooperate among ourselves after your departure. And for the most part we have. However, what about the billions of other Kṛṣṇa devotees out there covered by illusion? Aren’t we supposed to cooperate with them also?

Unfortunately, in some ways we have failed in this endeavor. Consequently, we are now suffering a heavy dose of misunderstanding from these potential Kṛṣṇa devotees. We have only ourselves to blame, for you never told us to work against the grain. Rather you encouraged us to develop “lazy intelligence.” You told us to imitate the snake, who eats the field mouse and takes over its home. You encouraged us to similarly “enter like the snake,” very sneakily, and then utilize what has already been created by the materialists. Not that we try to dig our own hole and waste valuable time.

Similarly, we believe that your Food for Life mission can act very much like the intelligent snake. By working co-operatively with other established organizations, we are increasing ISKCON’s credibility, and not only that, but amazing facilities and opportunities are being provided simply because of this co-operative spirit.

We need not compromise our principles of purity in order to do this. All that is required is a broad preaching vision, just as you so often displayed. For example, I remember Hari Śauri Prabhu telling me once how you suggested we sell statues of Gaura-Nitāi to the common people and explain to them that if they waved incense in front of these statues, they would get good luck!

Now that’s definitely a broad preaching vision.

Similarly, Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī has defined *yukta-vairāgya* as using everything in Kṛṣṇa’s service that can be used, without being attached to such things. We must learn the art of knowing how to engage everything, every situation, and every person in Kṛṣṇa’s service. That was your mood, Śrīla Prabhupāda. “Somehow or other…” you were very fond of saying. “Somehow or other spread this *saṅkīrtana* movement.”

In your Food for Life mission we are witnessing this pure desire manifesting. Indeed, this “secret weapon” of *prasādam* has the ability to capture the world by the tongue and the belly!

Finally, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mercy is all that we, your uncultured servants of the Australian *yātrā*, are made of. Without your broad preaching vision, compassion, and determination, the *saṅkīrtana* movement would never have made its way to these lonely shores. Please help us imbibe your mood of preaching to the fallen. Please train us to be your representatives so that we can factually help these souls. And most of all, please give us shelter under the lotus feet of your servants, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for this is the only real refuge within this horrible material world.

Your Food for Life top secret agents in Australia.  
(written by Priyavrata dāsa)

## Back to Godhead Magazine

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories unto you, who are the only sure shelter and means of deliverance for all souls suffering the pangs of material, conditioned life in the present Age of Kali.

Since you are a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa, your desire to please Him knows no bounds. Just to please Him you accepted as your life’s mission the task of sending the fallen conditioned souls of Kali-yuga back home, back to Godhead.

From the First Canto of your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* we learn that Śrīla Vyāsadeva foresaw that the people of this age would be impatient due to lack of goodness. Yet somehow, by your mercy, you induced these unfortunate souls to buy and read your books and this *Back to Godhead* magazine.

You constantly stressed the importance of BTG as a key component of your mercy plan to bless the spiritually needy. The following quotes support this statement:

You should take BTG as your life and soul. Your work for BTG is first and foremost above all. If you do not find any time for other things, there is no objection, but I want to see that you make BTG a successful magazine like *Life* magazine or *Illustrated Weekly of India*. \[Letter to Rāyarāma dāsa, March 23, 1968\]

We want that BTG be printed in every major language and distributed all over the world, so that there is no language barrier in taking to our movement. \[Letter to Citsukhānanda dāsa, March 25, 1971\]

So the challenge to us, the staff and advisers of BTG, is to use all resources at our disposal—computerized translation, direct-to-plate technology, simultaneous satellite-to-plant transmission, multi-point printing and distribution … every available technology!—in making your vision a reality.

By your mercy you’ve engaged us to assist you. We beg for your mercy so that we may remain thus engaged—remain a part of the solution, Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and never again become a part of the problem.

Millions of your books and BTG’s have penetrated the darkness of Kali-yuga, eternally benefiting those who have come in contact with them. This is not surprising, because they are your mercy, and your mercy knows no limits.

Your servants at Back to Godhead magazine.  
(written by Arcita dāsa)

## Back to Godhead Magazine (Italian)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

You are still present where there are devotees that inspire unity.

Lord Caitanya came with His associates. You also surround yourself with other Vaiṣṇavas, your disciples.

Being together as devotees is possible only when we see each other first as devotees of the Lord, and then as men, women, husbands, wives, presidents, *pujārīs,* etc.

And for keeping this vision one has to be in your close association by satisfying your greatest desire: preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness together with other Vaiṣṇavas.

Let us be engaged in any kind of activity that promotes attraction for Śrī Kṛṣṇa, cooperating with other Vaiṣṇava preachers of your family, ISKCON, and in this way making our lives meaningful.

And even more, as we watch the disintegrating forces of Kali-yuga tearing down nations, families, alliances, and peaceful living conditions, let us be unifying factors within the family of your devotees. This, we think, will be our best service to Your Divine Grace.

Your humble servants at the Italian BTG, Milan, Italy.  
(written by Citraka dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Our most humble obeisances to you, our revered spiritual master. You make all things favorable for Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and you are delivering Kṛṣṇa to all. This world is eternally bound up in the blindness of material illusion, but by your mercy even the most fallen souls can glimpse the spiritual world through the books that you have written, through the Deities and the temples that you and your ISKCON society have established, and through the *harināma* chanting parties that go out daily all over this planet under your direction.

Your eminence in the ranks of Vaiṣṇavas and in the history of Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavism is inconceivable to us. But we do know that since the time of Mahārāja Parīkṣit, you are the first in this *yuga* to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. You have fulfilled the prophecy of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura that a great personality would come to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. What’s more, you have fulfilled the prophecy of Lord Caitanya Himself:

*prthivīte āche yata nagarādi-grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

**“In every town and village My holy name will be preached.”**

We can only stand in awe of these accomplishments. Kṛṣṇa has engaged you for these transcendental services. Outwardly there is no explanation for your success. In 1965 the mundane view would have seen only an elderly man with no money, no power, no prestige, no followers, and no organization. However, your devotion to Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa’s mercy upon you have confounded these mundane views and spread the light of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every corner of this planet.

On this auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day we honor you through whatever humble means are at our disposal, and we pray that we can continue to receive your mercy in the form of service to you and your ISKCON society and the preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Archives.

## Bhaktivedanta Institute

They teach us all in school today  
How man evolved from dirt and clay.

From a big explosion at the beginning of time,  
All the chemicals combining to form a slime.

“We have all the answers,” the scientists declare,  
“We need no religion; for God we don’t care.”

And so …

Born into ignorance, each child in the dark,  
They feed him on theories of lepton and quark.

And like a fish out of water drowning in air,  
The poor soul is crying in the depths of despair.

“What about me?” cries the bird in the cage.  
This temporary body is doomed to old age.

“There’s no life after death, no ‘ghost in the machine,’  
No soul to be touched, or heard, or seen.”

So they have spoken; the scientific decree  
Their experiments have proven, and we must agree.

What misfortune was ours, for we too were misled!  
All atheistic theories and nonsense in the head.

We took so many classes to achieve our foolish goal,  
But the lesson we missed was the science of the soul.

No power on earth this illusion could end  
till Your Divine Grace, Kṛṣṇa did send.

You cut through their lies, your words like a knife,  
To establish the reality, that life comes from life.

You asked us, “What use is your so-called Ph.D.,  
If silence you keep when all nonsense you see?

Scientific dogma is what you must expose.  
Preach *bhāgavat-dharma* till everybody knows.”

Your soldiers you took from the enemy’s camp,  
And now you deploy them in Śrī Caitanya’s ranks.

Now we’re scientists for Kṛṣṇa, by name the B.I.,  
Trying to convince them that there’s a spiritual science.

Your order is clear: their theories we must defeat.  
For success we take shelter of your lotus feet.

If it weren’t for your mercy, O master divine,  
We’d have spoiled our lives, thinking I, me, and mine.

Now you’ve given us a chance to achieve our perfection.  
We pray for your mercy and beg for protection.

Your glories will expand without any limit.  
To your lotus feet we must humbly submit.

In fighting illusion you never did tire.  
To remain as your servants is our only desire.

O master, to please you will make us all glad.  
Please accept our small offering, Śrīla Prabhupada.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute in Bombay and San Francisco.

## Bhaktivedanta School for Sanskrit Studies

*anyābhilāṣita-śūnyaṁ jñāna-karmādy-anāvṛtam*  
*ānukūlyena kṛṣṇānuśīlanaṁ bhaktir ucyate*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our grateful obeisances to you again and again because you are the most perfect devotee of the Supreme Lord. As Rūpa Gosvāmī stipulates in his definition, *bhakti* is regular and appropriate service to Lord Kṛṣṇa that is free from ulterior motivations and unobscured by desires for material gain or deliverance. Thousands chose to follow you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and millions more will choose to do so, simply because your purposes were so obviously pure. You came to our degraded cities, to the capitals of self-aggrandizement, not to find your own status and power but simply to carry out your service to *guru* and Gaurāṅga, as a dutiful agent claiming our allegiance to the only possessor of all status and power.

Now we can learn from your example how to practice devotional service ourselves. We should emulate you, not imitate, by trying to finally give up our foolish material motivations, this useless baggage we have been lugging around with us through life after life. We should stop striving for ego gratification and false security, thus freeing ourselves for participating wholeheartedly in your preaching mission. Please bless us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we can transfer our misdirected urges to the appropriate object of striving, the success of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement all over the world.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta School for Sanskrit Studies.  
(written by Gopīparāṇadhana dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust Library Party

**Nothing Is Impossible for You!**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You have instructed us (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 9.5.20, purport), “A Vaiṣṇava can be understood by how much he is favored by the Supreme Personality of Godhead in terms of his inconceivable activities.”

Daily as we worship you, we often think of your extraordinary service, Śrīla Prabhupāda—your preaching to an endless stream of visitors late into the nights; your dictation of your Bhaktivedanta purports in the wee hours of the morning; your traveling constantly around the world, tirelessly training your “stumbling children” how to walk steadfastly on the path of *bhakti-yoga* in the midst of a turbulent world; your desire to please your Guru Mahārāja by spending money and energy producing and distributing profuse quantities of Vedic literature in all languages of the world.

You personally set the distributor’s example by placing your books in the libraries of Delhi and Bombay and in the shops of New York City. You were satisfied by giving the opportunity to all conditioned souls to hear *kṛṣṇa-kathā*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, like the previous *ācāryas,* who all had pure love for Kṛṣṇa, you found nothing impossible to accomplish for the service of your Guru Mahārāja. By your activities you proved you are the best follower of your Guru Mahārāja and the six Gosvāmīs, for you spread their teachings and the mission of Caitanya Mahāprabhu to every town and village. Nothing is impossible for your servants, either!

You encouraged us not to be satisfied simply distributing your books but to also “know my books like a lawyer knows his lawbooks.” You constantly encouraged us to maintain your ISKCON and expand your preaching work, offering the blessings of your Guru Mahārāja to those you did so.

In December 1970, while in Surat, you wrote Jagadīśa dāsa in Toronto: “I am so pleased to know that your efforts at placing our books in the local libraries are successful, and it is especially heartening to learn that they already have got our books on their shelves. That is factual proof that these purely transcendental literatures are very attractive to the reading class of the public.”

The servants of the old BBT India library party (1977) can amaze anyone as they tell how they boldly went out, overcoming the obstacles of their own hesitancy and the obstinacy of the public. It was not impossible for them then because they had such *guru-śraddhā*. They loved your BBT mission! Even now all devotees around the world who go out to preach are overcoming obstacles out of the same compelling love and devotion for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! This love is the glue, the cement, that binds the ISKCON devotees together.

When in 1975 a devotee in Los Angeles told you it was impossible to print seventeen books worth of backlogged *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* manuscripts in two months, which was your transcendental desire, you slammed your cane into the sand and said, “No, ‘impossible’ is a word in a fool’s dictionary!” You knew that “impossible” is a material word having no influence on the spiritual energy.

*duskaraḥ ko nu sādhūnāṁ dustyajo vā mahātmanām*  
*yaiḥ saṅgṛhīto bhagavān sātvatām ṛṣabho hariḥ*

**“For those who have achieved the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the master of the pure devotees, what is impossible to do, and what is impossible to give up?”**

This verse from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (9.5.15), spoken by Durvāsa Muni to Mahārāja Ambarīśa, inspires contemplation on the preaching spirit you exemplified and desired for all your disciples and followers in ISKCON.

Yet we wonder when we will get these extraordinary blessings of yours so that we may come up to the standard you desire for us. When we visit or preach in India, we sometimes are taunted by native Indian citizens as *lāl bandars,* “red monkeys.” So, examining the state of our hearts, which are still impure, we take it as Kṛṣṇa’s warning (through the public taunting) about just how much we must strive to serve in the footsteps of the past *ācāryas* in the Vaiṣṇava *brahmacārī-sannyāsī* line. Only by our attaining purity will people begin to regard us as they once did the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, whom you so feelingly sing about. They were so pure that they had no enemy (*dhīrādhīra-jana-priyau*). They were so absorbed in love of Kṛṣṇa, compassionately preaching, always setting the perfect example for mankind to follow.

Ordinary people may sometimes mistakenly feel your ISKCON soldiers are *lāl bandars,* but even if those Vaiṣṇavas consider the comment to contain some truth in it, that doesn’t make them any less qualified to reach the highest state of exalted Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Hanumān, the perfect servitor glorified as the exemplar of *dāsya-rasa* in the *śāstras,* had many *vāṇaras* (monkey servitors) who pleased the Personality of Godhead, Lord Rāmacandra. They performed unheard-of activities: they built the bridge to Laṅka, fought valiantly for Rāma, and helped win Sītā (Lakṣmī-devī herself) back for Lord Rāmacandra. Can anyone in this world find fault with Hanumān’s assistants? These *vāṇaras* had the real qualities of a Vaiṣṇava flowing from their hearts. Likewise, your ISKCON servitors have been (and continue to be) purified of material “monkey traits” by your mercy. Therefore, their endeavors on the path of devotional service are actually praiseworthy.

As Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura sings in his *Prārthana:*

O brother, please hear my words with rapt attention: The lotus feet of the Vaiṣṇavas are the most valuable treasure in the world. Those Vaiṣṇavas continually take shelter of Lord Kṛṣṇa and worship Him. They never abandon their Lord, but they are liberated from the cycle of repeated birth and death. The water which has washed the lotus feet of the Vaiṣṇavas bestows devotional service in pure love of Godhead. There is nothing as effective in attaining this divine love. I place the dust from the lotus feet of the Vaiṣṇavas upon my head. I wear no other ornament. The purifying power of the waters of the various places of pilgrimage is described in the *Purāṇas,* although this is something of a deceptive trick. Actually, there is nothing which is as purifying as the water which has washed the lotus feet of the Vaiṣṇavas. This water fulfills all desires. Moment after moment my mind finds constant pleasure in the association of the Vaiṣṇavas. I always seek out the company of those devotees who are devoted to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Poor-hearted Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura cries, “I cannot maintain my composure any longer. Why have I fallen into such a low condition of life that I cannot get the association of the Vaiṣṇavas?”

O Prabhupāda! When will we serve you and the *sampradāya-ācāryas* in this way? We are so far behind all your other servants, Śrīla Prabhupāda! All those devotees who have preached and distributed books for you in the cities around the world, and in the India BBT library party of 1977, are ever glorious. Their service to you is wonderful. We pray to you on your glorious appearance day for the strength to assist in placing the balance of your books in India’s libraries, and as far as possible in the hands of the people in the streets of India, for your pleasure.

Your servants in the BBT Library Party, India.

## Efland Gurukula

*gurur brahmā gurur viṣṇur gurur devo maheśvaraḥ*  
*guruḥ sākṣāt paraṁ brahma tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*oṁ ajñāna timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*sarva-śruti-śiro-ratna-samudbhāśita-mūrtaye*  
*vedāntāmbuja-sūryāya tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Although most of us, your disciples, were born and trained in the ways of those who are less than Vedic *śūdras,* by your merciful glance you have declared us to be more than *brāhmaṇas*—Vaiṣṇavas. Then, although most so-called *brāhmaṇas,* due to stopping the process of *saṁskāra,* have lost their twice-born status, you have given us a generation of children who often from the womb are greater than *brāhmaṇas*. You called them “Vaikuṇṭha children.”

We pray that we may never forget your qualities and instructions. By following those instructions in the training of these young Vaiṣṇavas, we hope to offer you a generation which can become the spiritual head of society. Let this next generations of devotess in your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, by their character, behavior, and devotion, be the instrument to spread Lord Caitanya’s movement all over the world. We beg to eternally assist you in this or any service in which you kindly engage us. Do not kick us away, for we have no other shelter.

Your servants at the ISKCON gurukula in Efland, North Carolina.

## International Life Membership Trust

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We never want to make the mistake of thinking you are an ordinary man. Only once in a millennium does one such as you appear. Such a great soul is very rare—we can all see that so clearly now.

I’m so fortunate to have had contact with you, let alone taken initiation from you, a servant of the servant of the Lord. My sojourn in the material world is now concluded: no matter how many crores of births await me, success is now assured by your mercy, so long as I do not become a nonsense. Or at least, more of a nonsense.

Sad it is that so many have left you. Strong are the waves of *māyā* that carry us all away. But for your mercy I too would be gone, swept away in the tide of illusion. Thank you for your kindness in allowing me to stay. Please, oh please, don’t kick me away. Please tolerate my offenses and my foolishness. But then, you always have, so why should you stop now? Mercy and tolerance are your astounding qualities—how else could you have tolerated us for so long?

We pray that we can be worthy sons and daughters, and that we may not embarrass you in front of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants at the International Life Membership Trust.  
(written by Jalakāra dāsa)

## The International Society For Cow Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

As a sweet-smelling blossom forces its way through tangling weeds,  
You appeared with the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s cow protection  
In this most vicious of meat-eating times.

You instructed us how the bull produces grain and is our father.  
As our mother, the cow gives milk, the most important of foodstuffs.  
You asked, “How can you kill and eat your father and mother?”

You knew; in the darkest ignorance  
We dined upon the flesh of the proud bull and the gentle cow.

To enlighten us, you told us how once the bull and cow  
Walked peacefully upon the Earth.  
The cow’s milk flowed profusely, giving and sustaining life.  
The powerful bull proudly pulled the plow, producing grain for all.  
The fruits of their labor were offered to Lord Kṛṣṇa,  
And man, cow, and the Earth lived in harmony.

In this vicious time, when man cruelly kills and eats his mother and father,  
We beg you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to give us the strength and determination  
To rise through the choking weeds of our lives  
To bring the sweet nectar of your blossom to the world of suffering man.

Just as you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have spread the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa  
To every town and village,  
Please give us your blessings and guidance  
That we may spread the knowledge of cow protection  
To the farthest reaches of this vicious world on your behalf and for your glory.

Begging to remain your servants in the protection of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s cows,  
Your servants at The International Society for Cow Protection.

## ISKCON Prison Ministries

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you and the holy place of your appearance, Tolly Gunge.

In preaching to Kṛṣṇa conscious inmates around North America, we at ISKCON Prison Ministries have one prominent realization—your books are touchstones! You are taking rocks and transforming them into precious transcendental gems. On Kali’s home turf you are snatching souls from his wicked grasp and creating Vaiṣṇavas by your magical touch, your *śikṣā,* your kindness. In the beginning we simply sent small packets of your books to prison libraries. It took months to hear from anyone, but steadily the letters continue to arrive. These letters are miracles. In fact, every day at ISKCON Prison Ministries we see five to seven miracles. Your books are working miracles in prison systems in America and around the world. It’s fascinating! We are thrilled to watch you convert bewildered *jīvas* into clear-headed *bhakti-yogīs*. Serving you in this preaching adventure is simply recreation, a pleasure sport.

Somehow or other, Lord Kṛṣṇa directs a desperate *jīva* to one of your books, and slowly, as he reads, he begins to realize, “It’s over. My long, perilous journey is over.” Gradually, he learns to love Lord Kṛṣṇa and appreciate the *saṅga* of His devotees. You are preaching so powerfully, so sweetly, all over the world. We cannot possibly estimate the strength of your *adhikāra,* your purity, your love for Lord Kṛṣṇa.

At ISKCON Prison Ministries we long to see a *Bhagavad-gītā* in every prison cell, neckbeads on all inmates, and in every heart a prayer of longing for Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa-seva.

We ask two boons of you, Śrī Gurudeva: Please allow us to hanker for preaching, and may we think of you at the time of death. Then we will never cry tears of sorrow again. In this tragic and dangerous *mahat-tattva,* serving you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we shall only cry tears of joy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us. Please guide us. Take us by the hand and drag us back to Kṛṣṇa if you have to. Please forgive our forgetfulness, but don’t forget us—we need you so much. You are our spiritual father, our transcendental master, *jagat-guru,* our ever well-wisher.

From all the *bhaktas* behind bars and all the devotees working with ISKCON Prison Ministries, we offer our heartfelt gratitude to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You saved us. We place our heads firmly under your lotus feet.

Your servants at ISKCON Prison Ministries.  
(written by Candraśekhara dāsa)

## ISKCON World Review

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to your divine mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, again and again we are reminded of your final instruction to your followers. You said that we can show our love for you by cooperating with each other. We here at your newspaper have found that we can not serve you at all without cooperating with the other members of your far-flung spiritual society. And we recognize that this instruction of yours is key to the success of all of ISKCON.

It seems, in analyzing your request, that in order for us to cooperate we must first do other things. We must each of us kick out our impersonalism toward others. This means that we must find the time to get to know one another and communicate nicely. When we give a communication, it must be with personal consideration and sensitivity so that the receiver may accept our message as we intend it to be received. And when we are receiving a communication, it must be accepted with patience and an open mind, free of any prejudice.

Once we have communicated thoroughly on a two-way basis, we can begin to work together. Here also, the communication (two-way) must continue. At every step, checking our pride and impersonalism, we must care enough about each other to really hear and speak well, from one heart to another. This takes mutual trust, respect, and love.

Then, once we can do all this, we can actually begin to continue your work, revitalizing and respiritualizing human society. Our realization regarding *ISKCON World Review* is that our role in this great task is to serve as an internal connecting device. You also said that one stick by itself can be easily broken, but when many sticks are tied together, they are strong. Please, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, help us do our part to bind many fragile souls together. Please empower us to help devotees work together in loving cooperation, free of envy and the kind of competition that is not transcendental.

Your Kṛṣṇa conscious society is a network of devotees from all stations of life. We are male and female. We are all ages and races and nationalities. And we need one another for encouragement, support, and selfless guidance in order to serve you as you wish—cooperatively. Since our society is often judged by how devotees relate to one another, we are convinced that improving our relationships will also improve our preaching.

We all want to realize the house you have built that is so multifaceted and secure that the whole world with all its diversity can peacefully live in it. Please make of us a portion of the mortar that helps to hold it all together. We have no other purpose than to serve you. So let us do it by serving your spiritual society, which needs to be woven together with threads of devotion.

We fall in the dust of your devotees’ feet and beg for your service.

Your sold-out servants at ISKCON World Review

## KrishnaFest

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We prostrate ourselves before you, relishing the transcendental dust of your lotus feet. Your Divine Grace has bestowed unlimited mercy upon us rotting souls.

When asked by a young radio interviewer about your mission, you replied that you had come to revive God consciousness among the people of this world. The interviewer’s eyes widened and he submissively inquired, “Does that mean that you’re the messiah?” You calmly answered, “Yes.” From the śāstric point of view, it is ascertained that you are nothing less than the empowered representative of the Supreme Lord known as the *śaktyāveśa avatāra,* one who descends by the request of the Almighty to execute extraordinary tasks.

Still, as the residents of Vṛndāvana preferred to think of Lord Kṛṣṇa as their intimate friend, child, or lover, putting aside knowledge of His unparalleled opulences, we prefer to meditate on Your Divine Grace as our loving savior, ever well-wisher, and most enjoyable object of remembrance.

We meditate on…

Your delightful morning gait punctuated by a cane’s accents;  
Your morning benediction-hand bestowing cookies upon all;  
Your bifurcating of the Darwinian Theory demon;  
Your swallowing of the blazing fire of material existence;  
Your ever-fresh purports, those personal ecstasies,  
Which, like the *Gītā*, continue to bathe our intelligence  
In a crystal-clear stream of knowledge that  
Cascades gently down from the *Param Parā* mountain.

We meditate upon…

Your harmonium’s delightful flute song,  
Mixed with your sonorous transcendental voice,  
Accompanied by your sweet *mṛdaṅga* rhythms,  
Attracting all living creatures, both moving and inert.

We meditate upon that…

Full-sun day of the summer season  
Within the groves of Golden Gate Park,  
Surrounded by thousands of loving disciples and friends,  
Where, dressed in brilliant saffron cloth,  
Wreathed with dozens of fresh colorful *vaijayantī* garlands,  
With upraised arms,  
You performed your cosmos-enchanting Ratha-yātrā Dance.  
When you heard of the plight of the hopeless multitudes  
Who, having drunk the drops of the poisonous Soviet lake,  
Lay paralyzed in lifeless ignorance,  
You waived the mango-laden isle, mounted your steel-winged carrier,  
And, tightening your seat belt, flew directly to the spot.  
Then, without delay, you sportingly dipped into the lake  
And quickly subdued the residing serpent of Atheism.

We meditate upon…

The gorgeous, shining necklace worn about your neck,  
Whose jewels emanate your Guru Mahārāja’s command,  
Which lights the way through the darkest nights  
And which saves this world from writhing agony.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, beloved Gurudeva, we at KrishnaFest are humbly attempting the impossible task of repaying the debt we owe to Your Divine Grace by preaching enthusiastically. Despite our inadequacies, we are trying to propagate Lord Kṛṣṇa’s teachings as received from you, through literature distribution, traveling festivals, *kīrtana* bands, theater, and the establishment of a Vedic college campus. We’ll engage everything possible and within our capacity to popularize Kṛṣṇa consciousness in America. We are small and Māyā is powerful; nonetheless, on this most auspicious day we beg Your Divine Grace to sprinkle just a tiny droplet of your unfathomable mercy on our heads, so that we may be successful in these attempts to please you.

Your servants at KrishnaFest.  
(written by Dānavīr dāsa Vānacārī)

## Marco Ferrini Design

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances to your lotus feet. All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga.

On this very special occasion, the most auspicious and blessed day, your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration day, I would like to express my most heartfelt thanks unto your lotus feet.

I think often about your holy self. Among so many feelings which crowd my mind, the most important, constant, basic, and strong one is, for sure, that of deep gratitude toward Your Divine Grace.

Now, almost fifteen years after your physical departure from this mortal planet, I am able to understand and appreciate somewhat better your teachings and lifestyle, as well as the prodigious services Your Divine Grace accomplished while living among us.

In the days when you came to the West, although people were culturally, politically, and in so many other ways much different from what they are now, the social chaos was the same as it is now. Today’s people are not suffering less, and the world situation now, after so many illusions, is even worse than before. Your Divine Grace, by your personal example and oral teachings, were able to show us the worldly pseudoknowledge and -culture, a result of illusion and ignorance. And by the strength of your devotion to the Supreme Lord, Śrī Krsna, at that time you changed our minds and hearts. Even now you are able to keep us purified by your recorded, ever-fresh teachings and sincere disciples.

Now, a quarter of a century after you first came to the West, there are so many established temples with wonderful Deities, so many devotees, a huge quantity of distributed books around the world, and many, many people who know and worship the Supreme Lord, Śrī Krsna, read Vedic literature, and are developing practical devotion. All this has already had a tremendous impact on the materialistic, worldly social system, changing the honest people’s general mentality in a very relevant way.

I think what you did is very, very rare and precious. I won’t be able to repay Your Divine Grace by any means. I can only try to serve you, your mission, and your devotees to the best of my present and future capacity, hoping to get your mercy. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Patita-pāvana, protect me and my family and give us the inspiration to better serve Your Divine Grace and the Supreme Lord, Śrī Krsna.

Your servants at Marco Ferrini Design.  
(written by Matsya-avatāra dāsa)

## Ministry of Vaiṣṇava Studies

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances at your lotus feet. All glory to your historic role in exporting the Vaiṣṇava way of life into the cultures around the world. Your short stay with us was remarkable, and even if we were not your disciples, we could never forget what you have accomplished. Indeed, as your disciples, we can never come close to comprehending fully all that you have done and done for us.

My greatest wish has always been, since I first experienced your *darśana,* to make a contribution to your work by creating, within the span of my short lifetime, a high-level, sophisticated image of Vaiṣṇava thought and practice, to irrevocably and permanently plant this image in the modern world.

In this regard, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have a dream. Of course, as you know, I have had many dreams. But recently I have had a dream, the very thought of which inspires me more and more every time I think of it: the establishing of a prestigious professorship of Vaiṣṇava Studies in your name at a fine, prominent university. The name of the professorship could be BHAKTIVEDANTA PROFESSOR OF VAISHNAVA STUDIES.

This academic chair would represent only the very best in historical and philosophical work. This professor, preferably a scholar from one of the Vaiṣṇava *sampradāyas,* would be expected to publish books and teach courses on *bhakti* and *bhāgavata-dharma*. The scholar chosen to fill this chair could perhaps be determined by a small board from the university as well as representatives from the Vaiṣṇava community.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I know that I am in no position to offer this strategy to you, for any cogent and powerful preaching strategy has been already possessed by you. All my strategies and ideas originate in your being and in your teachings. However, I feel fully blessed to be given the opportunity to offer you my intense desire to see this dream come true. I feel it is plausible, and I will attempt to see you ultimately honored in this way, which will indelibly plant the glories of the history and thought of Vaiṣṇavism in the fabric of modern culture.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me the opportunity to have such dreams and desires! Thank you very, very much.

Your servants at the Ministry of Vaiṣṇava Studies.  
(written by Garuḍa dāsa)

## Padayātrā America

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*Kānta-seva-sukha-pūra, saṅgama haite sumadhura:* “Service to the Lord is the home of happiness, and is sweeter than direct union with Him” (Cc. *Antya* 20.60).

Although we miss your physical presence, the sweetness of serving you keeps your presence with us more intimately. How can there be any happiness without serving you? A devotee needs service as he needs air to breathe or water to drink, because it is the occupation of the soul, which is the sustainer of the body.

It is useless to make any plans for personal happiness. Kṛṣṇa has His plan, and ultimately He will always have His way. All of creation has been arranged in this way. We must simply follow His plan under your kind guidance, for without serving you, haw can we be happy? How can we go on?

So we are humbly begging you to kindly keep us engaged. Help us fulfill your mission of bringing Padayātrā to at least one hundred countries by 1996. It is important to you, so we can achieve it by your grace.

We beg to remain in the dust of your lotus feet,  
Your servants on Padayātrā America.

## Padayātrā India

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master. By his mercy a lame man can cross over mountains and a dumb man can compose eloquent poetry.”**

It is so rare to become a perfect devotee in this Age of Kali. So many obstacles face us day and night, regardless of our *varṇa* or *āśrama*. A devotee must never think he is perfect or “above it all”; otherwise he is just another victim of false prestige. When will that day come when false ego will leave us peacefully? Becoming a pure devotee of the Lord is the most difficult thing in the world. It’s much easier to become a demigod than a pure devotee of the Supreme Lord, and the *Bṛhan-nāradīya Purāṇa* states that even personalities like Lord Brahmā and other demigods do not know the value of a pure devotee of the Lord!

What a challenge! What a goal! Is it actually possible for us insignificant souls to become such pure-hearted devotees? The odds are certainly against us. As mentioned in the *Garuḍa Purāṇa,* out of thousands of *brāhmaṇas,* one may be expert in performing sacrifices, among thousands of such experts, one may be expert in knowledge of the *Vedānta-sūtra,* and out of many thousands of Vedāntists, one may be a famous devotee of Lord Viṣṇu. There are many devotees of Viṣṇu, but one who has unflinching devotion is eligible to return home to Godhead. It is further stated, in the *Nārāyaṇa-vyuha-stava,* that if Lord Brahmā is not a devotee, he is most insignificant, whereas if a microbe is a devotee of the Lord, he is most famous!

Due to the causeless mercy of a pure-hearted devotee such as yourself, Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is a very good chance we can challenge those odds and cross over the difficulties, obtaining your love as well as Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s. As Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura prays,

*śrī kṛṣṇa caitanya prabhu—daya koro more*  
*tomā binā ke doyālu jagat-saṁsāre*

**There is no one else more merciful than Caitanya Mahāprabhu within these three worlds, and He is the most compassionate to all the fallen souls of Kali-yuga. He has incarnated just to reclaim us all, since we are all in the darkest, lowest regions of ignorance.**

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has sent you as His mercy-personfication; otherwise how is it possible to walk 30,000 kilometers around the Indian subcontinent twice? We are all certainly very lame, but by your merciful love we are willing to cross many more obstacles than just mountains! If you desire, please allow us to cross over every inch of every mountain, not only here but throughout this planet, just so we can spread your unlimited glories now, for your centennial, and eternally.

Please, please, please forgive us all for our countless offenses at your lotus feet and allow us to keep on walking for your transcendental pleasure.

Your insignificant walking servants of Padayātrā India.  
(written by Jaya Vijaya dāsa)

## Padayātrā Nepal

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All the devotees of Padayātrā Nepal thankfully and happily offer their humble obeisances to you, who showed us how to go to every town and village! We are always indebted to your dear servant Lokanāth Swami for getting us out on the lowland road skirting the Himalayan Mountains, through jungles, fields, and villages.

We see your little *mūrti* form at the feet of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, who ride on a cart pulled by Nepali bulls and accompanied by chanting devotees. Although personally you never came here, now we can bring you and your glories to everyone. Thank you for this opportunity to serve you.

When we visit Damauli soon, we will have *darśana* at Parāśara Muni’s *āśrama,* the birthplace of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the writer of the Vedic literature. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the Śrīla Vyāsadeva for this fallen age, and we wish to distribute more of your books. When all your books have been published in Nepali, surely then Mount Everest will be really conquered!

In the villages everyone knows the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* but they are glad to learn about Lord Caitanya. They sing it to their local tunes and dance outside in the evenings after our programs. They are very happy to hear your glories.

We twenty devotees feel great unity and comradeship on this *padayātrā* as we cooperate for your pleasure. Some of us are experienced *padayātrīs* from Padayātrā India; some of us are from Māyāpur but originally from Nepal; some of us are from Yugoslavia or New Zealand; and some of us are from ISKCON Kathmandu, Nepal. We hope that this will be the first of several Nepal *padayātrās* to celebrate your glorious centennial anniversary in 1996.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your humble servants on Padayātrā Nepal.

## Save the Cow

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at you lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are happy to be a part of your *saṅkīrtana* movement. We see how cow protection and ox power are very important parts of your plan, and are indeed the basis of *varṇāśrama.* We have seen how our herd of protected cows, and especially our working oxen, have brought people closer to Kṛṣṇa who might otherwise not have been attracted to Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

For example:

• Our neighbors down the road grew to like us simply by seeing the oxen work.  
• A man driving through Gainesville asked a local policeman if anyone in the area worked oxen, and he was given exact directions to us at the New Rāmaṇa-reti farm, twenty-five miles away.  
• Many people were so inspired by seeing our ox team on Padayātrā America that they wanted to do some service for them, the Padayātrā devotees, and the Padayātrā Deities.  
• Some neighbors on a nearby farm heard of us and gave us their two Jersey cows. The men on the farm were too sick to maintain it and wanted a place for their cows where they would not be sent to slaughter. So Honey and Agatha became Hema and Amṛta.  
• Many people have heard of our ox-power program and expect us to develop it more fully. For instance, the men who delivered a diesel tank for tractor-fuel storage (unfortunately, we’re not yet fully ox-powered) remarked, “Hey, why are you getting this? We thought you guys farmed with oxen.”

Many eyes are on us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, along with yours and Kṛṣṇa’s! We still have a lot of work to do, and we know that you (and others) are expecting us to do it!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our part in your *saṅkīrtana* movement is small, but we know that the service we are tackling is of utmost importance. We pray to be able to perfect our service more and more, and we also wish to thank you for the association of your sincere devotees, from whom we get strength and inspiration.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have tasted the nectar of many services available in ISKCON, and we have to tell you that we like this one the best!

Thank you for your kindness in engaging us fallen souls in service to you and Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Please keep us at your lotus feet, and let us continue to be a part of this ever-increasing preaching!

Your servants at Save the Cow, New Ramaṇa Reti farm, Alachua, Florida.
